# Verification digest
Every claim that an independent verifier corrected, refuted or could not verify, plus what each verifier said the analyst missed, for all research dossiers (research date 2026-09-29). Each verifier was told to try to refute the load-bearing claims by re-fetching primary sources and to default to "unverifiable" when it could not. The corrections here are already applied in `docs/PLAN.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md` and `docs/COMPLIANCE.md`; the dossiers themselves keep their original text, so read a dossier together with this file.
Verdict words: **corrected** (partly right; the corrected value is given), **refuted** (wrong), **unverifiable** (the verifier could not check it against a primary source).

## availability-at-scale
Dossier: `availability-at-scale.md`

### Verifier lens: skeptic (confidence high; 19 claims confirmed)

I re-derived 30 load-bearing claims from primary sources I opened myself. The core conclusions hold: RDAP 200 and DNS delegation prove only that a name is taken, and 404 or NXDOMAIN prove nothing about availability. Five findings need correcting or qualifying. (1) The zone-file claim wrongly excludes .com; the .com Registry Agreement Appendix 3 gives the same CZDA access terms. (2) The TL;DR says all three registries ban high-volume automated querying, but Verisign and Identity Digital restrict how the returned data is used, and only Google restricts access itself. (3) The GNSO RAPWG report never names Network Solutions as its one well-known case, so that identification is the analyst's inference. (4) The dossier says no premium example was observed, but the registrar price endpoint does show premium-tier prices for registered names. (5) Google Public DNS has a documented default limit of 1500 QPS per client IP, and Cloudflare publicly pledges not to sell resolver data. Some claims could not be re-checked because Identity Digital rejected my one request with a 429, so I did not retry. The .io-on-Identity-Digital 200 and the example.studio RDAP 404 are therefore unverified by me. Recent-change checks found nothing that overturns the conclusions: the 2026 Base RA still bans registry front-running, and the dossier's ICANN RDAP dates (WHOIS optional since 2025-01-28, profile mandatory 2025-08-21) are right.

Not confirmed:
- **corrected**: F12/F13/F14/F15/F16 and TL;DR item 3: all three registries ban 'high volume, automated' querying and publish no numeric limit -> Google bans high-volume automated ACCESS with no registration exception; Verisign and Identity Digital bar USING the data to enable high-volume automated queries, except as reasonably necessary to register domains. Identity Digital additionally states it throttles per IP. (Quotes and the absence of numeric limits are confirmed verbatim on all three terms pages. Wording nuance: Google restricts how you access the database ('Don't access ... through high volume, automated electronic processes ...', no exception, 'experimental', 'pilot'). Verisign and Identity Digital restrict how you use the data ('under no circumstances will you use the data to ... enable high volume, automated, electronic processes that send queries or data to the systems of ...', except as reasonably necessary to register domains). Also, 'Last Updated: February 12, 2026' on the Identity Digital URL sits before the concatenated Privacy Policy section, so it is not clearly the RDDS policy date. Lawyer question is correctly flagged, but the TL;DR flattens the clauses.)
- **corrected**: F46: GNSO RAPWG (2010-05-29) found only one confirmed case (NSI via SAC 024), considered front-running an abuse, recommended no policy development -> RAPWG referenced 'one well-known case ... described in SAC 024' without naming it; identification with the NSI four-day hold is inferred. (Recommendation, unanimity and 'generally considered front-running an abuse' confirmed. But the report says only 'One well-known case of front-running is described in SAC 024. Otherwise, the RAPWG was unable to reference any other confirmed cases.' It never names Network Solutions, and SAC 024 itself found 0 provable cases and did not examine the NSI service. 'NSI' is the analyst's inference. The report also says registrar 'Cart Hold' features may have been perceived as front running and that there are 'no known examples of it occurring presently'.)
- **corrected**: F61: CZDS zone-file terms (once per 24 h, at least 3-month grants, lawful use except high-volume automated queries) apply to new gTLDs but .com is not covered -> .com is covered by the same CZDA zone-file terms (Appendix 3, agreement dated 1 Dec 2024). (New-gTLD terms confirmed in the .studio agreement (Spec 4 2.1.3/2.1.5/2.1.6/2.1.7). But the current .com Registry Agreement Appendix 3 'Zone File Access' has the same CZDA-provider terms (no more than once per 24 hour period, not less than three months, no fee), with an even broader use restriction (marketing; high-volume automated queries to registry or registrar systems; interfering with registrants). So .com IS covered contractually; only .ai/.io remain unverified. Zone data must not drive bulk registrar checks, and held names (qvz.com) are absent from zones.)
- **corrected**: F30: no live premium example found; the Vercel availability endpoint returns only a boolean so premium is invisible outside the registrar channel -> Premium tier is observable via the registrar price endpoint (renewal price deviating from the TLD standard) for registered names; unregistered premium remains unobserved. (Boolean-only confirmed (docs schema and my calls). But the same registrar's price endpoint shows premium tiers today: registered harbor.app renewal $108.90 vs standard .app $15; example.app $218.90; example.dev $361.90 vs standard .dev $13. purchasePrice is null for all unavailable names. Standard first-year prices for unregistered names: .com $11.25, .dev $9.99, .app $9.99, .ai $160 (2-year minimum), .studio $21.99, .io $14.99. I found no unregistered premium name in about 100 probes, so that case is still unobserved.)
- **corrected**: F36-F38: Cloudflare keeps resolver logs up to 25 hours; Google keeps IP+query logs 24-48 hours; Google has per-IP rate limits with no figure -> Google Public DNS default limit 1500 QPS per client IP; Cloudflare states no sale or sharing of resolver personal data. (Retention figures confirmed on both privacy pages. Omissions: Google's ISP page (the one the analyst cited) states the default limit of 1500 QPS per client IPv4 or IPv6 /64, far above type-ahead volumes. Cloudflare publicly pledges it 'will not sell or share Public Resolver users' personal data', shares only limited anonymised logs with APNIC, and has a third-party audit. SAC 022's DNS-operator vector is real in principle but these operators publicly commit against monetising queries.)
- **unverifiable**: F06/F07: rdap.org has no service for .io; rdap.nic.io does not exist; whois.nic.io is a CNAME to Identity Digital; Identity Digital's RDAP host answers .io queries with 200 (Confirmed: rdap.org example.io 404 'No RDAP service is available for this resource' (max-age 28800); rdap.nic.io NXDOMAIN; whois.nic.io CNAME whois.identitydigital.services. Not re-verifiable: example.io returning 200 on rdap.identitydigital.services, because my one request to that host was 429 and I did not retry. Treat as the analyst's single observation.)

Missed by the analyst:
- Google Public DNS documents a default per-client limit of 1500 QPS (IPv4 or IPv6 /64) on the ISP page the analyst cited; the dossier says no figure exists. Cloudflare 1.1.1.1 states it will not sell or share resolver personal data (limited anonymised logs go to APNIC), which the dossier's DoH-leak argument omits.
- The .com Registry Agreement (1 Dec 2024) Appendix 3 gives CZDA-mediated zone-file access on the same terms as new gTLDs (once per 24 h, at least 3-month grants, no fee). The dossier's exclusion of .com is wrong. The use restriction also forbids using zone data to drive high-volume automated queries to registry or registrar systems.
- The operative RAA is the 21 Jan 2024 text (global amendment effective 5 Apr 2024), not only the 2013 original. The ICANN Registration Data Policy (effective 21 Aug 2025, revised 12 May 2026) s12 sets a minimum 15-month TDRP retention, which may overlap or displace the 'sponsorship plus 2 years' wording the dossier relies on.
- Positive recent change: the 2026 Base RA (Board approved 12 Mar 2026) keeps the registry anti-front-running clause 1(c), so the new-gTLD protection is not being loosened.
- No standard RDAP availability service exists: draft-newton-regext-rdap-domain-availability-00 (ARIN/DENIC, Dec 2016) proposed an RDAP availability profile but expired 19 Jun 2017 and never became an RFC.
- The registrar price endpoint exposes premium-tier pricing for registered names (harbor.app renewal $108.90, example.app $218.90, example.dev $361.90 versus standard $13-$15). A UI can flag premium by comparing against the TLD standard price, so 'premium is invisible outside EPP' is too strong for this provider.
- The McElroy complaint also named ICANN as a defendant. The RAPWG report never names Network Solutions as its 'well-known case', and mentions registrar 'Cart Hold' features as things perceived as front running.
- Cloudflare's error 1015 page warns that repeated attempts within a short period may extend the block, so any circuit breaker on Identity Digital should back off completely, not probe. My own 429 shows the throttle is live and my shared egress makes attribution to Mosshatch's own request rate impossible.
- INCIDENTAL for the parent project: Verisign RDAP shows mosshatch.com was registered on 2026-09-29T17:48:38Z (nameservers DNS1/2.REGISTRAR-SERVERS.COM, status client transfer prohibited) and the registrar reports it unavailable, while mosshatch.dev/.app/.io/.studio are available ($9.99/$9.99/$14.99/$21.99 first year; .ai $160 for 2 years). Registrant unknown; worth one line to the owner in case it was not them.
- Post-2010 front-running incidents remain unchecked: WebSearch is exhausted and the DuckDuckGo HTML fallback returned a 202 challenge page, which I did not try to bypass. Wikipedia's article lists nothing after 2009.

## icann-transfer-policy
Dossier: `icann-transfer-policy.md`

### Verifier lens: skeptic (confidence high; 23 claims confirmed)

The dossier's core holds up against primary sources I fetched myself. The Transfer Policy updated 21 Feb 2024 is the version in force (mandatory since 21 Aug 2025). The 60-day post-creation and post-transfer denials, the mandatory 60-day Change-of-Registrant lock (opt-out only at the registrar's option, before the request), the 5-calendar-day pending window and the 24-hour Losing FOA all match the analyst's quotes word for word. The Board adopted all 47 Transfer Policy Review recommendations on 7 Jun 2026 (Res. 2026.06.07.04, "subject to prioritization"). The TAC rules (336 h validity, 120 h issue cap, 10-minute notice, 720 h locks, CoR lock removed) are correctly quoted from the WG Final Report but are not in force. I found no ICANN announcement, blog, advisory or public IRT page giving any implementation or effective date, after paging 400 announcements (Jan 2024 to 24 Sep 2026), 8 pages of blogs and the advisories list. ICANN's own Consolidated Policy Scorecard of 21 Sep 2026, which the analyst did not cite, says "Implementation Start TBC ... Currently in the planning/resourcing process". Two corrections. (1) The 2024 update was not terminology-only. It removed the Administrative Contact as an approver, added a Gaining-FOA data-access carve-out to I.A.2.1.1, and added AuthInfo "best practices" duties (I.A.5.7, I.A.6.1). It did not change any lock length. (2) The 15-month retention figure attributed to Rec 14 is only in its rationale; the operative 15-month text is in Rec 18.3, 27 and 28. The "not before Mar 2027" bound is valid as arithmetic only; ICANN precedent (IRTP-C 2012 to 2016, Registration Data Policy 2019 to 2025) points to 2028 or later. The WebSearch budget was exhausted, so all checks were direct fetches.

Not confirmed:
- **corrected**: The 2024 update only changed terminology/data model (no TAC, no FOA removal) -> Lock/CoR/5-day provisions unchanged since 2016; update also removed Admin Contact, added the I.A.2.1.1 Gaining-FOA carve-out and new I.A.5.7/I.A.6.1 AuthInfo duties (No TAC, no FOA removal and no change to any lock length is right (I.A.3.7.5/3.7.6/3.8.5 and II.C.2 differ from the 2016 text only in Whois to RDDS wording). But it was not terminology-only. I diffed the 2016 and 2024 texts. The Administrative Contact was removed as an approving authority, a Gaining-FOA data-access carve-out was added inside I.A.2.1.1, 'where the FOA is required' qualifiers were added, I.A.5.7 and I.A.6.1 added AuthInfo best-practice and registry verification duties, and II.A.1.1.4 was deleted.)
- **corrected**: Records of TAC issuance must be retained 15 months (Rec 14) -> 15 months is operative in Rec 18.3/27/28; Rec 14 defers to the Data Retention Specification (Rec 14's operative text says records fall under the ICANN Data Retention Specification; '15-month' appears only in its rationale. Operative 15-month figures are Rec 18.3 (no fewer than 15 months), Rec 27 (shorter of 15 months or the longest period permitted by law) and Rec 28.)
- **unverifiable**: .ai transfer renews 2 years; .io needs a domain at least 60 days old and transfer adds 1 year (The sentences exist on the Gandi pages, but no registry primary source: the nic.ai FAQ (rendered in headless Chromium, updated 24 Apr 2025) has no transfer question and the nic.io FAQ page has no transfer text. Treat as registrar-KB values.)

Missed by the analyst:
- ICANN's Consolidated Policy Scorecard of 21 Sep 2026 (https://www.icann.org/en/system/files/files/policy-development-consolidated-scorecards-21sep26-en.pdf) lists Transfer Policy as 'Board Approved June 2026, Implementation Start TBC, Currently in the planning/resourcing process'. This is ICANN org's own status statement and is stronger evidence for 'no date' than the GNSO project list.
- The Feb 2024 update was substantive beyond terminology: Administrative Contact removed as an approver, an I.A.2.1.1 carve-out (Gaining Registrar need not obtain an FOA or follow the Change of Registrant process where it cannot access then-current registration data and the registrant re-enters it), and new AuthInfo 'best practices' duties in I.A.5.7 and I.A.6.1. The summary omits the carve-out that the dossier body does quote.
- ICANN precedent for Board-adoption-to-effect lag is 4 to 6 years (IRTP-C 20 Dec 2012 to 1 Dec 2016; Registration Data Policy 15 May 2019 to 21 Aug 2025; Thick RDDS 7 Feb 2014 to 1 May 2018), so March 2027 should be labelled a theoretical floor and 2028 or later the planning date.
- Next watch points: ICANN87 in Bali, 17-22 Oct 2026 (Prep Week 5-7 Oct), and the Pre-ICANN87 PRSP briefing; the PRSP briefings index (Confluence page 472776707) listed only up to Pre-ICANN86 as of 3 Jun 2026.
- The Confluence page 'Implementation Projects in Queue' (modified 2026-08-21) links Transfer Policy Review to a wiki page that shows 'Page Not Found' to anonymous users, so 'no IRT' should read 'no public IRT page'.
- ICANN's May 2026 PRSP briefing says org's feasibility assessment 'found all recommendations to be implementable'; the Feasibility Assessment document itself was not located.
- Rec 14's 15-month retention is rationale-only; operative 15-month figures are in Rec 18.3, 27 and 28. Checklist item F-07 should cite those.
- IANA's .io record lists the manager at an address in Diego Garcia (British Indian Ocean Territory), last updated 2023-01-18; the sovereignty risk the analyst declared out of scope remains unresearched.
- ICANN's registrant-facing FAQ and 'Transferring Your Domain Name' page are stale against the 2024 policy (still reference the Administrative Contact and Initial Authorization form) and should not be quoted for current mechanics.
- Could not resolve, because the WebSearch budget was exhausted, whether Verisign or any registry already enforces an RFC 9154-style TTL for .com today, or whether Google Registry or Identity Digital apply registry-level post-creation locks for .dev, .app and .studio.

## lifecycle-and-consumer-law
Dossier: `lifecycle-and-consumer-law.md`

### Verifier lens: skeptic (confidence high; 27 claims confirmed)

I re-fetched every load-bearing primary source in the lens myself and refuted nothing. The ERRP notice schedule (about T-30 and T-7, safe harbour 26-35 and 4-10 days, a further notice within 5 days after expiry, DNS off for the last 8 days, 30-day RGP, fees shown on reseller sites), the AGP formula (max of 10% of net adds or 50, per registrar per TLD per month), the FTC sequence (8th Cir. vacatur on 8 Jul 2025, old rule restored 12 Feb 2026, ANPRM 13 Mar 2026 with comments closed 13 Apr 2026, no NPRM) and the California AB 2863 changes all match the dossier. Four refinements. (1) ERRP is not "mandatory since 21 Aug 2025": the substantive duties have applied since 31 Aug 2013, and the 2024 revision only changed terminology (I diffed the two versions). (2) New York GBL 527-a is now resolved: signed as Chapter 58 on 9 May 2025, so effective 5 Nov 2025 (computed) and in force. (3) "No NPRM" is now backed by the FTC's 2026 Unified Agenda entry (RIN 3084-AB84, prerule stage, last item "staff review of public comments 07/2026"). (4) On EU domains, the CRD text and the 2021 Commission guidance contain no domain provision (zero mentions), but the dossier's "probably can withdraw" inference is unsupported either way, and the one registrar contract I read (Gandi, French law) treats a domain as fully performed at registration or renewal with a consumer waiver. Points to tighten: the cadence has zero-margin timings (E-17 on Visa's 7-day floor; E-45 on California's 45-day edge if measured from E); AGP "net adds" excludes AGP-deleted names; California's ARL covers only individual consumers.

Not confirmed:
- **corrected**: ERRP is in force; contracted parties must implement the 21 Feb 2024 update by 21 Aug 2025 (TL;DR: 'mandatory since 21 Aug 2025'). -> Substantive ERRP duties in force since 31 Aug 2013; only the 2024 terminology revision had the 21 Aug 2025 implementation deadline. (The 21 Aug 2025 date applies only to the terminology update (Registered Name Holder, RDDS, Registration Data Reminder Policy cross-reference). I diffed the 2013 and 2024 texts: notice timing, DNS interruption, RGP and fee-display duties are unchanged. The current page's Notes still say all registrars and registries must comply beginning 31 August 2013.)
- **corrected**: New York GBL 527-a (Part W): notice 15-45 days, consent to price rises, effective 180 days after enactment; enactment date not verified. -> Signed 9 May 2025 as Chapter 58; effective 5 Nov 2025 (computed); in force now. (Bill text confirmed (notice 15-45 days before the cancellation deadline, not the renewal date; b-1 price-increase consent or 14-day cancel with pro rata refund; change notice 5 business days to 30 days). The Assembly record for A.3008 shows 'DELIVERED TO GOVERNOR 05/08/2025' and 'SIGNED CHAP.58' on 05/09/2025; Part W section 3 says it takes effect on the 180th day after it becomes law. 9 May 2025 plus 180 days is 5 Nov 2025 (computed), so it is in force. I could not rule out a later chapter amendment (nysenate.gov is behind Cloudflare).)
- **unverifiable**: A domain registration is probably not 'fully performed' at registration, so an EU consumer can probably withdraw within 14 days and owe only a proportionate amount (analyst's practical reading). (No primary source found either way; the analyst flagged it as inference. Counter-evidence from market practice: Gandi's Domain name registration Contract v2024.1 Art. 7 says the service 'is fully executed for each domain name upon its registration, renewal or effective transfer' and has the consumer waive withdrawal (French Consumer Code L.221-28). That is a T&C waiver, which the Commission guidance says would not satisfy express consent alone, so both positions are contestable. Remains a lawyer item; national case law was not searchable (WebSearch budget exhausted).)

Missed by the analyst:
- Zero-margin timings in the recommended cadence: E-17 (= C-7) sits exactly on Visa's 'at least 7 days' floor, and E-45 is exactly 45 days before E, so California's 15-45 day window has no margin if 'renews' is measured from the term boundary rather than the charge. This contradicts the dossier's own 2-day-margin rule. Suggest C-8 and E-43/E-44.
- ERRP text is unconditional ('any gTLD registration'), but with C = E-10 a successfully charged auto-renewal moves expiry before the one-week window, so those names get no E-7 notice. I found no ICANN guidance on auto-renewed names; also the ERRP 2.1.2 'within five days' reading ambiguity. Both belong on the lawyer list.
- ERRP date framing: substantive duties have bound registrars since 31 Aug 2013, not 21 Aug 2025; only the terminology revision had the 2025 deadline.
- AGP 'net adds' excludes names deleted within the add grace period (registry reporting Specification 3), so the refundable pool cannot be estimated from Mosshatch's gross sales; the pool is the sponsoring registrar's per TLD.
- New York is now in force: Chapter 58 signed 9 May 2025, effective 5 Nov 2025 (computed); NY measures its 15-45 day notice from the cancellation deadline, not the renewal date. Resolves the analyst's unverified item.
- California, Vermont, New York and Virginia protect individuals (CA 17601(a)(4) defines consumer as an individual for personal, family or household purposes), so business buyers may be outside them; ROSCA 8403 has no such definition. Partly answers the B2B open question for CA only.
- FTC agenda entry uses RIN 3084-AB84 (previously 3084-AB60) while the ANPRM uses 3084-AB54; the Dec 2025 CFA/AELP petition (90 FR 55701) does state its subject (renew the negative option rulemaking), contrary to the dossier's note.
- Market practice on EU withdrawal for domains (Gandi treats the service as fully executed at registration/renewal with a consumer waiver) is the opposite of the dossier's practical reading, and a T&C waiver is contestable under the Commission's express-consent guidance; the dossier should present both positions.
- Digital Fairness Act status and the long-term status of the .io delegation remain unchecked; the .io registry terms page is dated 2021.
- Not verifiable by me: Mastercard rules, Massachusetts/Illinois/Colorado statutes, the 2026 Virginia amendments' effective date, and any later chapter amendment to NY Part W (nysenate.gov Cloudflare wall); WebSearch budget was exhausted.

## reg-centralnic
Dossier: `reg-centralnic.md`

### Verifier lens: legal (confidence high; 18 claims confirmed)

I re-fetched the MSA, Reseller and R&M Schedules, Registration Agreement, T&C, Anti-Abuse Policy, FAQ, program, pricing, promo and sign-up pages, the NIS2, sanctions, ERRP and renewal KB pages, IANA, the ICANN RAA and Transfer Policy, and the status feed. All 49 dossier quotes I tested match verbatim. The core legal finding holds: resale under Mosshatch's own brand, price and checkout, ordering via API, is expressly granted (MSA 5.2, Reseller Sched. 1, R&M 1.2.2). There is no "own use only" API clause, and no separate API terms exist (the API is defined in MSA 1.1 and 1.25). The end customer is the registrant, and Key-Systems GmbH (IANA 269) is the accredited registrar. Caveats: R&M 1.2.1 bans resale unless agreed in writing, and "Schedule" is defined as signed. It is unclear whether an unverified, unsigned account already holds the permission. Problems in the dossier: (1) REFUTED: the default renewal mode is AutoRenew, not delete-at-term. (2) CORRECTED: the 100-domain rule and "no minimum" sit in the same pricing-FAQ answer, so the pricing page does not contradict the rule. Neither is in the MSA, which has an entire-agreement clause. (3) CORRECTED: both limbs of MSA 13.1 start at month 36. The dossier omits the 15-day-cure breach termination and 13.7, which obliges Mosshatch to transfer out all domains before the end of the term. (4) CORRECTED: records must be kept for the Term plus 2 years, and evidence "as long as legally possible" or for the "life-time of the Registration". (5) CORRECTED/UNRESOLVED: the B2B / EU-consumer attestation is ambiguous and broader than the dossier's reading. It may block EU natural-person registrants, and the stated remedy is immediate termination. (6) CORRECTED: clause 30 does not rank the Registration Agreement, so "MSA prevails over the 14-day rule" is unsupported. Omitted material terms: the DPA (not public), Reseller Sched. 3.6 (Mosshatch is liable for all fees whether or not it collects), 3.1-3.3 (the whole MSA must be flowed down into Mosshatch's own terms), 4.8 (SP may itself change registrant data, transfer and renew), MSA 23, 28 and 31, the promo activation conditions, and ICANN website-disclosure duties. Recommendation "fallback" is consistent with what I found; the contract risk profile is somewhat worse than the dossier states.

Not confirmed:
- **corrected**: Own brand, own price, own checkout; 'white label' with the registrar hidden -> Own brand/price/checkout is contractually allowed, but the sponsoring registrar (Key-Systems GmbH) must be identifiable and registrants must accept its Registration Agreement. (Program and pricing-page quotes are verbatim. But the marketing says a white-label reseller sells 'with no mention of the underlying registrar' and 'no CentralNic Reseller branding visible', while the contract requires the opposite. Reseller Sched. 4.3: registrants must accept the Key-Systems Registration Agreement, and Mosshatch must identify Key-Systems as sponsoring registrar on inquiry. ICANN RAA 3.12.2-3.12.3 (opened) requires the same. Reseller Sched. 3.7: SP may contact your customers. Reseller Sched. 5.2: do not imply authority to represent SP. Treat 'invisible registrar' as marketing, not a right.)
- **corrected**: Record keeping is '2 years' (Reseller Sched. 3.10) -> Term + 2 years; authorisation evidence 'as long as legally possible'; registrant-accuracy evidence for the life of the registration (3.10 requires records 'during the Term of the Agreement and for two years thereafter', i.e. Term plus 2 years, not 2 years per record. Reseller Sched. 4.6 requires authorisation evidence 'for as long as legally possible'. R&M 1.3.3 requires supplementary documentary evidence 'for the life-time of the Registration'. Records must be produced to SP on reasonable request.)
- **corrected**: A 100-domains-per-year qualification exists but the pricing page FAQ contradicts it with 'no minimum purchase' -> No contradiction: price-list access has no minimum; customer qualification is 100 registered or transferred domains within a year, stated on both FAQ and pricing pages; not in the MSA text. (The FAQ states the 100-domain rule verbatim. But the pricing-page FAQ is ONE answer with two paragraphs: 'no minimum purchase required to access our domain reseller pricelist' and then 'to qualify as a CentralNic Reseller's customer, you should either register a minimum of 100 domains within one year or transfer at least 100...'. The pricing page therefore reaffirms the rule (as 'should') rather than contradicting it. The public MSA has no volume commitment, and its entire-agreement clause 26 supersedes prior communications. Enforcement, and whether the RSP General Agreement adds a minimum, is unverifiable. Get a written waiver.)
- **corrected**: Termination: 6 months' notice after 36 months, or immediately if no paid services are used -> 13.1 both limbs start at month 36; earlier termination is possible for breach (15-day cure), validation failure, price-increase refusal (2 weeks), amendment (30 days), or sunsetting (30 days); 13.7 requires transfer-out of all domains before the end of term. (MSA 13.1 lead-in 'Beginning 36 months after the Effective Date' governs both limbs. Omitted by the dossier: 13.2 either party may terminate for material breach after a 15-day cure, and any violation of SP terms/policies or Third-Party Policies, non-payment, or failure to answer urgent inquiries within 15 days is deemed a material breach. 13.5 failed validation can be treated as breach. 13.7 on termination Mosshatch must 'Transfer all Services ... to another provider prior to the End of the Term', pending orders stop, and SP is 'relieved of all responsibility for the further maintenance of Services not transferred'. Combined with the 60-day registration lock and the 2-week exit under 14.2, recently registered domains cannot be moved out quickly. This is the main exit risk.)
- **corrected**: Expired-domain 'utilization' clause lets SP park, auction, transfer or take over names; the MSA (clause 30) overrides the Registration Agreement's 14-day figure -> Registrants' own contract says 14 days after expiry (RA s.5) and loss of rights if renewal is unpaid 3 days before expiry; R&M 1.7 says 5 days; clause 30 does not resolve this. Ask CNR to switch utilisation off in writing. (R&M 1.7 quote verbatim. But clause 30 does not list the Registration Agreement, which is a separate contract with each registrant. The registrant is bound by RA s.5 (14 days) and by RA s.5 / T&C 4 ('3 days before' expiry non-payment = loses rights). Timelines of 3 days before, +5 days and +14 days coexist, plus the KB's 35-day renewal date. Mitigation missed: R&M 1.7 opens 'Unless otherwise agreed', and KB Subreseller Settings shows a lockable 'Domain utilisation' account setting (documented for subresellers; top-level account toggle unverified). Flow-down duty: Mosshatch must have registrants agree that non-payment is consent to utilisation, renewal, transfer and change of registrant.)
- **corrected**: The B2B/EU-consumer attestation binds only the account holder (Mosshatch), not its end customers -> Ambiguous; possible bar on EU individual/consumer registrants; remedy is immediate account termination unless written permission is granted. (The sign-up text says 'CentralNic Reseller is a B2B service that does not provide services to private consumers or individuals based in the EU... this account will only be used by either a legally established business entity or for the management of domain portfolios held outside the EU and not be used by private individuals based within the EU... this account may be terminated with immediate effect if CentralNic Reseller learns that I do not meet the above eligibility criteria unless CentralNic Reseller has previously granted explicit written permission'. The text does not say it applies only to the account holder, and the headline sentence is broader. Reseller Sched. 2 (No Consumer) is about the account holder, but the program page says resellers sell 'to the public'. The documents conflict, so the dossier's reading is an unverified interpretation. If Mosshatch will register domains for EU natural persons, obtain explicit written permission before onboarding.)
- **refuted**: Default renewal mode is automatic deletion at end of term (AUTORENEW merely recommended) -> Account default renewal mode is AutoRenew (KB renewal-system 3.2 and 6.1; MSA R&M 1.4.4). (KB renewal system 3.2: 'The default account setting is AutoRenew.' 6.1: 'reseller accounts are by default pre-configured for auto-renew.' Your First Day guide: AUTORENEW is 'the safe default'. MSA R&M 1.4.4: registrations 'will be automatically renewed' provided funds exist, unless set to Autoexpire/Autodelete. The sentence the dossier quotes ('automatic deletion of domains at the end of the registration period') is the KB's summary of the AutoExpire/AutoDelete account setting. Consequence: CNR renews and debits the prepaid balance by default, and Mosshatch owes that money under Reseller Sched. 3.6 even if the end customer never pays. Out of funds means the domain is removed and needs a paid restore.)
- **corrected**: Promo: .app and .dev USD 15.00 (1-year registration only) from 2026-07-01 to 2026-09-30 -> Promo needs Sales activation plus a newsletter or banner commitment; ends 2026-09-30. (Table rows verbatim. Missed condition: 'To take advantage of the promotional pricing, the registry requires that you actively promote the TLD to your customers - either through a newsletter or by placing a banner on your website. Please reach out to our Sales Team with these details so they can promptly activate the campaign in your account.' The promo is not automatic, needs a marketing commitment, and ends tomorrow, so it is not a usable price basis.)
- **unverifiable**: Data-handling terms (not addressed by the dossier): DPA and controller/processor roles (MSA 8 and R&M 1.3.8 incorporate a Data Processing Agreement 'signed separately by the Parties'. KB says a pre-filled DPA is in the control panel Downloads and can be accepted in the panel, 'valid without your written signature'. KB GDPR FAQ says Key-Systems is a 'data processor'. The Privacy Policy names Key-Systems GmbH and Team Internet Group PLC as joint controllers for their own processing and references a separate Domain Name Registration Privacy Policy that I did not locate. The DPA text is behind login and is not in the public legal index. MSA clause 30 does not rank the DPA or the RSP General Agreement. RA s.9 lets SP use customer data 'to improve the range of offered services'.)
- **unverifiable**: OT&E may or may not be usable before the EUR 75 activation (dossier: unknown) (The OT&E guide says 'OT&E is where you test an integration - it is not the account setup itself. If you have not yet funded your account... work through Your First Day first', which implies funding first but does not state a block. Credential statements conflict: 'OT&E credentials do not work against production' (REST guide) vs 'By default, OT&E credentials are identical to the production ones' (cutover guide).)

Missed by the analyst:
- Reseller Sched. 3.1-3.3: before ordering for any Customer-Client, Mosshatch must require that client to comply with the whole MSA and Schedules (incl. Anti-Abuse Policy) and all Third-Party Policies by incorporating them into its own agreements, and must be able to bind and enforce them. The dossier mentions only the Registration Agreement.
- Reseller Sched. 3.6: Mosshatch is liable to SP for all fees 'without regard to Customer's ability to collect fees owed to Customer by Customer-Clients'. Together with 4.5 (no DNS activation until payment is reasonably assured), this puts non-payment, fraud and chargeback risk on Mosshatch.
- MSA 13.7: on termination, Mosshatch must transfer all domains to another provider before the end of the term, and SP is relieved of maintaining anything not transferred. The 60-day registry lock and the 2-week exit right in 14.2 make a fast exit impractical for new names.
- Data Processing Agreement (MSA 8 / R&M 1.3.8): exists, is accepted in the control panel, and is not public. SP claims processor status while the privacy policy names Key-Systems and Team Internet as joint controllers. A separate Domain Name Registration Privacy Policy is referenced. Mosshatch must obtain registrants' explicit consent to processing by SP and registries (R&M 1.3.5, Reseller 4.13).
- Reseller Sched. 4.8 full text: SP may at any time update Registrant Information, initiate Transfers and request Renewals; registrants must accept Mosshatch's acts as their own; Mosshatch and SP are both 'designated agents'. R&M 1.4.7, 1.4.3 and 1.4.5 require registrant consent before contact/nameserver changes, transfers and deletions (no refund of unused term).
- R&M 1.7 flow-down: Mosshatch must have registrants agree that non-payment or non-renewal is consent to suspension, deletion or utilisation, including renewal, transfer and change of registrant. The Registration Agreement adds a '3 days before expiry' loss-of-rights clause, a 14-day post-expiry utilisation start, and an option to undo mis-priced premium transactions.
- ICANN/RAA duties to publish renewal and restore fees, the deletion and auto-renewal policy and the notification methods on the website (RAA 3.7.5.4-3.7.5.6; KB ERRP page), and to disclose renewal, restore and transfer fees at registration (Reseller Sched. 4.7). RAA 3.12.2: Mosshatch's registration agreement must contain all RAA-required provisions.
- MSA 23 (claims not asserted in court within 2 calendar years are waived), MSA 28 (SP records prevail), MSA 31 (no assignment or change of control without SP consent), MSA 25 (no use of the other party's name in publicity). The public MSA page carries no version or effective date and is a template, so snapshot and hash it. Order of precedence (clause 30) omits the RSP General Agreement, DPA and Registration Agreement.
- Promo pricing is not self-serve: it needs Sales activation and a newsletter/banner commitment, and .app/.dev promos expire 2026-09-30.
- Marketing vs contract conflicts: 99.9% uptime claimed vs 99% 'commercially reasonable efforts' with no credits; 'no revenue share, no surprise fees' vs an undisclosed account service fee; 'no mention of the underlying registrar' vs mandatory sponsoring-registrar identification.
- Anti-Abuse Policy allows action without notice for trusted-notifier reports, has a vague 'company policies and/or values' restriction, and requires flow-down of the policy to third parties.
- MSA 5.4 classes 'bulk registration attempts of deleted domains names' as abusive, which is relevant if Mosshatch offers backorder or drop-style automation.
- IANA lists a second accredited entity, Key-Systems, LLC (ID 1345); the KB .ai page still shows registry epp.whois.ai and T&C whois.ai, while the IANA .ai record points to nic.ai with an Identity Digital RDAP server (stale KB). Registry-specific .ai/.io terms bind registrants as Third-Party Policies.
- CNR customises ERRP expiry emails (sender, language) via API but charges 'a one time fee per template' for fully custom templates.

### Verifier lens: tech (confidence medium; 17 claims confirmed)

I re-fetched the OpenAPI specs for OT&E and production, about 25 KB pages, the pricing and promotions pages (raw HTML and Chromium-rendered), the status Atom feed and the npm SDK. Most of the analyst's technical claims hold. All 13 adapter methods map to real REST operations. The 1 command/second limit is documented for EPP only, so its application to REST is inference. There are no webhooks and no idempotency key. The OT&E sandbox exists and both REST hosts are live. The .com prices are exact. .ai, .io and .studio are not published anywhere public, and the two 15.00 promos end 2026-09-30. Corrections:
- The account default renewal mode is AutoRenew. The dossier says the default deletes the domain at end of term, which is wrong and matters for approval-gated renewals.
- StatusDomain `status[]` is not documented as EPP codes. A separate `x-registry-status[]` exists that the analyst missed.
- The OT&E and production specs share parameter names but differ in every operation's raw JSON, and the two hosts answer an unauthenticated request differently (401 vs 400).
- The OT&E password menu path is given three different ways.
- The 100-domain rule and "no minimum purchase" sit in the same FAQ answer, so it is not a page-versus-page contradiction.
Unverifiable without an account or an independent source:
- .ai and .io register and renew prices.
- The REST limit.
- Behaviour on a duplicate register.
- Whether OT&E works before funding.
- The Verisign fee claim.
- Event retention.
I also found a bi-weekly OT&E maintenance window and an unstated API changelog (none exists).

Not confirmed:
- **unverifiable**: register: a duplicate AddDomain (e.g. blind retry after timeout) returns 554 'Domain already registered' (Return code 554 'Domain already registered' and 546/555/540 exist in the return-code table, but no page says AddDomain returns 554 for a duplicate in the same account, nor what a repeated identical cltrid does. Needs an OT&E test. The analyst's 'safe retry' pattern (StatusDomain then event poll) is plausible but untested.)
- **corrected**: getDomain returns status[] carrying EPP codes such as clientTransferProhibited, plus rgp_status, transfer_lock, dates, renewalmode, pending_job, auth -> Read both status[] and x-registry-status[] (plus rgp_status, transfer_lock); do not assume status[] = EPP set; ccTLD (.ai/.io) status data may be thin. (Dates, rgp_status/rgp_enddate, transfer_lock, renewalmode, pending_job, auth, x-authcode-expiration all exist. But `status` is only described as 'Status of the domain' (AddDomain example returns ACTIVE) and a separate `x-registry-status[]` ('Status of the domain at the Registry') exists that the analyst did not list. KB says ccTLDs often do not offer EPP status. Which field carries clientTransferProhibited/redemptionPeriod, and what .ai/.io return, is undocumented; test in OT&E.)
- **corrected**: renew and default renewal mode: 'Default mode = delete at end of term; AUTORENEW recommended' (dossier lifecycle section) -> Account default renewalmode is AUTORENEW, so domains auto-renew and debit the prepaid balance unless the account or domain is set to AUTOEXPIRE/AUTODELETE/RENEWONCE. (KB renewal-system 3.2: 'The default account setting is AutoRenew'; 6.1: reseller accounts are 'by default pre-configured for auto-renew'; first-day guide calls AUTORENEW 'the safe default'. Domain-level DEFAULT means inherit the account mode; the 'automatic deletion' wording describes the alternative AutoExpire/AutoDelete strategy. Renewal date = expiry + 35 days for gTLDs (45-8-2) is confirmed. An unfunded AUTORENEW domain is left to expire and needs a paid restore.)
- **corrected**: Sandbox/OT&E exists (REST https://rest-ote.rrpproxy.net/v1, legacy api-ote.rrpproxy.net); credentials are the account login with OT&E password set in the live Control Panel -> OT&E password path is inconsistent across three docs; OT&E has a recurring bi-weekly maintenance window. (Existence confirmed: docs say 'every reseller' gets a dedicated OT&E and both REST hosts answer (unauthenticated OT&E GET /account -> 401 code 530; Swagger UI 200). Credential model confirmed (login = account login; separate OT&E password; REST guide: prod and OT&E credentials differ; cutover guide says identical by default). Correction: the password menu path is stated three ways (Account>Settings>Passwords; Account>Password & Security; Account>Security & Passwords). REST page says credentials are generated after account creation; usability before EUR 75 funding/verification remains unverifiable. Missed: OT&E has bi-weekly maintenance (even-week Wednesdays ~07:00-09:00 UTC in summer, up to 2 h) not shown on the status page.)
- **corrected**: OT&E and production OpenAPI specs have the same 287 operations and identical parameters (only version label differs) -> Same operations and parameter names (286/287), different response schemas and error behaviour; OT&E is not a byte-identical predictor of production. (Path set (208) and operation count (287) identical, and parameter/body property names identical in 286 of 287 operations (POST /acmeaccount differs). But every operation differs in raw JSON (x-ptf-command labels, response schemas, descriptions) and info.version is 2.0.0-ote.3 vs 0.0.3. Live behaviour also differs: unauthenticated GET /account returns HTTP 401/code 530 on OT&E but HTTP 400/code 505 on production.)
- **unverifiable**: .ai and .io register/renew (and .studio) wholesale prices are not public (Not in the static or Chromium-rendered tier table (30 TLDs), not in the promotions table (36 TLDs), not on the TLD list page, and the OpenAPI carries no prices. So the analyst's 'unverified' is correct, and I could not source them either. Docs do show structural facts that affect quoting: .ai registration 2-10 years, renewal 2-9 years (no 1-year price can exist), transfer bills 2 years; .io registration 1-10, renewal 1-9.)
- **corrected**: Pricing page contradicts itself: 100-domain qualification vs 'no minimum purchase' -> Same FAQ answer says no minimum to see the price list but 100 domains/year to qualify as a customer; confirm enforcement in writing. (Both statements sit in the same FAQ answer: 'no minimum purchase required to access our domain reseller pricelist' followed by 'to qualify as a CentralNic Reseller's customer, you should either register a minimum of 100 domains within one year or transfer at least 100 domains'. FAQ page repeats the 100-domain rule plus 'You'll also need API access'. Ambiguous rather than flatly contradictory; enforcement still unknown.)
- **unverifiable**: CNR blog says Verisign's .com fee rises from USD 10.26 to 10.97 from November 2026 (The sentence exists on CNR's blog, but Verisign's fee pages redirected to its home page and my web-search budget was exhausted, so there is no independent source. Only relevant as context for the Basic Plus 18.33 margin.)

Missed by the analyst:
- OT&E has a recurring bi-weekly maintenance window (even-numbered calendar weeks, Wednesday 09:00 CEST/07:00 UTC in summer, up to 2 hours, limited reachability) that is not listed on the status page: https://kb.centralnicreseller.com/help/newsletters-maintenances-status/
- StatusDomain also returns x-registry-status[] ('Status of the domain at the Registry'); `status[]` is only 'Status of the domain' (AddDomain example: ACTIVE). Which field carries EPP codes such as clientTransferProhibited or redemptionPeriod is undocumented, and the KB says ccTLDs often do not offer EPP status.
- Account default renewal mode is AutoRenew (KB renewal-system 3.2 and 6.1). The dossier's 'DEFAULT = delete at end of term' is wrong; out of the box, domains auto-renew and debit the prepaid balance without Mosshatch-side approval.
- OT&E and production behave differently on an unauthenticated GET /account (OT&E HTTP 401 code 530; production HTTP 400 code 505) and their response schemas differ in all 287 operations; POST /acmeaccount has a body property only in OT&E.
- The OT&E password menu path is documented three different ways (Account>Settings>Passwords; Account>Password & Security; Account>Security & Passwords).
- Extra transfer statuses FOREIGN_PENDING and USERTRANSFER, and extra writable domain statuses clientRenewProhibited, registrar-lock, registrar-hold; account property GENERAL-DEFAULT-CLIENTTRANSFERPROHIBITED and the caveat that auto-lock applies only where the registry supports it.
- Sign-up attestation full text: 'this account will only be used by either a legally established business entity or for the management of domain portfolios held outside the EU and not be used by private individuals based within the EU ... may be terminated with immediate effect'. This is broader than the analyst's shortened quote and may restrict EU-consumer end-registrants; flag for the legal lens: https://www.centralnicreseller.com/sign-up/
- Structural quoting constraints: .ai registration is 2-10 years and renewal 2-9 years (no 1-year price can exist); .io has 'Handle updates: no'; the tier boundaries are inconsistent (Basic Plus 'less than 249' tooltip vs 'less than 250' in the FAQ; exactly 1,000 domains falls in no tier).
- REST responses document only 200/default in the OpenAPI; HTTP status mapping exists in practice (401/400 observed) but is undocumented, so the adapter must branch on the JSON `code`, not the HTTP status.
- Price of the optional Anycast DNS tier (premiumdnsset=ANYCAST1) is not stated anywhere I found, although KeyDNS itself is described as free.

## reg-dynadot
Dossier: `reg-dynadot.md`

### Verifier lens: legal (confidence high; 16 claims confirmed)

I re-fetched the Reseller Agreement (v2023-08-15), GDG ToU and Dynadot ToU (both v2026-08-18), the reseller program pages, the ICANN RAA and the IANA registrar list. The analyst's quotes and overall reading hold: resale under Mosshatch's own brand and checkout is expressly contemplated, but the paper is one-sided, and the API clauses (Dynadot ToU s13.1, GDG ToU s12.1) and Dynadot ToU s3.1(b) need a written waiver. FALLBACK is a defensible role. Three things need correcting. (1) The RAA is cited from the superseded 2013-09-17 text; the agreement in force is the 21 Jan 2024 version (global amendment effective 5 Apr 2024). 3.12 and 3.7.7 are unchanged, but 3.18 now has a DNS Abuse mitigation duty. (2) Program requirements are not unpublished. Dynadot's blog of 2025-12-26 says no fees, no mandatory or minimum deposits, no quotas, approval "usually instant", and integration into "your own website, app, or platform" via the API. That is marketing, not contract (ToU s3.13 gives the ToU precedence), so it softens but does not cure the s13.1 risk. (3) Several clauses were missed: Dynadot ToU s3.1(d) (customer terms must be subordinate to Dynadot's ToU); the Reseller Agreement indemnity, one-way fee shifting and Dynadot third-party-beneficiary status; the total exclusion of provider liability (s3.4); the registry indemnity (GDG s14.5); no DPA, GDPR or transfer terms anywhere; the Dynadot ToU designated-agent clause covers only "You", not "Your customer"; and contract text that already permits auctioning expired reseller domains. Residual unknowns are inherent without an account: which paper binds a dynadot.com-created reseller, the sign-up and acceptance flow, and who sends ICANN notices.

Not confirmed:
- **corrected**: Registrant of record is the person whose data Mosshatch supplies (RAA 3.7.7); Mosshatch is the account holder and stays liable unless it names the customer within 7 days -> RNH = the customer whose data Mosshatch submits; Mosshatch is contractually liable unless it discloses the customer within 7 days; Designated Agent appointment for the customer is explicit only in the GDG ToU, so Mosshatch's own agreement should include it. (Reading is sound (RAA 3.7.7/3.7.7.3 unchanged in the 2024 text; Reseller Agreement and GDG s3.9 extend the 7-day rule). Corrections: the 7-day disclosure removes only wrongful-use liability, not GDG s4.2 account liability or the indemnities. Also, Dynadot ToU s11.3 appoints Dynadot as designated agent for 'You' only; the GDG s11.3 version adds 'and Your customer's'. If the Dynadot paper governs, Mosshatch's own registration agreement must have each end customer appoint the registrar as Designated Agent and opt out of the 60-day lock.)
- **corrected**: ICANN RAA flow-down provisions: 3.7.7 (registration agreement with RNH), 3.12 (reseller obligations), 3.18 (abuse contact, quoted as 'Registrar shall maintain an abuse contact') -> Cite the RAA as approved 21 Jan 2024 (in effect for existing registrars from 5 Apr 2024); 3.12 and 3.7.7 unchanged; 3.18.1-3.18.4 as renumbered, with the new DNS Abuse mitigation duty in 3.18.2. (Analyst cited the original 2013-09-17 text. ICANN's index says the RAA was approved 21 January 2024 with a global amendment effective 5 April 2024. I fetched the current text: 3.12.x and 3.7.7.x are unchanged, and RAA 1.26 defines 'Reseller', so Mosshatch is one and must be under a written agreement. 3.18 was rewritten: 3.18.1 now covers 'DNS Abuse and Illegal Activity' with acknowledgement to the reporter; new 3.18.2 requires prompt mitigation on actionable evidence of DNS Abuse; old 3.18.2/3.18.3 became 3.18.3/3.18.4. Practical effect: more, faster registrar-initiated action against reseller-customer domains under GDG s7.3.)
- **corrected**: Program requirements: free to join; application-gated; account must have 'no existing domains or active orders'; no published minimum deposit, volume commitment or KYC -> Dynadot publicly states no fees, no mandatory or minimum deposits, no quotas and near-instant approval (non-contractual); eligibility requires a clean account; the API still draws from a prepaid balance. (Free, the 'no existing domains or active orders' eligibility (with 'such as' implying more criteria), 'Submit your application for review' and GDG 'our team will assess' are all confirmed. But deposit/volume are published, not unknown: Dynadot blog 2025-12-26 says 'no registration fees, no mandatory deposits, no requirement to pre-purchase domains', 'no minimum deposits, and no sales quotas', and account approval 'usually instant' with 'no complicated approval process'. This is marketing, not contractual; the Reseller Agreement is silent. Program KYC remains unpublished/unverifiable.)
- **corrected**: Expired reseller-account domains entering Dynadot expired-domain auctions is unverified -> Permitted by contract (GDG ToU s8.4-8.5, Dynadot ToU s8.4-8.5); practice for reseller accounts unverified. (Contract already answers the permission question: GDG ToU s8.4 lets GDG 'auction for sale any domain name that is not renewed within ... the first 30 calendar days of the Post-Expiration Period' and s8.5 lets it transfer the name to another registrant, with no reseller carve-out; s8.1(b) removes the holder's right to use the domain post-expiry. Dynadot help (updated 2026/06/04): a domain 'not renewed by the registrant and ... past its grace renewal period ... will go to expired auction'. Whether this is applied to reseller accounts in practice remains unverified.)
- **unverifiable**: Who sends ICANN-required emails (expiry reminders, WHOIS verification, change-of-registrant) for reseller-account domains is undocumented (Dynadot help says the WHOIS Contact Record Verification email is sent to the contact owner and that failing to verify within 15 days blocks managing or renewing the domain; the webhook field verify_link is 'Not returned for reseller accounts'. No source says who mails an end customer of a reseller or whether the mail is white-labelled. Needs written confirmation.)
- **unverifiable**: Which terms govern a reseller account (2023 Reseller Agreement + Dynadot ToU vs GDG ToU 2026-08-18) is unresolved (Confirmed unresolved from public sources: the Reseller Agreement imports the Dynadot ToU and has no precedence clause; GDG ToU s1.2 says it is 'the entire and only agreement', and its Part I applies to 'any person or entity which creates a user account with GDG'; the GDG RDAP notice names the GDG terms. The sign-up and acceptance flow is behind login. Ask Dynadot/GDG in writing which paper binds a dynadot.com-created reseller account.)

Missed by the analyst:
- Dynadot ToU s3.1(d): any agreement between the reseller and its customer must say Dynadot's ToU prevail over it (and s3.1(a) requires disclosing Dynadot, not GDG, as registrar). Incompatible with Mosshatch owning its customer contract if the Dynadot paper applies.
- Reseller Agreement indemnity of GDG and its partners/affiliates for 'Your violation of this Agreement, use of Dynadot's website and participation in GDG's distribution channel'; GDG ToU s3.2 (same) and s14.5 (indemnify the registry operator within 30 days of demand for claims 'arising out of or relating in any way to' registrations); one-way attorney-fee shifting in GDG's favor; Dynadot is a third-party beneficiary of the Reseller Agreement (GDG ToU s1.5 and Dynadot ToU s1.5 add registries, Verisign and 8648255 Canada Ltd).
- Total exclusion of provider liability (ToU s3.4: no liability whatsoever, no consequential damages, disclaims loss from unauthorized use of the reseller's and its customers' accounts and from data non-delivery). Combined with s4.2, the reseller bears account-compromise losses.
- No GDPR, controller/processor, DPA, international-transfer or sub-processor terms in any of the three documents; Part II allows targeted marketing, disclosure of non-public RDDS to third parties and ML training on processed data. Material for EU/UK end customers.
- ToU s3.13 (both documents): anything 'associated with this website' that conflicts with the Agreement yields to it, so marketing statements (white-label, API in your own platform, no deposits) are not contractual. Also conflicting entire-agreement clauses (Reseller Agreement vs GDG ToU s1.2) and no precedence rule between them.
- Dynadot ToU s11.3 appoints Dynadot as designated agent for 'You' only; GDG ToU s11.3 adds 'and Your customer's'. Mosshatch's own registration agreement should contain the customer's designation and 60-day-lock opt-out.
- GDG ToU s2.2: willful inaccuracy, failure to update within 7 days, or failure to respond for over 15 days to GDG's accuracy inquiries is a material breach and basis for suspension/cancellation; Dynadot help says an unverified contact blocks renewal and settings changes after 15 days. Mosshatch needs a fast relay to end customers.
- The RAA in force is the 21 Jan 2024 version (global amendment effective 5 Apr 2024), not the 2013-09-17 text cited; 3.18 now includes a DNS Abuse mitigation duty (3.18.2) that increases the chance of registrar-initiated suspension of reseller-customer domains. RAA 1.26 defines 'Reseller', so Mosshatch must be under a written registrar agreement (3.12).
- Dynadot's own marketing (blog 2025-12-26 and reseller guide) says program resellers may integrate 'your own website, app, or platform' via the API and that there are no mandatory or minimum deposits, no quotas and usually-instant approval; the same post says 'To them, you're the registrar' and 'Dynadot is an ICANN-accredited registrar', which contradicts the contract's no-ICANN-accreditation-claim and identify-GDG duties.
- GDG ToU s9: until nameservers are set, a default parked page may show third-party links and ads 'related to ... Your and Your customers' competitors'; s11.4(i) allows refusing transfers when the reseller is 'in default of any obligation towards GDG'; s4.5 allows deactivation of inactive accounts; s5.3 and s6.4 make fees and renewals non-refundable.
- Reseller Agreement's stale ICANN registrant-education link (http://www.icann.org/en/registrars/registrant-rights-responsibilities-en.htm redirects to a 2009-RAA-era page); Dynadot's footer uses https://www.icann.org/resources/pages/benefits-2013-09-16-en. Confirm which URL GDG accepts.
- Reseller-account API restrictions beyond the two the analyst named: 'The feature is currently not available to reseller accounts' also applies to add_backorder_request and delete_backorder_request; register/transfer_in return 400 'The domain is not supported by your account type.'
- Privacy/proxy clause in the Reseller Agreement: GDG may bar the reseller from knowingly accepting registrations from unaccredited privacy/proxy providers and the reseller must follow the ICANN Specification on Privacy and Proxy Registrations. Mosshatch should not layer its own privacy or proxy service over customer registrations without checking this.

### Verifier lens: tech (confidence high; 23 claims confirmed)

I could not refute any load-bearing technical or pricing claim. I re-derived them from Dynadot's own machine-readable command feed, the rendered docs, the pricing page (Regular and Reseller tabs), the six TLD pages, the grace-deletion page and the help pages. Corrections are minor: the IP-block 403 message is documented only on 3 CNNIC commands, getAuthCode's skip-lock requirement comes from the legacy docs, and "prepaid only" needs a caveat. The analyst missed several things. A REST v1.0.0 (103 commands, not labelled Beta) is live alongside v2. An `Accept: */*` header is rejected. `register` has an undocumented daily quota. GDG advertises "at cost" pricing that the Dynadot Reseller tab does not show. Reseller-tab .dev and .app prices sit above retail list. I could not verify a real reseller price via `get_tld_price`, sandbox behaviour for transfers, expiry or DNS, webhook retries, or outage history. No credentials were available and the WebSearch budget was already exhausted.

Not confirmed:
- **corrected**: getAuthCode: synchronous GET /domains/{d}/transfer_auth_code; 409 during 60-day lock, expired/expiring, UDRP, user auction; unlock_domain_for_transfer needs 'api skip lock agreement'; account must be unlocked -> Skip-lock/unlock prerequisites are documented for legacy api3 (and for setLock in v2), not for the v2 auth-code endpoint. (Endpoint, params and the 409 list are confirmed (also 409 'is disabled'; 404 'The domain is non-auth'; 400 'does not need auth code'). The skip-lock-agreement requirement and 'must unlock your domain and account' come from the LEGACY api3 docs only ('unlock_domain_for_transfer (Optional. Requires api skip lock agreement to use)'). The REST v2 feed ties the skip-lock agreement only to set_domain_lock_status. REST v2 behaviour for a locked domain or locked account is therefore undocumented.)
- **unverifiable**: Listed prices are what a reseller/API customer pays (one-tier pricing since Jan 2025; Reseller tab identical for .com/.ai/.io/.studio) (The price page FAQ says the two tabs are 'one for regular and one for reseller accounts', and the Jan 22 2025 blog says all account levels get the same prices. So the public Reseller tab is the best proxy, and it equals retail for four of six TLDs (no wholesale discount visible). The price actually charged to an API caller comes from get_tld_price / search show_price for the account's price_level, and I could not call it without an account. Whether promo prices or coupon codes apply to API orders is undocumented. GDG's own page claims prices 'listed at cost, straight from the registries' but shows none (see missed).)
- **corrected**: All orders come from a prepaid balance (HTTP 402 when empty); prepay min $5 non-refundable; auto top-up needs an account at least 1 year old -> Balance is the documented and expected funding path (402 when short), but an order_payment_required / 'Waiting for Payment' state exists, so do not assume every API order fails fast. (Confirmed: 402 'Insufficient funds.' on register/renew/transfer_in; prepay minimum $5 USD and non-refundable; auto-prepay 'only ... available when your Dynadot account is at least one year old and has an order history'; the WHMCS listing says operations are billed against the balance. Correction: the feed defines an `order_payment_required` webhook with status 'Waiting for Payment' and a payment_method enum (credit card, paypal, bank wire ...), so orders that wait for payment exist on the platform. Whether an API order can land in that state is undocumented, so 'balance only' is likely but not proven.)
- **corrected**: IP allow-list: blocked callers get 403; reseller accounts need not enter any IP since 2025-10-09 (REST only) -> 403-on-blocked-IP is documented only for create/set/remove_cnnic_privacy; behaviour elsewhere is undocumented. (Allow-list mechanics (single IP or CIDR, at least 10 minutes) and the 2025-10-09 changelog line ('Reseller account no longer required to enter at least 1 IP to use API(RESTful API only)') are confirmed. The specific 403 message 'The request is not allowed from the current IP address' appears only in the error lists of 3 CNNIC-privacy commands; the general docs list only a generic 403. What other commands return for a blocked IP is undocumented.)

Missed by the analyst:
- REST v1.0.0 exists and is live: 103 commands under /restful/v1/... (register, renew, transfer_in, transfer_status, domain_lock, records, auth code, orders, authorize_transfer_away), on prod and sandbox, and the V1.0.0 docs view carries no Beta banner (only the selector label 'V2.0.0 Beta'). It lacks remove_dns (DELETE records), get/set_nameserver, set_account_lock_status, post_grace_delete, power_search and domain_appraisal, and its feed has no per-command error catalogue. On v1 a bad key returns HTTP 200 with body code 401 'Invalid key', unlike v2's real HTTP 401. Dossier only discusses v2 and legacy api3.
- Default HTTP-client header trap: `Accept: */*` (curl and most libraries) is rejected with HTTP 200 and body {"code":400,... "Unsupported accept value in the header."}. Clients must send Accept: application/json or application/xml. Combined with 'HTTP 200 + body code' semantics this can look like success to naive clients.
- `register` and single `search` carry a documented daily-quota 429 ('Daily quota for the command has been reached') in addition to the 1,000/day bulk-search figure; the register quota size is not published anywhere I found (only search, domain_appraisal and register have this error).
- GDG's reseller page says 'Below, you'll find all the registration, renewal, and transfer prices listed at cost, straight from the registries' (heading 'Over 530+ TLDs'), yet the table shows only serial/name/type for 612 names and no prices. A separate GDG wholesale price list may exist and could differ from the Dynadot Reseller tab; the dossier does not mention this claim.
- Reseller tab shows no wholesale discount: identical to Regular for .com/.ai/.io/.studio, and higher than both promo and non-promo retail for .dev/.app registration. A reseller's cost basis is roughly retail list, so margin comes only from Mosshatch's markup, and Dynadot retail customers may pay less than Mosshatch's cost during promos.
- transfer_in has a documented 202 Accepted response as well as 200; a client must treat both as success and then poll.
- An `order_payment_required` webhook with status 'Waiting for Payment' and a payment_method enum exists, which weakens the 'prepaid balance only, else 402' mental model; need to ask Dynadot whether API orders can enter that state.
- The two 429 texts differ: the overview says 'maximum allowed requests within the concurrent limit' (concurrency) while per-command lists say 'Rate limit exceeded. Please try again after 60 seconds.' Backoff logic should handle both.
- Google Registry TLDs (app, dev, page, how, new, day, zip, mov, foo, rsvp, ing, meme, dad) are all absent from the API-supported list (only .soy present), which points to a systematic platform gap and makes .dev/.app/.page unsuitable to assume without a sandbox test.
- register/transfer_in list 400 'The domain is not supported by your account type.', a possible mechanism by which a reseller-account API call refuses a TLD that the public price page shows.
- Aftermarket features are closed to reseller accounts (403 'The feature is currently not available to reseller accounts.'; 'The auction type is not supported for the reseller program'), so the expired-auction and backorder paths that a normal account gets do not apply to a reseller account.
- Docs statement that the test/live mode is 'determined by the API key used', while a separate sandbox base URL is also required; and the REST docs say bulk creations, updates and deletes are not supported (one object or action per request) even though the legacy API has bulk_register for 100 domains.

## reg-gandi
Dossier: `reg-gandi.md`

### Verifier lens: legal (confidence high; 24 claims confirmed)

I could not refute the analyst's core legal conclusion. Gandi's own paper and marketing authorise an independent company to resell to its own customers under its own brand and checkout, ordering through the API. I re-fetched and read all seven governing contracts and six per-TLD special conditions myself. I found no resale prohibition, no "own use only" API clause, no approval gate, no minimum deposit and no volume commitment. Three analyst statements need correction. The Art. 8 non-reimbursement rule has a written-exception carve-out. The exit right under Art. 11.1 needs Gandi's acceptance or a registered letter with one month's notice. The US paper is a three-document, two-law split, not a two-document conflict. Several ICANN and .ai details also change. The analyst missed about a dozen clauses, and four matter most for Mosshatch. Art. 4.2 bars using the domain as collection leverage and requires honoring customer rights despite non-payment. MSA Art. 12.2 makes contracting for a third party without mandate an immediate-termination breach, which bears on agent purchases. Art. 7, Art. 9 and RAA 3.12 bar presenting Mosshatch as a registrar, and the brief calls it one. Gandi may withdraw any Service at any time, and the reseller position cannot be assigned without consent. Pricing, IANA ID 81 and the Feb 2023 ownership change all check out. I did not check the sandbox, idempotency, webhook or single-name-check claims, and the ICANN compliance history remains unchecked.

Not confirmed:
- **corrected**: Gandi will not reimburse prepaid services if the customer does not pay (Reseller Art. 8) -> Default: no reimbursement for services prepaid on behalf of customers who did not pay, unless customer service expressly validates otherwise in writing; operations that fail or can still be cancelled at the registry are reimbursed. (Art. 8 begins 'Unless otherwise expressly validated by Our customer service department', so a written exception is possible. It also promises reimbursement for 'any operation that could not be completed, provided that this operation can still be cancelled and reimbursed by the Registry'. The FAQ separately says auto-renewals cannot be refunded and 'the risk of non-payment must be borne by the reseller'.)
- **corrected**: Reseller may leave anytime (one month notice by letter) -> Exit by request subject to Gandi's acceptance, or by registered letter with acknowledgment on one month's notice; plus exit duties under Art. 11.3. (Art. 11.1: termination by email request is 'subject to acceptance by Gandi'; the unconditional route is a registered letter with one month's prior notice. Exit also carries duties (11.3): seek a buyer for the domains on ceasing activity, and get customers' express written agreement before management passes to Gandi.)
- **corrected**: Three regional counterparties; US Reseller Agreement (French law) conflicts with US General Terms (California law) -> US: Reseller Agreement under French law (venue Gandi US Inc.'s office); General Terms and Domain contract under California law and courts. LU: Luxembourg law and courts. Intl: French law, Paris courts. (Counterparties confirmed. The US position is understated: three documents, two laws. US Reseller Agreement Art. 17.1 French law with venue at Gandi US Inc.'s registered office; US General Terms Art. 27.1 California law with exclusive California courts; US Domain registration contract (listed by the analyst, never read) Art. 17 California law and courts. Reseller Agreement prevails over the General Terms (Art. 1.2). The US and LU papers say the counterparty is itself a reseller of Gandi SAS, so Mosshatch would be second-tier there. The three Reseller Agreements are otherwise textually identical (sentence diff).)
- **corrected**: ICANN flow-downs: Art. 4.2/4.3.2 obligations and RAA 3.12.2 / 3.7.5.6 as underlying rules -> RAA 3.7.5.6 uses 'should'; the mandatory obligation is the contractual Art. 4.3.2. (RAA 3.12.2 quote is accurate and 3.12 makes the registrar responsible for reseller compliance. RAA 3.7.5.6 says a registrar 'should' state the redemption fee, not 'must'. The binding duty on Mosshatch comes from Reseller Art. 4.3.2 (publish renewal, late-renewal and restoration rates, expiry-notice terms, two ICANN links).)
- **corrected**: .ai lifecycle: late renewal 30 vs 45 days conflict (treat 30 as safe) and transfer term 1 vs 2 years unresolved -> .ai: 45-day hold with late renewal, then 30-day redemption; transfer extends registration by 2 years. (The binding .ai special conditions v3.0 (15 Jan 2025) resolve both: expiry hold is 45 days with late renewal at normal price, then a 30-day redemption period; on transfer 'the registration period is extended by two years'. The docs deadlines page and transfer table are the outliers. .com, .io and .studio special conditions match the analyst (+1 year on transfer, 45-day hold, 30-day redemption).)
- **unverifiable**: Sandbox limited to .com/.net/.org/.fr without transfers; no idempotency key; no webhooks; one-name availability checks (Outside the legal lens; not re-checked by me, so not confirmed.)

Missed by the analyst:
- Reseller Art. 4.2 forbids soliciting Gandi's intervention 'to put pressure on Your customer via the domain name(s)' and requires honoring owners' rights 'even in the event of non-payment by your customers'. Mosshatch's dunning cannot rely on locking or suspending domains. This is consistent with ICANN Transfer Policy 3.9.1 and 3.10 (transfer may not be blocked for non-payment of future periods or used to secure payment). The brief's transfer-out 'cooling-off' must stay within the 5-day AuthInfo rule (I.A.5.2).
- MSA Art. 12.2 makes 'the conclusion of a Service Agreement in the name and on behalf of third parties without authorization or mandate' a material breach allowing immediate termination without notice, and Reseller Art. 4.2 requires written proof of authority on first request. Agent-initiated purchases need an auditable human approval record per order; the brief's passkey approval log can serve.
- MSA Art. 11: Gandi may withdraw or cease providing any Service at any time for any reason (best-effort six months' notice when withdrawn from sale). Reseller Art. 3.1 calls the right 'revocable' and 'non-transferable', so Mosshatch cannot assign the reseller position or customer base to an acquirer without Gandi's written consent (MSA Art. 24).
- Liability asymmetry: Gandi's liability is capped at amounts paid for the unavailable service and excludes indirect loss (MSA Art. 16; Domain contract Art. 12), while Mosshatch indemnifies Gandi, registries and their officers with no stated cap (Reseller Art. 5; MSA Art. 17). Registrants also indemnify the registry, Identity Digital and Gandi within 30 days of demand (.ai and .studio special conditions).
- Branding: Reseller Art. 9 lets Mosshatch use the 'Gandi' mark only to communicate about Gandi's services, revocable at any time, and forbids claiming registrar or accredited status; RAA 3.12.1-3.12.3 bar resellers from representing themselves as ICANN-accredited and require identifying the sponsoring registrar. The project brief calls Mosshatch 'a domain registrar'; customer-facing copy must say domains are registered through Gandi SAS, an ICANN-accredited registrar.
- Flow-down deliverables: the Domain contract preamble obliges the reseller to give each owner 'a copy of the Agreement'; Reseller Art. 4.2 requires inserting Annex 1 owner obligations into Mosshatch's own contracts; Art. 4.3.2 requires publishing renewal, late-renewal and restoration rates plus expiry-notification terms and two ICANN links (so the flat-fee pricing page must still state restoration fees); Art. 14 requires informing customers about data collection and Whois publication and warrants their consent.
- Data protection: Mosshatch is the controller and carries the DPA Art. 6.2-6.4 duties (inform data subjects, answer access requests, notify its own authority of breaches). Domain data flows to ICANN, an escrow agent and registries outside the EU under SCCs or Art. 49(1)(b). None of the six launch TLDs appear in the DPA's sub-processor appendix. This is relevant to the public hatchkind.com gallery.
- Website Terms of Use s.4 forbids mass or automated queries to Gandi's Whois database; use the API's /v5/domain/check, not whois.gandi.net, for availability. s.3 restricts redistribution of data communicated through the services (garbled wording, low risk for showing Mosshatch's own prices).
- Per-TLD special conditions (the analyst listed the index but opened none): all six read; none restricts resale; all reserve registry sole-discretion rights to deny, cancel, suspend or transfer domains; .ai and .io are open to anyone; the .io registry is Internet Computer Bureau Ltd with Identity Digital as technical operator, and the v1.5 text (Nov 2023) has no retirement or sovereignty clause.
- Notice mechanics and language: contract changes are notified only 'when You log in'; French versions prevail (MSA Art. 26.3, Domain contract Art. 16; the en-GB Reseller Agreement calls both languages authentic but French prevails). Mosshatch needs a process to monitor contract.gandi.net for new versions.
- Prepaid-funding and audit terms: Gandi may disable the org if a card top-up is reversed and the balance cannot cover it (Schedule 1 Art. 3.3.3), and repeated payment problems allow cancellation (MSA Art. 5.4); material breach may incur administrative fees debited from prepaid (MSA Art. 12.2); MSA Art. 3.4 audit right over identity and authority documents.
- Still unresolved: ICANN compliance history of Gandi SAS (the ICANN notices URL returned 404 and web search budget was exhausted); whether Gandi SAS holds a direct written agreement with a second-tier US or LU reseller such as Mosshatch; which region's paper Mosshatch would be assigned.

### Verifier lens: tech (confidence high; 19 claims confirmed)

I found no refuted claim. I re-fetched the v5 API reference pages (domains, livedns, billing, organization, authentication, reference, changelog), the sandbox guide, docs.gandi.net pages (reseller FAQ and benefits, price rates, deadlines, transfer table, autorenew, prepaid, PAT), the status feed, two helpdesk articles (headless Chromium), and all price pages. Every price matched to the cent, including .com, .ai and .io register and renew on grids A and E, and .dev, .app and .studio. Every coverage row matched the API docs. Nothing was exercised live: I created no account and made no authenticated call, so behaviour claims are documentation-only. The analyst's unverified list is accurate.

Corrections:
- The 2-9 year renewal figures (.com A $34.54, E $26.45; .dev $35.98; .app $43.18) are labelled "Promo Renewal" with a struck-through normal price. They are not a permanent tier.
- Autorenew is not paid from prepaid only. Gandi tries prepaid first, then any saved payment method.
- DNS record DELETE returns 204, and PUT documents 201, not a blanket 200/201.
- A missing token returns 401 with only {"message":...}. A bad or expired bearer returns 403 with the documented {object,cause,code,message} shape, so expiry and scope failures share a status.
- "No incident touching the six launch TLDs" is too strong. Three provider-wide incidents were not TLD-scoped, and change_owner had "up to 12 hours delay" on 2026-09-07.
- Gandi's own docs disagree on Filtered-Count: the changelog says removed 2026-07-27, but the list and billing reference pages still list it.

Price basis: there is no wholesale list; the word "wholesale" appears on none of the Gandi pages I fetched. The price-rates page, the reseller pricing page and the reseller benefits page point resellers to the A-E volume grid. The Billing and check APIs expose a per-organisation grid. I could not observe an authenticated quote for a real reseller organisation, so "a new reseller pays grid A" is inferred, not seen.

Not confirmed:
- **unverifiable**: 72-hour unlock delay in the web UI unless the account uses TOTP MFA; whether it applies to API/PAT calls is unverified (Helpdesk article 18536928521244 re-read via headless Chromium: 'the unlocking process for your domain may take up to 72 hours ... does not apply if your user account is secured with multi-factor authentication using TOTP' (updated ~1 year ago). It says nothing about the API and the lock route docs are silent. The UI claim is confirmed; API applicability cannot be checked without an authenticated call.)
- **corrected**: DNS record CRUD via LiveDNS at RRset granularity: POST 409/200/201, PUT overwrite, PATCH add/remove values, DELETE, whole-zone PUT/DELETE; TTL 300-2592000; writes return immediately with 200/201 -> POST 201/200/409; PUT 201; PATCH 200; DELETE 204 (All verbs and semantics confirmed (POST 'Will raise a 409 conflict if the record already exists, and return a 200 OK if the record already exists with the same values'; PATCH verb added 2025-03-20; TTL 300-2592000; whole-zone PUT/DELETE exist). Correction: DELETE returns 204, PUT RRset documents 201, PATCH 200, so 'writes return 200/201' is imprecise.)
