- **unverifiable**: register idempotency/duplicate handling: 'duplicate or retried POST behaviour undocumented' and recommended GET-before-retry pattern (Docs are silent (confirmed absence), so the analyst's 'undocumented' statement is accurate, but what actually happens on a duplicate POST (409, double charge, dedupe) cannot be checked without authenticated calls. The recommended reconciliation pattern is sound but is the analyst's design, not a Gandi feature.)
- **corrected**: .com prices: register $11.00 (A and E); renew A $38.38, E $29.39 (2-9y A $34.54, E $26.45); transfer $11.00; restore $55.00 -> 2-9y .com renewal $34.54 (A) / $26.45 (E) is a promo price (All numbers match the per-TLD price pages (grids A-E fetched; B/C $38.38, D $30.79 also match). Correction: the 2-9y per-year figures are shown as 'Promo Renewal' (raw HTML: <del>$38.38</del> then $34.54 under a 'Promo' badge), not a permanent multi-year tier. The 1-year renewal is $38.38 at A/B/C, $30.79 at D, $29.39 at E.)
- **corrected**: Autorenew: opt-in, attempts at 31/15/1 days (60 days for app/dev/org/net/info), cannot be cancelled or refunded once launched, paid from prepaid -> Autorenew tries prepaid first, then any saved payment method (Schedule, exceptions (also es/de at 30 days) and non-refundability confirmed. Funding is not prepaid-only: 'The renewal will first try to use money in your prepaid account. If your prepaid account does not have sufficient funds ... the system will then use any saved payment methods'. Prepaid can also auto-refill from the default saved card at a threshold; the API has no top-up route (Billing API has only info, info/{sharing_id}, price/{type}).)
- **corrected**: Operational incidents 2025-09..2026-09 include none affecting the six launch TLDs -> No TLD-specific incident for the six launch TLDs, but at least three generic domain/API incidents could have affected them (Feed confirmed (50 incidents through 2026-09-10; status now all-operational; ccTLD-specific outages as listed). But several incidents are provider-wide and not TLD-scoped: 2025-12-16 'Database incident' (~9 h, emergency maintenance on the main DB cluster; components include PUBLIC API v5, Domain name registration, LiveDNS, Billing/payments), 2025-11-27 'Domain update delayed' (Domain registration + API v5), 2026-07-21 'Domain name availability issue'. The 2026-09-07 change_owner incident stated 'up to 12 hours delay'.)

Missed by the analyst:
- Changelog 2026-08-27: new LiveDNS route POST /v5/livedns/domains/{fqdn}/verification/{rrset_name}/{rrset_type} (TXT/CNAME only, required scope dns:domainverify). It is a narrowly scoped DNS-challenge write path, useful for a least-privilege agent token.
- The reseller flag can be set through the Organization API (PATCH /v5/organization/organizations/{id} reseller=true, irreversible; also settable on create), so onboarding is not UI-only. PAT creation is UI-only: the API can only renew an existing PAT, so a lapsed PAT must be recreated in the Gandi Admin UI.
- An expired, invalid or under-scoped bearer returns HTTP 403 (not 401), so token expiry cannot be told apart from a scope failure by status code. A missing token returns 401 with only {message}. Every response carries a Trace-ID header worth logging for support.
- Domain/billing list docs still show Filtered-Count although the 2026-07-27 changelog says it was removed; the changelog shows no deprecation policy, and breaking changes ship in place.
- State/subdivision fields must be UN/LOCODE codes with a hyphen (for example US-MN) per docs.gandi.net contacts_api. Owner-field requirements differ between the API schema (country, email, family, given, streetaddr, type) and the reseller FAQ (adds zip, city, phone, and a legacy password); validate with Dry-Run: 1.
- Autorenew falls back to any saved payment method when prepaid is short, and prepaid can auto-refill from the default card at a threshold. Billing API exposes outstanding_amount (payment by terms) and prepaid_monthly_invoice, so deferred-payment orgs exist. POST /restore documents no Dry-Run header.
- price_rates docs say Corporate Services customers have their own negotiated price rate outside the A-E table. The /domain/tld page says B-E rates are 'available' to resellers or customers with over 45 domains depending on volume; neither is mentioned in the dossier and both are worth raising with Gandi.
- Multi-year renewal per-year prices on .com/.dev/.app are promo-flagged (strikethrough normal price) and may be time-limited. The API surfaces promos via discount/normal_price fields, but eligibility for reseller sharing_id orders is unverified.
- POST /v5/domain/transferin/{domain}/available and GET /v5/domain/domains/{domain}/renew return per-domain minimum/maximum durations. They are the runtime way to resolve the .ai 2-year renewal unit and the .ai transfer-term conflict.
- Provider-wide incident risk: the 2025-12-16 database incident (about 9 hours, emergency maintenance on the main database cluster) listed API v5, domain registration, LiveDNS and billing among its components. On 2026-09-07 change_owner operations could be delayed up to 12 hours.

## reg-namecheap
Dossier: `reg-namecheap.md`

### Verifier lens: legal (confidence medium; 15 claims confirmed)

Adversarial legal/program check of the Namecheap dossier, all fetched 2026-09-29. The analyst's central position holds: Namecheap's support pages, verbatim, say there is no domain reseller program, that you may resell via the API at your own prices, and that the API costs the same as retail. I could not read the binding contracts either. Universal ToS, Registration Agreement, Refund Policy, DPA and Court-order policy return Cloudflare 403 to curl and headless Chromium ("Just a moment"). The challenge host brunhild.challenges.cloudflare.com is connect_rejected by egress policy (proxy status log), and I did not work around it. The Wayback availability API says snapshots exist (ToS 20260912160849, Registration Agreement 20260912162304), but web.archive.org tunnels close. WebSearch budget was already exhausted (200/200). So the decisive question, whether the ToS and Registration Agreement permit white-label resale with automated ordering, or contain own-use-only, suspension, price-change, indemnity or data clauses, stays unverifiable. Only the support-doc permission is confirmed.

Corrections and findings the analyst missed:
- The RAA cite is to the superseded 2013 text. In the current RAA (21 Jan 2024, global amendment effective 5 Apr 2024) Reseller is 1.26, and 3.12.2 now names lookup.icann.org. The substance of 3.12 is unchanged.
- The Transfer Policy cited is the 2016 version, which ICANN says ran "through 20 August 2025". The current 2024-02-21 policy keeps 5.2 to 5.4.
- The sitemap-based "no domain reseller or API agreement" inference is weaker than presented. The footer links a legal page (domain-registration-data-disclosure-policy-guide) that is not in the sitemap, and the /legal/ index is blocked.
- "Unpublished" 50+ tier pricing is not established: /domains/tier-pricing/ exists on the site (403 to me).
- "No KYC" is overstated: Namecheap documents ad hoc identity and payment verification, and account locks that suspend services.
- The .studio price basis is wrong. Namecheap's own KB 10835 (updated 9/23/2026) shows .studio at $39.98 register, $52.98 renew and $39.98 transfer, rising on 2026-10-06 to $51.48, $68.48 and $51.48.
- Suspension risk is account-level, not just domain-level.

Overall the FALLBACK recommendation is supportable, but the unread ToS and Registration Agreement should be gating items before any reliance.

Notes file: no separate verify .md was written, because this session forbids writing report files. All verdicts and evidence are inline here. Raw fetched evidence (KB texts, RAA and Transfer Policy text, sitemaps, SDK docs) is saved under working-directory/research/nc-verify-legal/.

Not confirmed:
- **unverifiable**: The binding Universal ToS / Registration Agreement permit white-label resale, automated API ordering, or contain no own-use-only, suspension, price-change or indemnity traps (Could not be checked. Every /legal/* URL returns 403 (curl, UA-spoofed browser UA). Headless Chromium gets the Cloudflare "Just a moment" page, and its challenge request fails with ERR_TUNNEL_CONNECTION_FAILED; the proxy status log shows connect_rejected for brunhild.challenges.cloudflare.com:443 (egress policy, not bypassed). The sandbox host www.sandbox.namecheap.com legal paths and /support/api/intro/ are also 403. The Wayback availability API reports snapshots (ToS 20260912160849, Registration Agreement 20260912162304) but web.archive.org tunnels close and archive.ph is unreachable. tosdr.org has only bot-generated PENDING points (2021, unreviewed), which is not usable. Nothing in any reachable source shows the text, so resale permission rests entirely on support articles.)
- **corrected**: Namecheap's legal sitemap lists no domain reseller agreement or domain API terms, so none is published -> No domain reseller agreement is listed in nc-main-sitemap.xml, but that sitemap demonstrably omits at least one footer-linked legal page and the /legal/ index is unreadable, so absence is not proven. (The listing is accurate: nc-main-sitemap.xml (165 URLs) has universal-tos, data-processing-addendum, privacy-policy, copyright-trademark-policies, court-order-and-subpoena-policy, refund-policy, details-for-specific-products-services, registration-agreement, supplemental-registry-agreement, udrp, registrant-rights, fast-transfer-agreement, rdap-tos, premiumdns-tos, whois-privacy-service-agreement, phishing-reports-api-tou, affiliate-agreement and an SSL reseller-service-agreement. But the inference is weaker than presented. (1) The site footer links /legal/general/domain-registration-data-disclosure-policy-guide/, which is NOT in the sitemap, so the sitemap is not a complete legal inventory. (2) The /legal/ index itself is 403. (3) /support/api/intro/ and global-parameters (403) may carry API usage terms. (4) The sitemap lastmod values are all crawl-time stamps (2026-09-29T19:4x), not document revision dates. (5) The unread details-for-specific-products-services page could hold domain-specific terms. Treat as "no reseller agreement found", not "none exists".)
- **corrected**: ICANN RAA 1.24 (Reseller) and 3.12/3.12.2/3.12.5/3.12.7 make Mosshatch a Reseller with flow-down duties -> Current RAA (21 Jan 2024): Reseller = 1.26; 3.12.x unchanged except 3.12.2 references lookup.icann.org. (The substance is confirmed, but the analyst read the superseded 17-Sep-2013 text. The current RAA (approved 21 Jan 2024; global amendment effective 5 Apr 2024) numbers the Reseller definition 1.26 (not 1.24), and 3.12.2 now says "such as a link to the ICANN Registration data lookup tool (https://lookup.icann.org)" instead of InterNIC Whois. The 3.12 lead-in reads: "Registrar is responsible for the provision of Registrar Services... regardless of whether... provided by Registrar or a third party, including a Reseller. Registrar must enter into written agreements with all of its Resellers that enable Registrar to comply with... all of its obligations". The 1.26 definition catches Mosshatch under (a) an agreement or (b) Namecheap's actual knowledge; the KB endorsing API resale arguably supplies that knowledge. Clauses 3.12.2, 3.12.3, 3.12.5 and 3.12.7 (publish Registrants' Benefits and Responsibilities) are unchanged.)
- **corrected**: Registrant of record = whoever Mosshatch puts in the Registrant contact block (RAA 3.7.7.3) (Right for RDDS/WHOIS, incomplete for control and contract. RAA 3.7.7.3 (both versions): a registrant that licenses use of a name "is nonetheless the Registered Name Holder of record" and "shall accept liability for harm caused by wrongful use of the Registered Name, unless it discloses the current contact information provided by the licensee and the identity of the licensee within seven (7) days". So Mosshatch as registrant would carry that liability; the end customer as registrant is cleaner. But Namecheap's KB treats the account holder, not the named registrant, as controlling party: KB 10326 says "the domain name will remain in the original Account... Only the Account owner can manage the domain", and KB 1187 says the new domain holder "becomes responsible for all the domain payments". Practical effect: the customer-registrant has no standing or login at Namecheap, and RAA Transfer Policy 5.2 to 5.4 duties (AuthInfo within 5 calendar days, no more-restrictive mechanism) must be met through Mosshatch's own UI. Who "you" is in the Registration Agreement is unread, so the contractual counterparty is unverifiable.)
- **corrected**: No application, contract, KYC or volume commitment is documented for API/resale use -> No onboarding KYC/contract is documented for API activation, but Namecheap can demand government/business ID under its Universal ToS and lock the account during payment verification. (True of API activation as documented in the FAQ. Overstated overall, because Namecheap documents ad hoc identity and payment verification that can bite a reseller funding a large prepaid balance. KB 10465 ("Why we can request the documents"): "Under our Universal Terms of Service Agreement, you agree to provide government-issued photo identification and/or government-issued business identification, plus whatever else we deem required and necessary, to verify your identity" (second-hand quote of the ToS). Documents include organization formation documents; collection is via Veriff. KB 10463: all incoming payments are fraud-monitored, Risk Management may phone or email, and "during verification, your account may be temporarily locked". KB 10520 describes the Veriff flow. So expect a reseller-scale top-up to trigger KYC-like checks and a possible temporary lock.)
- **corrected**: The 50+ domain special pricing is unpublished / no published schedule -> A tier-pricing page exists at /domains/tier-pricing/ (content unread); the schedule may be published. (The FAQ only says "we can set a special pricing for your account", which suggests a negotiation. But nc-main-sitemap.xml lists a public page https://www.namecheap.com/domains/tier-pricing/ (403 to me, contents unread), so a published tier-pricing page evidently exists. The analyst's dossier acknowledged the page was blocked but the summary still says "unpublished". Verdict: whether the tiers are published and what they contain is unverifiable; "unpublished" is not established.)
- **corrected**: .studio prices: register $14.98 "regular", renew $52.98, transfer $39.98, renewal 3.5x register -> .studio (pre-2026-10-06 list): register $39.98, renew $52.98, transfer $39.98; from 2026-10-06: $51.48 / $68.48 / $51.48. (Namecheap's own KB 10835 "Price update on 226 Identity Digital extensions" (Updated 9/23/2026) lists .studio: Registration old $39.98 -> new $51.48; Renewal $52.98 -> $68.48; Transfer $39.98 -> $51.48, effective 2026-10-06. So $39.98 is Namecheap's standard registration price and the third-party $14.98 "regular" is evidently a promotion (the KB notes promo pricing is honoured only for the promotion's duration). The renewal and transfer numbers match the pre-change list but rise about 29% in 7 days. The list-price renewal:register ratio is about 1.33x (both before and after), not 3.5x; the 3.5x is a promo artifact. Only .studio of the six TLDs is in the notice (.com/.ai/.io/.dev/.app do not appear). All other analyst prices remain third-party scrapes and unverified against Namecheap primary sources.)
- **corrected**: ICANN Transfer Policy 5.2 (AuthInfo within five calendar days) and 60-day rules, citing the 01-06-2016 policy -> Current: Transfer Policy updated 21 Feb 2024 (mandatory by 21 Aug 2025); 5.2 five-calendar-day AuthInfo rule retained; no TAC-based policy published. (The 5-day rule holds, but the cited URL is a superseded version. ICANN's transfers page lists "Transfer Policy (Effective 1 December 2016 through 20 August 2025)" and the current "Transfer Policy (Updated 21 February 2024... must implement no later than 21 August 2025)" at https://www.icann.org/resources/pages/transfer-policy-2024-02-21-en. In the current text, 5.2 is unchanged: registrars must provide the AuthInfo code and remove ClientTransferProhibited "within five (5) calendar days" if there is no self-service. 5.3 (no more-restrictive mechanism than for changing contact/NS data) and 5.4 (cannot refuse AuthInfo solely over a payment dispute) are also there. Neither the current policy nor the transfers page mentions a TAC / Transfer Authorization Code, which partly answers the analyst's open question 15: no TAC-based policy is published as of 2026-09-29.)
- **unverifiable**: Registration and operational terms Namecheap requires of resellers (written reseller agreement, registrant terms) are contractually assigned to Mosshatch (RAA 3.12 requires Namecheap to have written agreements with all Resellers and to enforce them (3.12.6), yet Namecheap says it has no reseller program. The gap is either covered by the ToS/Registration Agreement accepted at account creation (unread), or Namecheap is relying on the account holder being treated as the customer/registrant. No reachable source resolves this. This is the single most important item to get from Namecheap in writing, together with confirmation that commercial resale to third-party registrants under one API account is accepted.)

Missed by the analyst:
- Wayback snapshots of the two key contracts exist per the Wayback availability API (http://archive.org/wayback/available): ToS 20260912160849 and Registration Agreement 20260912162304 at web.archive.org/web/<ts>/https://www.namecheap.com/legal/... . I could not fetch them (web.archive.org tunnel closes), but a human in a normal browser could read them today. Reading them is the fastest way to close the main unverifiable item.
- Account-level lock and suspension exposure (KB 10474, 10463, 10465, 10520, 10626): chargebacks, fraud-monitoring holds, screening-tool flags and ToS violations can lock the whole account and suspend services, sometimes without warning, and "a refund or transfer isn't possible in accordance with our TOS" if a domain cannot be unsuspended. Account closure is impossible while it holds active domains (KB 303: domains must be pushed or removed via Support first), so exit is lock-in.
- Namecheap treats the Account owner as sole controller (KB 10326: "Only the Account owner can manage the domain"), so end-customer registrants have no Namecheap-side standing. RAA Transfer Policy 5.2 to 5.4 duties (AuthInfo within 5 calendar days) therefore fall on Mosshatch's own tooling in practice.
- RAA obligations the analyst did not list, all in the current 21 Jan 2024 RAA: 3.12.1 (resellers must not display the ICANN or ICANN-Accredited Registrar logo or claim accreditation); 3.12.6 (Namecheap must enforce against a reseller that causes breach, which is the contractual basis for suspending Mosshatch); 3.18.1 to 3.18.4 (abuse contact, and prompt mitigation on "actionable evidence" of DNS Abuse, so Namecheap can act on a domain without asking Mosshatch); 3.4.2 (registration records including registration contracts and communications with registrants kept for the Term plus two years, "itself or by its agent(s)"); 3.7.5.5 and 3.7.5.6 (a website offering registration or renewal must clearly display deletion and auto-renewal policies and the RGP recovery fee, which Mosshatch's storefront will need, and the redemption fee table is 403); Registrar Information Specification items 26 and 27 (Namecheap must list known Resellers to ICANN on request, so expect Namecheap to ask Mosshatch to identify itself).
- Data-handling documents exist but are unread: /legal/universal/data-processing-addendum/, /legal/general/privacy-policy/, /legal/domain-privacy/whois-privacy-service-agreement.aspx (Withheld for Privacy LLC is a separate third-party privacy operator), and the footer-linked /legal/general/domain-registration-data-disclosure-policy-guide/. Controller/processor roles for end-customer registrant data that Mosshatch submits are unresolved.
- Namecheap runs a formal SSL Reseller Program with tiered levels, its own agreement (/legal/ssl-certificates/reseller-service-agreement/), and a statement that white-label reselling is not available for Comodo certificates (KB 9532 and 9533). No domain equivalent exists, and the SSL program's clauses cannot be assumed for domains. It matters if Mosshatch later sells certificates.
- Prepaid-funding limits: card/PayPal top-ups are $5 to $100,000 per transaction, with limit-increase requests to Support (KB 527, Updated 12/10/2021). The nc-tlds and support sitemaps were also scanned for reseller/partner pages and found only the SSL reseller pages.
- The Namecheap MCP KB (10824) says its domain_register sets auto-renew "right after the domain is registered and charged" as a separate step, so an auto-renew setter exists somewhere in Namecheap's stack. The analyst's "no auto-renew setter found" refers only to the public API docs, and whether it is exposed to API users is unverified.
- KB 9739 states "you can register all the TLDs listed on our pricing page" via API, which supports registering the six TLDs via API but does not cover transfer-in (the documented transfer.create TLD list, confirmed in the SDK transcription, still omits .ai/.io/.dev/.app/.studio).
- Sitemap lastmod values (all 2026-09-29T19:4x) are crawl timestamps, not document revision dates, so they cannot be used to date the legal documents.

### Verifier lens: tech (confidence medium; 15 claims confirmed)

I could not refute the analyst's core technical picture. I found two errors of substance and one conflict I could not settle. The official API docs site (namecheap.com/support/api/*) and all Namecheap pricing pages are still behind a Cloudflare 403, so I did not read them and I did not work around the block. Coverage claims were re-derived from four sources: Namecheap's own GitHub org SDK (a fresh clone at commit 6f5a73e, including its transcription of the method pages), Namecheap knowledgebase articles, Namecheap's MCP Tools Reference (KB 10824), and live probes of the production and sandbox endpoints. WHMCS's Namecheap module matrix (2026-08-04) gave independent corroboration. Confirmed: no getAuthCode, no startTransferOut, poll-only transfers, replace-all DNS, no idempotency key on register, no registry EPP statuses in getInfo, and the sandbox with its credential steps. The 50/min versus 20/min rate-limit conflict is real, and Namecheap's own KB and SDK are the two sources that disagree. The two errors: register is not always synchronous, because getTldList exposes a NonRealTimeDomain flag and Namecheap's own MCP reference defines a 'pending' status. The .ai '1-year' price figure is not a purchasable unit, because .ai has a 2-year minimum on both registration and renewal. All six prices reproduce exactly on domainoffer.net and tldes.com, the cited sources. hostingcompass.com, a third aggregator I added, independently matches the regular register and renew figures except .ai registration. No Namecheap-owned price page was reachable, so absolute prices are unverifiable. KB 9739 does confirm that API customers pay retail, with a special tier only at 50+ domains. The change-log page is unreadable. Namecheap's status feed and the SDK changelog show nothing touching the six TLDs. The transfer-in TLD gap, duplicate-create error code, sandbox transfer/RGP simulation and restore fees remain unverifiable.

Whether getPricing 'Price' includes the $0.20 is undocumented, so budget list + $0.20 on gTLDs. Wording is only partly corrected.)

Only exit1.dev's '$88.88+' figure is available. Restore is via Support only after funding the balance (KB 242); fee 'set by our upstream Registrar and Domain Registry' (KB 1420).)

Absence of evidence only.)

Missed by the analyst:
- NonRealTimeDomain flag (getTldList, create response) and the MCP 'pending' status: register is not guaranteed instant for every TLD.
- Namecheap's own MCP gateway toggles auto-renew as a post-registration step and exposes lifecycleStatus (creating/registered/grace1/redemption). getInfo Modificationrights lists rights types autorenew and dnsSec. Namecheap therefore has capabilities beyond the documented XML API; ask whether a newer API exists.
- The docs are incomplete: whoisguard.allot/unallot/discard exist but are undocumented (SDK CHANGELOG). Closing getAuthCode/startTransferOut on sitemap absence alone is weak; get written confirmation from Namecheap.
- TTL minimum conflict: 60 (SDK validation code) vs 300 (docs transcription).
- DNSSEC exists but is UI-only (Advanced DNS toggle for BasicDNS/PremiumDNS; manual DS for custom DNS). The unsupported-TLD list does not include the six TLDs. No API method.
- UI bulk auth-code retrieval for transfer-out (Domain list > Actions > Transfer Out) makes a workable operator runbook, but expired domains cannot be unlocked without Support. UI bulk transfer-in is capped at 50 per order.
- .ai cost shape: no 1-year purchase exists; every register and renew action is at least 2 years, and the .ai regular registration price moved from 79.98 (Aug 6) to 89.98 (now).
- Rate-limit scope differs between sources ('whole key' vs 'account level'), and the HTTP 405 throttle signal is SDK-only knowledge, not documented.
- Vercel Static IPs are a shared pool and the Vercel docs never state IPv4, while Namecheap accepts IPv4 only.
- Whether users.getPricing 'Price' includes the $0.20 ICANN fee is undocumented; the MCP gateway does include it in amount. Budget list + $0.20 on gTLDs.
- API error codes reference 'Enom' upstream (3031510, 3016166), and KB 1420 says redemption fees are set by the 'upstream Registrar and Domain Registry'. Ask Namecheap in writing about upstream dependence and who can change terms.
- Legal sitemap includes /legal/general/details-for-specific-products-services/ and /legal/domains/icann-fee/, both unread and possibly holding product-specific or API terms.

## reg-namecom
Dossier: `reg-namecom.md`

### Verifier lens: legal (confidence high; 17 claims confirmed)

The core legal conclusion holds. I re-fetched the Reseller Agreement (RSA), API Access Agreement, Reseller Pricing terms, Registration Agreement, Refund, Subpoena, Acceptable Use and Whois Privacy agreements from name.com's own JS bundles, plus the docs.name.com guides, the OpenAPI file, the ICANN RAA and Registration Data Policy, the IANA registrar list and Verisign RDAP. The RSA does grant a worldwide, non-exclusive right to resell under the reseller's own prices, brand and checkout. It has no exclusivity, minimum volume, non-compete or white-label ban. The API Access Agreement defers resale use of the API to the RSA. The analyst's quotes are accurate. Three corrections: (1) the TL;DR says the contract is "clean", but the RSA is permissive on the right to resell and one-sided everywhere else; (2) the 12-month liability cap and the consequential-damages waiver protect Name.com only, while the reseller's indemnity is uncapped; (3) the amendment clause is "30 days after posting or earlier on acceptance, including by continued performance", not a flat 30 days. Several material clauses were missed. The RSA includes a Data Processing Addendum (24-hour breach notice, Annex 3 security controls, delete-or-return on termination that conflicts with the 7-year retention duty). There is no price-change notice or price lock in the RSA. Name.com may end any end-customer registration on 30 days' notice for any reason, and may discontinue one within 30 days of purchase or renewal. The sponsoring registrar may be any of six accredited Name.com entities. RAA 3.7.7.3 and Registration Agreement section 13 are the legal basis for the reseller-as-registrant risk. .ai is absent from Name.com's Additional Registry Requirements page. The program-requirement claims (no deposit or volume minimum, $2,500 default purchase limit, Bronze/Silver/Gold thresholds, self-serve versus "select number" conflict) are confirmed, with nuance on how account credit counts toward the spend tier. Discount percentages, which contract track a self-serve token click-through binds, and whether promo prices apply to API orders remain unverifiable without an account. Fallback is a defensible recommendation from the legal lens, and a negotiated agreement is possible because the RSA yields to any other executed resale agreement.

Not confirmed:
- **corrected**: TL;DR: 'the contract is clean' -> Resale permitted (conditional); contract is permissive on the resale right but materially one-sided (Overstated. Resale is permitted, but the RSA is one-sided: no price-change notice or price lock; Name.com's liability capped at 12 months of fees while the reseller's indemnity is uncapped with no fault carve-out; in the current RSA the consequential-damages waiver protects Name.com only (the legacy RSA on the same page says 'NEITHER PARTY'); immediate no-cure termination if Name.com decides a customer breached any term; a Data Processing Addendum with 24-hour breach notice. Better wording: 'permits resale, reseller-hostile terms'.)
- **corrected**: Additional Terms may be the reseller's own but must say the registrar agreement controls and must 'identify Name.com, Inc. as the sponsoring registrar' -> Identify the sponsoring registrar or provide a lookup means; the sponsoring registrar may be any of six accredited Name.com entities (Text is 'must also identify Name.com, Inc. as the sponsoring registrar OR provide a means to Your Customers to identify the sponsoring registrar', plus identify it on inquiry (mirrors RAA 3.12.2/3.12.3). This matters because the Registration Agreement footer lists six registrars (Name.com Inc 625, Name.net 826, DomainSite 466, Name105 452, Name106 1040, Name117 1547), 'any one of which will be the registrar for your domain name'. IANA registrar-ids.xml (updated 2026-09-23) shows all six as Accredited. IANA 625 is not guaranteed for every name.)
- **corrected**: Reseller Pricing Program thresholds: Bronze $1,000 and 100 registrations, Silver $2,500 and 250, Gold $10,000 and 1,000, over the trailing 365 days; percentages unpublished; Name.com may modify or cancel without notice and is sole arbiter of eligibility -> Qualifying Total = trailing-365-day spend plus current account credit; threshold changes get 30 days' notice; other program terms can change without notice (Thresholds and 'sole arbiter' are exact (https://www.name.com/_nuxt/BlG9FYeC.js, Last Updated August 2024). Corrections: 'Qualifying Total' is trailing-365-day spend PLUS current Account credit at enrolment, so prefunding counts toward the spend leg (the registrations leg still needs 100 new registrations, renewals and transfers excluded); eligibility is per Account, no aggregation; discounts last one year with an annual review and automatic tier drop; program-wide changes can be 'without notice' but threshold changes need 'at least 30 days' notice'; a participant gets 'the lower of promotional pricing or Program Discounts' (no stacking). Discount percentages are not published on any page I could reach, so they stay unverified.)
- **unverifiable**: No reseller KYC form documented (Absence claim. I found none in the RSA, Access Form, docs or /nameapi pages, but I cannot see the signup flow. Related terms confirmed: RSA sanctions clause allows termination and freezing the account and all funds without notice if Name.com 'cannot confirm' a person is not a Sanctioned Person; Registration Agreement lets Name.com request passport or ID documentation at its sole discretion; the reseller warrants it is a duly organized entity in good standing.)
- **corrected**: Amendments effective 30 days after posting -> Material amendments take effect at the earlier of 30 days after posting or acceptance (including continued performance); non-material on posting; ICANN-driven changes possibly sooner (Non-material changes are effective on posting. Material changes are effective at the earlier of 30 days after posting or 'upon Your acceptance ... including by continuing Your performance under this RSA after you have been notified'. ICANN or registry-driven changes 'may become effective fewer than thirty (30) days' after notice. The reseller's 'sole remedy is to terminate'. The RSA page carries no version or effective date (only the DPA is dated 5 December 2022), so changes cannot be dated from the page.)
- **corrected**: Liability capped at 12 months of fees; JAMS arbitration in King County, Washington -> 12-month cap protects Name.com only; reseller exposure is uncapped (Arbitration venue, JAMS and Washington law confirmed. The 12-month cap (and a per-transaction fee cap) limits Name.com's liability only. The reseller's indemnity (breach; any product or service of Yours; use of the Services; domain business) has no cap and no negligence carve-out. In the current RSA the consequential-damages waiver protects only Name.com ('NAME.COM SHALL NOT BE LIABLE'); the legacy RSA on the same page says 'NEITHER PARTY'. The API Access Form separately caps Name.com at fees received during the Access Form term and uses King County courts rather than arbitration.)
- **unverifiable**: Unverified: whether public promo prices are charged on API orders (TLD Price List says promo codes unsupported; Create Domain has promoCode) (Still unverifiable without an account. The OpenAPI narrows it: 'Promo codes are not supported through the API' (TLD Price List) while account-level price 'includes applicable rebates, promotions and/or account-level discounts'. The Reseller Pricing terms say program members get 'the lower of promotional pricing or Program Discounts'. So automatic promotions may reach API accounts, but the .com 12.99 sale cannot be confirmed without a live quote.)
- **unverifiable**: Reseller Pricing Program discount percentages and any negotiated wholesale rate (No page I could reach (docs, policies, /nameapi, /pricing, resellersolutions) publishes percentages. RSA and /nameapi say custom rate cards exist ('unless otherwise communicated to You in writing'). Needs reseller@name.com. Also note: RSA Confidentiality (term plus 3 years) covers 'costs, prices', so a negotiated rate card may be non-publishable.)

Missed by the analyst:
- RSA includes a Data Processing Addendum (last updated 2022-12-05; reseller is processor, Name.com controller; the DPA prevails on conflict): breach notice to Name.com within 24 hours (legal@name.com cc privacy@name.com), Annex 3 minimum security controls (SOC/ISO/NIST-equivalent, MFA, annual external pen tests, FIPS 140-2 aligned crypto, background checks), Registrar audit rights, and on termination 'irrevocably delete ... or return, in full, all Shared Personal Data to the Registrar'. That delete-or-return duty collides with the RSA audit duty to keep registration and payment records for the term plus 7 years. The DPA still cites Privacy Shield. The SCC PDF exists but was not read.
- Reseller liability is uncapped and one-sided in the current RSA: indemnity for anything 'relating to any product or service of Yours' or 'Your use of the Services' with no negligence carve-out, while the consequential-damages waiver protects only Name.com (the legacy RSA on the same page was mutual). The API Access Form adds its own broad indemnity, King County courts and fee-shifting for Name.com, which conflict with the RSA's JAMS arbitration.
- No price-change notice or price lock anywhere in the RSA; renewals float with Name.com's retail list (scheduled increases are visible via future_renewal_price in the public pricing feed). Named price-change notice only exists for registrants in the Registration Agreement ('reasonable efforts' 30 days) and for program threshold changes.
- Name.com can end an individual end-customer registration on 30 days' notice for any reason (pro-rata refund) and may reject or discontinue service for any reason within 30 days of purchase or renewal (Registration Agreement). The Acceptable Use Policy also allows suspension, cancellation or transfer of domains 'without prior notice'. This sits on top of the RSA termination rights.
- Sponsoring registrar is one of six accredited Name.com entities (IANA 625, 826, 466, 452, 1040, 1547 per the Registration Agreement footer; all Accredited in IANA's list updated 2026-09-23), so Mosshatch's terms should use the 'means to identify the sponsoring registrar' option.
- Legal basis of the reseller-as-registrant risk: Registration Agreement section 13 and RAA 3.7.7.3 (registrant of record is liable for harm from a licensee's use unless it discloses the licensee within 7 days); the Registration Agreement also says a person registering on behalf of someone else 'will bind that person as a principal'. The ICANN Specification on Privacy and Proxy Registrations (still in force until an accreditation program exists) defines a 'Proxy Service' as a registrant licensing use to a customer while showing the registrant's contact in RDDS; counsel should assess whether a Mosshatch-as-registrant model resembles it.
- .ai has no entry on Name.com's Additional Registry Requirements page, the document the RSA points to for registry-required flow-down terms, and .ai is not labelled a Registration Partner TLD; Mosshatch must source the Anguilla/.ai registry terms elsewhere. .com is listed as 'N/A'.
- Additional RSA obligations for a launch checklist: publish redemption/restore fees (yet restore is UI-only and 'not guaranteed'); link ICANN Registrants' Benefits and Responsibilities and the ICANN educational page on the registration site; no ICANN logo; Name.com trademarks only as pre-approved in writing; 'shall not branch or otherwise prepare derivatives of the API' (ambiguous for generated clients); RSA assignment needs Name.com's consent (matters on an acquisition); confidentiality covers 'costs, prices'; fees exclude taxes and API TotalPaid includes VAT when applicable; Name.com may 'directly engage with' the reseller's customers if the RSA ends or the reseller is unresponsive.
- Two RSA versions are on the same page: the current text and a legacy 'Reseller Agreement for resellers onboarded prior to 5 December 2022'. The current text has no version or effective date, and the Access Form's cross-references use the legacy section numbers.
- Name.com's Registration Agreement claims ownership of the domain database and registrant/contact data (footnote 1 says this denotes controller responsibility), and is written for direct account holders ('You must create an account'), with a US$400 end-customer liability cap and Name.com as 'Designated Agent' for Change of Registrant. Reseller Additional Terms lose any conflict with it, which limits Mosshatch's ability to promise more generous refund or cancellation terms.
- Program gating details: ACH funding needs to be enabled through reseller@name.com; the $2,500 default purchase limit scope is undefined; program Qualifying Total counts current account credit as well as trailing spend; eligibility is per account with no aggregation; sub-account creation, contact verification, premium lists and internal transfer-in are gated to 'approved' resellers.

### Verifier lens: tech (confidence high; 20 claims confirmed)

I re-fetched the OpenAPI (v1.34.0, parsed with PyYAML), the docs guides, the changelog, the pricing feed and CMS pages, the contract JS chunks, the status API and npm, and found no refutation of any load-bearing coverage claim. Five items need correcting or tightening. (1) DNS "duplicates return 409" is documented only for Update (changelog 1.6.0); the OpenAPI lists no 409 on Create or Update Record. (2) Transfer-in "refundable until completed" is Refund Policy wording; the API cancel endpoint only works in pre-registry statuses, and cannot cancel in pending_transfer or pending_insert. (3) The Reseller Pricing Program qualifying total includes account credit at enrolment, threshold changes carry 30 days notice, and members get "the lower of promotional pricing or Program Discounts". (4) The multi-year price feed is internally consistent (promo year 1 plus list year 2), contrary to the dossier. (5) The promo-price question is narrower than "unverified": the TLD Price List doc says account-level and retail prices include public promotions, and only promo codes are excluded. All six launch-TLD prices (register, renew, transfer, restore) match the live feed fetched 2026-09-29 20:16 UTC. Sandbox existence is confirmed by the docs and by both api.dev.name.com and api.name.com answering 401 with api-version Reseller-1.34.0. Rate limits (20 req/s, 3,000 req/hour account-wide) are confirmed verbatim. Nothing behind credentials could be checked (sandbox TLD list, .ai allowedRegistrationYears, expirationGracePeriod, real account-level tldpricing). The analyst missed several items: no documented error for registering an already-taken domain, ExpirationClientHold, the X-RateLimit-Reset header, a 2FA contradiction in the docs, and the 2026-10-06 Verisign cTLD maintenance.

Not confirmed:
- **corrected**: startTransferIn: POST /core/v1/transfers {domainName, authCode, privacyEnabled?, purchasePrice?}; 402 if credit short; 409 if registered under 60 days or a transfer is already processing; premium needs purchasePrice=transferPrice; eligibility endpoint; cancel refunds to account credit; fee charged at submission -> API cancel (POST /core/v1/transfers/{domain}:cancel) is possible only before the transfer is pending at the registry; from pending_transfer onward there is no API cancel/refund path, whatever the Refund Policy wording says. (Endpoint, body, 402, premium rule, eligibility endpoint and cancel-refund text are confirmed in the OpenAPI/llms.txt. The 60-day 409 is from changelog 1.9.3 (2025-10-27), not the spec (its 409 text is generic: 'The status of the domain indicates that we are unable to process a transfer'). 'Charged at submission' is inferred from 402-at-create plus cancel-refund text, not stated. Material correction: the dossier lifecycle line 'refundable as long as the transfer has not completed' is the Refund Policy sentence (confirmed in DG3_37Xa.js), but the API cancel endpoint only works in pending, submitting_transfer, pending_new_auth_code, pending_unlock, pending_registry_unlock, rejected; it cannot cancel in pending_transfer or pending_insert.)
- **corrected**: DNS record CRUD: A/AAAA/ANAME/CNAME/MX/NS/SRV/TXT only (no CAA), min TTL 300, perPage default 500, per-record CRUD by numeric id, PUT is a full overwrite, no upsert/replace-all, duplicates return 409 -> 409 on duplicate is documented only in changelog 1.6.0 for Update Record; Create Record duplicate behaviour is undocumented. (Types, TTL 300, perPage 500, full-overwrite PUT, delete-by-id (204) and absence of upsert/idempotency header all confirmed from the Record schema and operations. The '409 on duplicates' claim is only supported for Update (changelog 1.6.0: 'DNS Record Update: Duplicate record DB errors now return 409'). The OpenAPI response lists for POST .../records and PUT .../records/{id} contain no 409; duplicate-on-create behaviour is undocumented.)
- **corrected**: Price basis: a new API account pays the public retail price (RSA: standard retail price); no published wholesale or discounted tier; whether public promos are charged on API orders is unverified -> Docs imply an undiscounted API account is quoted the promo-inclusive retail price (e.g. .com 12.99 in year 1), but the RSA says only 'standard retail price' and the discovery field is described as 'list'; actual charge remains unverified. (RSA payment terms re-read in DG18NICY.js: 'Unless otherwise communicated to You in writing by Name.com, You will be charged Name.com's standard retail price' (ambiguous: could mean list 17.99 not promo 12.99). No wholesale/cost-plus rate is published on /pricing, /nameapi or in docs; only 'Custom rate cards available for high-volume partnerships' and 'For bulk prices contact accountservices@name.com'. Refinement: the TLD Price List doc defines account-level price as including 'rebates, promotions, or account-level discounts', retail as including 'public rebates or promotions', says account price equals retail absent account-level pricing, and excludes only promo codes. So the docs imply the promo-inclusive price is what the API quotes; actual charge is unverifiable without credentials. SearchResult.purchasePrice is described as the 'minimum-term list ... price', a contrary hint.)
- **corrected**: Reseller Pricing Program: Bronze $1,000 and 100, Silver $2,500 and 250, Gold $10,000 and 1,000 on trailing-365-day spend plus new registrations; percentages unpublished; Name.com may modify or cancel without notice -> Qualifying Total = last-365-day spend + account credit at enrolment; 30 days notice for threshold changes; lower-of promo vs Program Discount applies. (Thresholds confirmed in BlG9FYeC.js (Last Updated August 2024). Corrections: (a) the enrolment 'Qualifying Total' is trailing-365-day spend on Qualifying Products PLUS the Account credit balance at enrolment, so prefunding counts toward the spend test (the registration count must still be real new registrations); (b) changes to the minimum spend/registration requirements need 'at least 30 days' notice', only 'other Program Terms' change 'without notice'; (c) members get 'the lower of promotional pricing or Program Discounts'; (d) eligibility is per Account with no aggregation; (e) 'Qualifying Products' are not enumerated. Discount percentages are not published on the terms page, /nameapi or /resellersolutions (redirects to /nameapi).)
- **corrected**: Multi-year feed values for non-.ai TLDs looked internally inconsistent so only 1-year figures are used -> 2-year totals are promo year 1 plus list year 2; only .studio's 2-year 'original' field is anomalous. (The duration=2 feed is consistent: promo applies to year 1 only, so 2-year registration = promo + list: .com 12.99+17.99=30.98, .io 53.99+79.99=133.98, .dev 14.99+19.99=34.98, .app 14.99+22.99=37.98, .studio 21.99+41.99=63.98. The only odd values are .studio registration_original_price 41.99 at 2 years (should be 83.98) and the 2-year .com renewal (39.98 = 2 x 19.99) ignoring the scheduled 21.99 increase. Using 1-year figures is still safe.)

Missed by the analyst:
- No documented status code or error body for POST /core/v1/domains when the name is already registered or no longer purchasable: the only documented 409 on create is idempotency-key reuse (generic GenericConflict409 says 'Object already exists'). Duplicate/race handling on register must be discovered empirically in sandbox/production.
- ExpirationClientHold (changelog 1.12.0 and domain-locks guide): for some reseller accounts a ClientHold is applied at expiry (day 0), removing the domain from DNS so the customer's site and email stop working immediately, well before the day-25 API renewal window ends. The dossier does not mention it.
- The 429 header documented across the OpenAPI is x-ratelimit-reset (Unix epoch); Retry-After is only in prose for the per-domain registration limit. Clients should handle both.
- Docs contradict each other on 2FA: the API Overview says 2FA-enabled accounts are not supported (disable 2FA); Getting Started, Authentication and the FAQ say keep 2FA and enable the API Access toggle.
- Reseller Pricing Program enrolment counts Account credit toward the spend test; threshold changes need 30 days notice; and a member receives the lower of promotional pricing or Program Discounts (BlG9FYeC.js).
- The API cancel endpoint for transfer-in cannot cancel once the transfer is pending_transfer or pending_insert, so the Refund Policy 'refundable until completed' does not carry through to the API.
- OpenAPI locks[] vocabulary is inconsistent (EPP strings in the schema example vs Name.com lock names in changelog 1.20.0 and the lock webhook lockType), so registry-status mapping for the adapter needs a live-response check.
- Other deprecated operations beyond :lock/:unlock: enable/disable autorenew and enable/disable WHOIS privacy endpoints, plus host-based URL forwarding operations, all marked to be removed in a future release without a date.
- Additional maintenance windows not listed: 2026-10-06 21:00-01:00 UTC Verisign cTLD (cc, name, IDN .com/.net variants; not launch TLDs) and 2026-09-30 08:30-13:30 UTC .nrw/.bayern.
- Spec inconsistency: tldpricing 'duration' parameter description says default 1 but the schema default is 5; explicit duration should always be sent.
- Cancel External Transfer Out returns 422 for TLDs that cannot EPP-reject an outbound transfer (it then expires on its own) and 502 when the registry rejects the cancel.
- Sub-account creation (POST /core/v1/accounts) exists but is approved-reseller-only; whether sub-accounts get their own 20 req/s and 3,000/hour budget is undocumented, which matters for the shared-account rate-limit risk.
- The dossier's 'ns1.name.com' example of the default nameserver is not sourced in the docs I read (docs say only 'the account's default nameservers').

## reg-namesilo
Dossier: `reg-namesilo.md`

### Verifier lens: legal (confidence medium; 21 claims confirmed)

The analyst's headline holds: resale is conditional, and the in-account reseller terms are unreadable to me too. `/account_reseller.php` redirects to a login page, and I found no public copy. Public text openly invites Mosshatch's model. The reseller landing page says "Whitelabel all NameSilo services and brand them as your own". The FAQ says you can "create an entirely custom reselling web site using our API". NameSilo ships official WHMCS, HostBill, Blesta, BoxBilling and Clientexec modules. The Registration Agreement has zero mentions of "reseller", "resell", "API" or "automated". I found no resale prohibition, no "own use only" clause and no approval gate. Legal permission risk is therefore lower than "unread contract" implies. Contract and money exposure is higher than the dossier shows. The analyst missed these:

(1) The rest of AGENTS AND LICENSES. A licensor "remain[s] the domain name holder of record" and accepts liability for harm unless it discloses the licensee within 7 days. This matches RAA 3.7.7.3. It bites if the 302 default-contact fallback makes Mosshatch the registrant.
(2) The Account Funding Terms and Conditions (`/popups/account_fund_terms.php`), never opened. Balance refunds are allowed only if the last funds purchase was more than 60 days ago. Refunds carry a 3.4-4% fee per transaction. Changes are at sole discretion and NameSilo "may or may not notify". The program can be cancelled "without cause". These terms also document automatic replenishment, so the dossier's "no auto top-up documented" is refuted.
(3) The Discount Program FAQ: withdrawing funds after opting out is refunded "minus the discounts you received".
(4) Data-handling terms. NameSilo claims ownership of registrant contact data. It allows bulk-WHOIS and "targeted marketing" use. It requires notice to, and consent from, any third party whose data Mosshatch submits. The Privacy Policy cites only the EU-U.S. Privacy Shield. There is no DPA, and the "GDPR Policies" link points to gdpr-info.eu.
(5) A performance-bond demand right, and a $100/hour fee plus attorneys' fees for alleged unauthorized-access disputes.
(6) Termination and at-will rejection. NameSilo may reject or discontinue service "for any reason within 30 days of a Service initiation or a Service renewal".

Corrections: RAA 3.12.2 is quoted truncated. The liability cap is the lesser of fees paid and $200. "No KYC/approval" is too strong, because the Privacy Policy reserves proof-of-ID requests. The RAA 3.12 preface requires a written reseller agreement, which makes the unread terms load-bearing. I also re-confirmed the .com Discount Program pricing and the batch-API policy text. Recommendation stays FALLBACK (weak), with the reasons rebalanced toward contract and money exposure.

(Quote is exact and there are zero hits for reseller/resell/API/automated in ~99,900 chars. The analyst omitted the rest of the same clause: a licensor 'remain[s] the domain name holder of record', stays responsible for all obligations, and 'accept[s] liability for harm caused by wrongful use of the Registered Name' unless it discloses the licensee within 7 days (RAA 3.7.7.3 nearly verbatim). 'You' is defined as the account holder plus the WHOIS registrant. Only the registrant and admin contacts may approve or deny transfers.)

WebSearch budget was exhausted, and I did not open the torrentfreak or HN links. The status page is egress-blocked per the analyst, and I did not retry. I neither confirm nor refute these.)

Missed by the analyst:
- Account Funding Terms and Conditions (https://www.namesilo.com/popups/account_fund_terms.php), a governing document the analyst never opened. Balance retrieval only if the last funds purchase was over 60 days ago, full balance only, 3.4-4% fee per refund transaction, NameSilo may withhold up to the full balance, no interest, terms changeable in sole discretion with notice optional, program cancellable without cause. - Discount Program clawback: after opting out, withdrawn funds are refunded 'minus the discounts you received on your domain registrations and renewals'. Bulk-order discounts still apply while enrolled. Drop-catch API orders are excluded, which implies ordinary API orders may be eligible but this is not stated.
- AGENTS AND LICENSES second half: a licensor 'remain[s] the domain name holder of record' and accepts liability for harm from wrongful use unless the licensee is disclosed within 7 days (RAA 3.7.7.3). It applies if Mosshatch is the registrant, including via the 302 default-contact fallback.
- Data-handling gaps: NameSilo claims ownership of registrant/contact data, may allow 'targeted marketing' and bulk WHOIS use, and requires notice to and consent from third parties whose personal data Mosshatch submits. The Privacy Policy cites only the invalidated EU-U.S. Privacy Shield, with no DPA or SCCs. The 'GDPR Policies' link points to gdpr-info.eu. The Privacy Policy claims WHOIS privacy is 'enabled by default' but the API default is off.
- Indemnity and cost clauses: NameSilo may demand 'written assurances ... performance bond(s) or other guarantees' and may treat refusal as a breach costing the right to control the domain. Unauthorized-access disputes cost $100/hour plus attorneys' fees. The account holder is liable for all access, and the API key is a full-account credential.
- At-will rejection right: NameSilo may reject or discontinue service 'for any reason within 30 days of a Service initiation or a Service renewal', beyond the 30-day no-cause termination; also a 24-hour duty to answer payment inquiries.
- Marketing and program evidence understated: the landing page headline 'Whitelabel all NameSilo services and brand them as your own', 'active resellers of ours', and official WHMCS/HostBill/Blesta/BoxBilling/Clientexec modules. NameSilo's MCP page (mcp.namesilo.com) explicitly targets AI agents, with an X-API-KEY header and POST JSON-RPC; the key-in-URL objection applies only to the raw GET API.
- RAA 3.12 preface requires NameSilo to have a written agreement with every reseller, so the click-through reseller terms are legally load-bearing. Also 3.12.1 (no ICANN logo or accreditation claim), 3.12.5/3.12.7 (link ICANN registrant education page; publish the Registrants' Benefits and Responsibilities Specification), and Registrar Information Specification items 26-27 (Mosshatch will be on NameSilo's reseller list available to ICANN).
- Reseller Complaints path is customer-triggered and requires WHOIS-matching ID, so it fails when privacy hides the customer or Mosshatch is the registrant. It also imposes a de facto 72-hour reseller response duty.
- The Terms make the reactivation period and RGP participation discretionary and allow auction of expired names; the published 30/40/64-day timeline is policy rather than contract. The Terms say restoration pricing is on the Expiration Process page, but no amount appears there.
- The public Terms have registry-specific flow-down clauses for Donuts, Rightside, Verisign, PIR, Afilias, Neustar, .mobi, .io/.ac/.sh, .uk and .biz, but none for Google Registry (.dev/.app) or .ai; TLD-Specific Details lists only '.CA Domains'. Whether registry agreements require additional flow-down for .dev/.app/.ai is unverified.
- No public Discount Program or API terms of use document was found; the batch-API article's 'violates our terms of service' points to a document I could not locate, most likely the unread reseller terms.
- web.archive.org fetch failed (connection reset, proxy 'ws_closed_mid_exchange'), so no archived copy of the reseller terms was checked; a throwaway logged-in account, done with the owner's consent, is the only route to the real text.

### Verifier lens: tech (confidence high; 19 claims confirmed)

I found no refutation of a load-bearing technical or pricing claim. I re-fetched the API operation pages, the response-code table, the support articles, the Terms, the Discount Program page, the three pricing tabs (headless Chromium) and Verisign's 10-Q myself. All six TLD price rows (.com, .ai, .io, .dev, .app, .studio; register, renew and transfer) match the pricing page exactly. The coverage rows for transferDomain, checkTransferStatus, retrieveAuthCode, lock/unlock, DNS CRUD and register/renew (no idempotency key, codes 262/400/301/302/210) re-derive from the official reference. Corrections are minor: the sandbox host is inferred from a probe and not documented; listDomains has a grace-period filter; NameSilo's own DNS article says changes take up to 48 hours; NameSilo's pages disagree on outbound-transfer timing. The most important item the analyst missed: Verisign's 10-Q confirms .com wholesale rises from 10.26 to 10.97 on 2026-11-01, which puts the at-cost floor (11.17) above NameSilo's 11.05 Discount Program price, so that number is likely to change within five weeks. I could not verify: the transfer status list (Cloudflare 403), sandbox scope, real duplicate-register behaviour, whether API orders get the Discount Program price end to end, and the in-account reseller terms.
