Missed by the analyst:
- Verisign's own 10-Q (2026-06-30) confirms .com wholesale goes 10.26 to 10.97 on 2026-11-01, so the NameSilo .com Discount Program price of 11.05 falls below the at-cost floor (about 11.17 with the ICANN fee) five weeks from now. Treat 11.05 as a short-lived number and model a reprice.
- Discount Program clawback: FAQ says if you opt out and withdraw remaining funds NameSilo refunds 'the amount minus the discounts you received'. Enrollment must also be explicitly activated, and the tier is set by active domains at the time of each transaction.
- NameSilo's own reseller landing page advertises retail order-size tiers ($17.29 .com for 1-49 domains, renewals $17.29), not the $11.05 DP price; the DP price is only reached via an opt-in, prefunded-balance route.
- registerDomainDrop (drop-catching, .com/.net only, callable only 10:45-12:15 PT, must use /apibatch, not eligible for DP discounts), whoisInfo, countExpiringDomains, registered-nameserver (glue host) ops, the whole auctions/* and marketplace/* groups, and NameSilo-published WHMCS/HostBill/Blesta/BoxBilling/Clientexec modules are not in the coverage table.
- Terms give NameSilo wide latitude on expired domains that the red flags do not quote: it may choose not to participate in RGP, may change the expired domain's WHOIS registrant, uses a 120-day 'ERGP' if it pays the registry fee, and says all fees are non-refundable.
- dnsListRecords returns the FQDN in host while dnsAddRecord/dnsUpdateRecord take a relative rrhost ('@' becomes blank), so list-diff-apply needs host normalisation. NameSilo's own DNS article says changes can take up to 48 hours.
- orderDetails has no structured domain or timestamp per line, only a description string like 'testdomain.com - registration' with status Processed/Credited, and listOrders returns only order number, date, method and total, so the reconciliation protocol must parse description text.
- UI-only 'Reset Auth Code' exists; no API equivalent. Outbound-transfer timing differs across NameSilo pages (15 min, 30-60 min; 5-6 days, 5-7 days).
- Additional doc drift: transferUpdateResendAdminEmail sample names the wrong operation; the MCP marketing page lists methods (getApiUsage, getAccountNotifications, getAbuseReports, dnsUpdateSOA, dnsApplyTemplate) absent from both the REST reference and the MCP schema, so a usage or quota endpoint cannot be excluded.
- The renewDomain restoration override and the reject-invalid-contact toggle are referenced in API pages but are described nowhere in the API Manager article, so where to switch them is only discoverable in-account.
- The sandbox host URL is not published; only the word 'sandbox' and code 116 appear in the docs. It has to be confirmed when credentials are issued.

## reg-opensrs
Dossier: `reg-opensrs.md`

### Verifier lens: legal (confidence high; 15 claims confirmed)

The core legal claim holds. I found nothing that stops an independent company from reselling OpenSRS domains under its own brand and checkout, ordering through the API in an automated way. I downloaded the MSA PDF myself and it is byte-identical (sha256 1e170823...) to the analyst's copy. I read all 40 pages, the standalone MDRA (HTML and PDF), the DPA, the TLD clauses, the payment terms and the API page. I found no 'own use only', anti-white-label, exclusivity, non-compete or non-solicit clause. The MSA preamble says the customer 'wishes to use, resell and/or provision the Tucows Services for itself and the benefit its own Users'. OpenSRS's own API page says 'Your product. Your checkout. Your customer', and the API FAQ says 'You set your own margins' with 'no monthly fees and no minimum purchase requirements'. Signup is documented as 'automated and self-serve'. Tucows Domains Inc. is IANA registrar ID 69, Accredited.

The analyst's headline numbers hold: the $95 activation fee, the prepaid balance, the four tiers, the six launch-TLD prices, the scheduled rises and the restore fees all re-derive from primary sources.

Corrections are mostly one-sided emphasis or thin spots:
- The analyst's own summary groups MSA 17.3 under 'sole discretion', but 17.3 says 'reasonable discretion' and 'to Tucows' satisfaction acting reasonably'.
- The tl;dr says higher tiers are '4-12 dollars cheaper'; the true range is 1-12.
- The maintenance outage on 2026-09-28 was a 6-hour window, extended once, not just 2 hours.
- 'Reseller sets retail price' has one exception, for aftermarket premium names (MSA 10.3).
- The DPA is not simply 'customer controller, Tucows processor'.

The analyst missed the liability terms (21.4-21.6, including a sole-remedy clause), fast-track termination for ICANN-policy violations (3.9), the assignment bar (23.4), the Parked Pages advertising program (Section 8), the premium-data no-caching rule (10.9), the stale 2018 DPA, and ICANN Transfer Policy auth-code rules (5.2-5.4). These are detailed under 'missed'.

Not verifiable from here: whether a hidden review step follows the email-only signup form (I did not submit it), whether 30 days' notice was actually given for the .studio and .com price rises (Wayback fetch failed and the web-search budget is exhausted), and any live API behavior (horizon.opensrs.net:55443 tunnel closes here too).

Per harness rules against writing report files, I did not write reg-opensrs.verify-legal.md, so verify_file is unset and these notes are in this structured output. Raw evidence I fetched is in working-directory/research/opensrs-verify-legal/ (msa.compact.txt, dpa.compact.txt, mdra.txt, pricing_*.txt, kb_*.txt).

Not confirmed:
- **corrected**: MSA 3.10 means the customer (not Mosshatch) must be the registrant. -> 3.10 permits proxy registration only with Tucows Whois Privacy or a conspicuous no-escrow notice plus indemnity. The customer-as-registrant requirement is from MDRA/RAA/RDP, not 3.10. (3.10 does not say that. It bars registering names 'using any proxy contact information or privacy services' unless the customer uses Tucows' WHOIS Privacy 'OR' shows a conspicuous notice that data is not escrowed, plus an indemnity for using its own proxy/privacy service. The end-customer-as-registrant rule really comes from the MDRA ('person named as Registrant on record... shall be the Registered Name Holder'), MDRA's Agency clause (the Registered Name Holder is liable for harm unless it discloses the licensee), the RAA, and the ICANN Registration Data Policy. KB 201000115819 says the Organization field 'will officially determine who is recognized as the legal owner'. Registering in Mosshatch's name would make Mosshatch the Registered Name Holder with the attendant liability. The practical conclusion stands; the clause citation is an over-read.)
- **corrected**: The reseller sets the retail price. -> Reseller sets retail price, except aftermarket premium names (no markup, MSA 10.3; no tying, 10.8). (True for standard names and registry-premium names (Appendix A: 'Customer will in its own discretion determine the fee to charge to its User'; API guide pricing is wholesale). MSA 12.4 and 12.5 cover Storefront only. One exception: MSA 10.3 says for Aftermarket Premium Domain Names the customer 'agrees to charge its Users an amount equal to, or less than, the price set by the current domain name registrant', so no markup. 10.8 bars tying a premium purchase to other purchases. Mosshatch's six launch TLDs are unaffected unless aftermarket premium is enabled.)
- **unverifiable**: No manual approval or KYC step is documented for API resellers. (Consistent with the docs: KB Quickstart says signups are 'automated and self-serve' and the Setup Guide only requires an 'activated reseller account' (confirmation email with username and password). The join form is a HubSpot form whose first step is email only; I inspected it in headless Chromium and did not submit it, so the fields and any review after the email step were not observed. The API overview says 'Speak with our sales team to get started' and 'Talk to our team', so a sales-led gate for API use cannot be excluded. The absence of a documented step is confirmed; the absence of any real-world review is unverifiable.)
- **corrected**: Higher tiers are 4-12 dollars cheaper than Essential (tl;dr item 5). -> 1-12 dollars per domain-year (Advanced 1-4; Enterprise 3-12) (I rendered all four tiers. Advanced saves $1 on .com/.dev/.io/.app, $4 on .ai and $2 on .studio. Enterprise saves $3 (.com, .dev, .io, .app), $6 (.studio) and $12 (.ai). Minimum saving is $1, maximum $12. The dossier's recommendation text says '1-12'; the tl;dr '4-12' is internally inconsistent.)
- **unverifiable**: Price changes require 30 days' notice (Appendix A), and the .studio and .com rises comply. (The 30-day notice clause is confirmed: Appendix A says 'We will provide you with at least thirty (30) days' notice before any price change. It is your sole responsibility to periodically review pricing information'. It also says Tucows may 'change the non-standard fee for... Registry Premium Domain Names without notice'. MSA 12.2 says only 'upon notice'; 12.6 lets Tucows pass through ICANN/registry fee changes. Whether the .studio rise (effective Oct 6, page last updated Sep 29) was posted 30 days ahead cannot be checked: web.archive.org connections were reset by the tunnel, and the web-search budget is exhausted. The .com rise (Nov 1) has 33 days from Sep 29, so it would comply if first posted by then.)
- **corrected**: RISK: Tucows has sole-discretion suspension, 30-day termination and unilateral contract/price change rights (2.10, 19.2, 17.1, 17.3, 23.3, 12.2, 12.6). -> 17.3 is reasonable-discretion, not sole-discretion; 23.3 amendments must be generally applicable and given with reasonable notice. (Confirmed in substance. 2.10: 'Tucows, in its sole discretion, may temporarily suspend access'. 19.2: 'in its unilateral discretion, immediately suspend any or all... if Tucows deems it necessary for the security and stability'. 17.1: either party on 30 days' written notice, and Tucows may end 'any or all of its service offering' with 'all reasonable notice... as is practicable'. Correction: 17.3 is a 'reasonable discretion' clause ('Tucows, in its reasonable discretion... to Tucows' satisfaction acting reasonably'), so it should not be grouped under 'sole discretion' as in the analyst's summary. 23.3 permits amendment without consent only if 'generally applicable to all Customers' and with 'reasonable notice' (the dossier row keeps this qualifier; the summary bullets drop it). 3.16 ('revoke Customer's access to modify User domain names at any time') is confirmed.)
- **corrected**: Data Processing Addendum: customer is controller, Tucows is processor. -> DPA contemplates both controller and joint-controller roles; it is a 2018 text with obsolete SCC/Privacy Shield references. (The DPA (PDF Last-Modified 2026-07-22, but headed 'Revision May 2018') labels the parties 'Controller or Joint Controller' and 'Processor or Joint Controller', and section 11 sets a joint-controller regime. Tucows is also an ICANN-accredited registrar with its own privacy policy and Data Use Information page (its abuse page says it may disclose complainant email to 'the registrant, reseller, or a trusted third-party'). Annex A is the pre-GDPR SCC text ('Article 26(2) of Directive 95/46/EC'); Annex B refers to EU-US Privacy Shield; Annex C lists sub-processors (Iron Mountain, DENIC escrow, Zendesk, American Express, PayPal, Bluesnap, Pentaho). Breach notice by Tucows is 'without undue delay' (no fixed hours). The customer's own breach-notice duty is MSA 2.8: within 4 hours of learning of password compromise (confirmed). If Mosshatch has EU/UK data subjects, the transfer mechanism needs review.)
- **corrected**: MDRA (Exhibit A) HTML and the PDF Appendix B differ only in wording (dossier flag 13). (A word diff of MSA Appendix B against the current standalone MDRA HTML shows substantive additions in the standalone version: a new suspension ground 'to combat DNS Abuse'; 'Upon cancellation for any reason, you will not receive a refund... Tucows may, in its sole discretion, assist you in transferring your domain name to another registrar'; 'Tucows bills in United States dollars and is not responsible for any change in price due to exchange rates'; a domain-selection clause; and an account-credential safeguarding clause. The standalone MDRA PDF is about 98.5% word-identical to the HTML. Appendix B in the MSA PDF also says 'Tucows Domains Inc.', so that name does not distinguish the versions. MSA 3.3 lets Tucows require 'such other agreements as Tucows shall post on its website', so Mosshatch should present the current standalone MDRA.)
- **corrected**: ICANN Transfer Policy 3.7.5/3.7.6 supports the 60-day locks; the ICANN RDP took effect 2025-08-20. (3.7.5 and 3.7.6 exist with those numbers. The cited page is the 2016 version, whose banner says an updated version was published 2024-02-21 (implementable from 2024-08-21, mandatory by 2025-08-21); the same clauses, and 3.8.5 (COR lock), appear in the 2024 text. OpenSRS KB says the RDP goes into effect 'August 20, 2025', while ICANN's banner says implement 'no later than 21 August 2025'; the one-day difference is immaterial. The 2024 text also holds provisions the dossier does not use (see missed).)
- **corrected**: Status-page incidents (2025-12-02, 2026-01-29, 2026-02-03, 2026-08-05/06 registry link ~24h) and a 2-hour full API outage for maintenance on 2026-09-28. -> 2h full unavailability (22:15-00:15 UTC) inside a 22:00-04:00 UTC window that was extended once; announced 2026-09-15. (incidents.json confirms: 2025-12-02T17:49-19:27 'OpenSRS/HRS API Failures'; 2026-01-29 and 2026-02-03 'Increased Latency & Error Rates in OpenSRS API' (about 6h each); 2026-02-24 SystemDNS record deletion; 2026-04-19 .AI/.LLC/.center; 2026-06-17 identity degradation (41 min); 2026-08-05T19:04 to 08-06T19:40 Identity Digital connection (.me .info .io .ai); 2026-08-11 SystemDNS resolution (~5h40m). The maintenance claim needs correction. The notice of 2026-09-15 announced a 4-hour window from 22:00 UTC with 'API endpoints, web applications, and related services... unavailable between 22:15 and 00:15 UTC' and 'intermittent for the remainder', adding 'if orders submitted via the API fail, they need to be resubmitted'. The window was extended by two hours and completed at 04:00 UTC. So the outage was 2 hours of full unavailability inside a 6-hour disruption window announced 13 days ahead. The 2026-10-11 01:00-05:00 UTC .COM/.NET SRS planned outage is also confirmed.)
- **corrected**: Unverified item: whether the OpenSRS Manage Web Interface (MWI) can be disabled or restricted for API-created registrants. -> MWI access and auth-code visibility are toggleable at the reseller-account level in the RCP Branding tab. (This can be partly resolved. KB 201000063287 (Manage Reseller Branding) says the MWI is an account-level setting: 'You can enable or disable it, hide or show auth codes to end users, and allow or block password resets'; if disabled, a redirect destination is required. It is a reseller-account toggle, not per-registrant, and I have not tested it. Transfer-approval and registrant-verification pages can carry the reseller's logo and custom hostname (CNAME to approve.domainadmin.com).)

Missed by the analyst:
- MSA 21.2, 21.4-21.6 (liability): the API and services are 'as is', Tucows excludes lost revenue, profits, business or goodwill (21.5), and 21.6 makes the customer's SOLE REMEDY for any misrepresentation, breach, negligence, error or defect 'termination of this Agreement, and the return of any unused deposit'. The dossier cites 21.6 only for deposit refundability. It is a headline liability limit: outages or failed orders carry no damages.
- MSA 3.9: violation of, or refusal to comply with, any ICANN policy 'may result in immediate termination', a fast track that bypasses the 30-day cure period in 17.2. MSA 2.5 and 2.7 also bind the customer to use the API and services 'as specified by Tucows from time to time' and to comply with all terms 'established by Tucows from time to time', which is open-ended policy-making separate from the 23.3 amendment clause.
- MSA 23.4 lets the customer not assign the agreement without Tucows' prior written consent (not to be unreasonably withheld), with no matching restriction on Tucows: an M&A, financing or restructuring constraint. MSA 17.4 says only 'access... shall cease' on termination, with no wind-down or portfolio-transition duty. The current MDRA says Tucows 'may, in its sole discretion, assist' with moving a domain to another registrar. KB says an account cannot be closed while active domains remain. The exit plan is therefore an ICANN transfer-out of every domain, or an RSP-to-RSP push, before or despite termination.
- Registrar escalation and disclosure: KB 'Communications at OpenSRS' says the public 'Find Reseller' tool (tucowsdomains.com) returns the reseller's contact information for any domain, and unresponsive resellers are reported to compliance@tucows.com (registrant ID plus documents; follow-up in 24-96 business hours). This is a concrete route to a 17.3 'failed to provide adequate support to Users' suspension and it limits how anonymous the white-label is. Tucows' abuse page (tucowsdomains.com/report-abuse) also says it may disclose complainants' emails to 'the registrant, reseller, or a trusted third-party'.
- MSA Section 8 (Parked Pages): new registrations not yet propagated and expired-but-not-deleted registrations 'may be redirected to a stagnant default or parked page', Tucows controls the content and 'may post pay per click advertisements'; API customers get 50% of net revenue; 8.5 lets customers opt out generally or per domain. The current MDRA also says a stagnant page may be posted after registration 'and any revenues generated from it will be for our own account'. Mosshatch should opt out to avoid third-party ads on customer domains.
- Premium-domain terms: 10.9 says the customer 'shall not copy, store, archive, warehouse or repurpose' Premium Domain Names Services Data and must use only live API data, which conflicts with the dossier's 'cache availability aggressively' advice if premium tiers are enabled. Also 10.3 (no markup on aftermarket premium), 10.6 (no refunds for registry-premium transactions), 10.7 (no auto-renew for registry premium) and Appendix A ('without notice' price changes).
- Automation and agent implications of the MSA: each User must agree to the Tucows registration agreement per registration (3.3), Tucows-mandated consent protocols must be used (3.4), registration IPs, timestamps and communications must be kept and produced within 2 business days (3.9), and User data may be modified only 'in accordance with the instructions of the User' (3.16). Any agent-driven ordering or modification flow must capture and log explicit user assent and instruction. If Mosshatch's own users resell domains onward they become 'sub-resellers' (4.1-4.5), and Mosshatch is financially and contractually responsible for them.
- ICANN Transfer Policy (2024 update) sections 5.2-5.4: registrars must give the Registered Name Holder the AuthInfo code and clear ClientTransferProhibited within 5 calendar days if there is no self-service; the mechanism may not be 'more restrictive than the mechanisms used for changing any aspect of the Registered Name Holder's contact or name server information'; and release may not be refused over a payment dispute. These constrain the proposed passkey-gated auth-code delivery, which must be no harder than a contact or nameserver change. The dossier's cited page is the 2016 version.
- Registration data / ownership: KB 201000115819 says the Organization field 'will officially determine who is recognized as the legal owner'. Mosshatch must not place its own organisation in the registrant Organization field. Resellers can see full WHOIS contact data in the RCP (KB 201000063098), so Mosshatch becomes a holder of registrant personal data (MSA 3.5 privacy notice; 3.6 flows the same protection down to any third-party provider it uses).
- DPA staleness: 'Revision May 2018', SCCs framed under Directive 95/46/EC Article 26(2), Privacy Shield in Annex B, and DPA 1.2 calls Canada non-adequate. A data-transfer mechanism review is needed if Mosshatch serves EU/UK registrants.
- Smaller money and program terms not in the dossier: MSA 12.3 (gross-up for tax withholding), 12.10 ($35 fee on chargebacks or disputed payments), ACH failure charges ($1 second attempt, $25 service charge), unquantified daily minimum/maximum card top-up limits, and GST/HST on the $95 activation fee. MSA 16.2 says the API software is GPL while the documentation index links the LGPL; this matters only if OpenSRS toolkit code is embedded. The MSA gives no trademark licence to use OpenSRS/Tucows marks, though 3.18 requires identifying as a reseller.
- Storefront-side refund evidence on the unresolved add-grace question: KB 201000114626 says a domain 'registered within the last 5 days' can be revoked (with a $1 revoke fee) and later registrations, renewals and Contact Privacy are non-recoverable. This is Storefront-specific, not API documentation, but it is the only concrete grace-window figure found.
- Pricing page vs KB tier timing inconsistency (calendar-year vs April 1), and TLD-specific checkout duties from Google Registry (HSTS/SSL disclosure for .dev/.app), which the dossier does cover.

### Verifier lens: tech (confidence medium; 17 claims confirmed)

Nothing load-bearing was refuted. Confidence is high on prices and medium overall, because every API verdict rests on documentation only. I have no credentials, and a single probe of horizon.opensrs.net:55443 was reset by the tunnel, reproducing the analyst's result.

**Prices.** I re-derived all six TLDs across all four tiers from the Chromium-rendered rate card. .com 14.50, .ai 111 and .io 60 match for register, renew and transfer, and the restore fees match. The scheduled .com (15.25 on 2026-11-01) and .studio (51 on 2026-10-06) rises match the price-changes page dated Sep 29, 2026. The rate card is the contractual reseller fee schedule, since the MSA's Appendix A points to it through a 308 redirect. Two cost details were understated. The 3% card top-up fee is a gross-up (deposit / 0.97, about +3.09%). The .ai first charge of 222 is still an inference.

**Corrections to the analyst's coverage rows:**
- **startTransferIn:** the Transfer-in KB article says the legacy Reseller Web Interface is the only interface that can supply an auth code with the order. The API reference documents `auth_info`, and Horizon cannot exercise transfers, so this row is documentation-only.
- **DNS:** nothing in the docs says `set_dns_zone` replaces the whole zone. The Reseller Control Panel guide says a template overwrites only the record types it defines, so the semantics need a test.
- **Renew retry codes:** the renew doc has a 400 "already renewed" variant the analyst missed.
- **Allow-list cap:** the "max 5" IP rules is a soft cap (contact Support for more).

Also:
- `cancel_pending_orders` is a bulk, date-based cancel, so it is a poor fit for clearing a single stuck renewal.
- The 2026-09-28 maintenance was extended to 02:15 UTC, so "2-hour outage" understates it.
- There is no changelog. The docs' per-page update dates are the only signal, and I found several deprecation notes the dossier lacks (see `missed`).

Not confirmed:
- **corrected**: IP allow-list: live only, maximum 5 rules, /25-/32, changes take up to 15 minutes; one reseller-wide key; only the primary user can authenticate; new key invalidates the old immediately -> Five IP rules is the default cap; more available on request to Support (All confirmed except that the cap of five is soft: KB 201000063062 says 'You can add a maximum of five IPs ... If you need more, contact OpenSRS Support'. Rules can be Allow or Deny. Auth scheme (X-Username, X-Signature = md5(md5(xml+key)+key), text/xml, XML only) confirmed in the Quickstart and Overview docs.)
- **corrected**: renew: retry after success returns 555/465 'already renewed' or 541 (natural idempotency guard); period 1-10; currentexpirationyear required -> Treat 465, 555, 541 and 400 ('already renewed'/'renewal applied') as already-renewed or in-flight signals (Parameters confirmed. The renew page lists 465 (already renewed), 541 (year mismatch) and 400 variants ('Domain already renewed; another renewal cannot be applied until the first request completes at the registry' / 'already in renewal applied state'); 555 appears only in the global codes table. The analyst's list omits the 400 variants, which an adapter must also treat as already-renewed or in-flight. Premium-name renewals need premium_price_to_verify, documented only on the page with slug renew-domain_deprecated ('Renew (premium domain)').)
- **corrected**: DNS CRUD is partial: SET_DNS_ZONE is replace-all only, types A/AAAA/CNAME/MX/SRV/TXT, no TTL or CAA; per-record CRUD exists only in the Storefront JSON API -> Per-record ops absent; whole-zone vs per-record-type replacement semantics of set_dns_zone are undocumented (Record types and the absence of TTL, CAA and NS confirmed, and no per-record DNS command exists in the 154-page API doc index. But set_dns_zone is documented only as 'Set the records for a domain's DNS zone'; nothing states it replaces the entire zone. The control-panel guide (KB 201000063118) says applying a template 'overwrites only the record types defined in that template', so type-scoped overwrite is possible. It also says TXT text is limited to 254 characters (a risk for long DKIM keys; not stated for the API). The Storefront JSON API (600 req/min, CAA/NS/DS/TTL, OAuth client credentials) covers only Shopco-NS Storefront domains and has no register or renew endpoint. Semantics need a live test, and Horizon SystemDNS support is unknown.)
- **corrected**: startTransferIn: yes via SW_REGISTER reg_type=transfer with auth_info (skips owner approval email); pre-check CHECK_TRANSFER -> Documented but conflicting (API auth_info vs KB 'RWI only'); untestable in Horizon (The API reference documents auth_info ('If provided and valid, the transfer will not send an approval email'). But the Transfer-in KB says 'The RWI is the only interface that lets you supply the authorization code with the order itself', an unflagged doc conflict. Horizon cannot exercise transfers, so this cannot be resolved in the sandbox. Owner-approval window is five days in some passages and seven in others, even within one KB article (analyst's 5 vs 7 conflict confirmed). The admin email is parsed from the losing registrar's WHOIS; missing or invalid goes to a manual holding queue, a risk after gTLD redaction when no auth_info is given. Downgrade to 'yes per docs, unverified'.)
- **corrected**: Incident history: 2025-12-02, 2026-01-29, 2026-02-03 API incidents; .ai/.io registry link down about 24 h on 2026-08-05; SystemDNS incidents 2026-02-24 and 2026-08-11; 2-hour full API outage for maintenance 2026-09-28 -> Maintenance ran 2026-09-28 22:00Z to 2026-09-29 02:15Z after extension; hard-down window 22:15-00:15Z (Dates and durations all match the status-page JSON (2026-08-05 19:04Z to 2026-08-06 19:40Z; API latency incidents about 6 h each). Correction: the 2026-09-28 maintenance was extended by two hours to 2026-09-29 02:15 UTC (update posted 01:41Z); the 22:15-00:15Z figure is only the announced hard-down window, with intermittent behaviour for the remaining hours. The notice cites 'improving the security, stability and resiliency', and what changed is not stated. The analyst missed the 2025-10-10/11 domain-lookup outage (about 9 h, relapsed) and 2025-09-15 registration/renewal/transfer errors.)
- **corrected**: Docs changelog or deprecation notices: none noted by the analyst (No formal changelog exists (/changelog and /docs/changelog return 404, only version v1.0, and the KB 'Updates & Announcements' folder holds just two unrelated articles). Deprecations found in the docs: lookup 'noservice' and 'price_status'; get_transfers_in 'pending_admin'; modify whois_privacy_state values N/Y; tld_data 'registrant_type'; slug renew-domain_deprecated. Recent page updates (per-page updatedAt): webhook/event pages 2026-07-28; codes and lookup 2026-06-22; sw_register 2026-03-11; get_registrant_verification_status 2026-05-07 (.de NIS2 fields); 129 of 154 pages unchanged since 2025-11-11.)

Missed by the analyst:
- MSA 3.2 requires use of the lookup capability 'mandated by Tucows' to determine availability, so availability cannot lean on third-party checkers as the authority, and the excessive-use fee is on that same tool.
- MSA 21.2 provides the API and services 'as is' without warranty, and no uptime SLA or service credits were found in the MSA. Combined with the incident history, there is no contractual recourse for outages.
- The 2026-09-28 maintenance ran to about 02:15 UTC on 2026-09-29 (extended). Its stated aim was security/stability/resiliency, and whether it changed API behaviour, endpoints or IP handling is unknown, so re-test connectivity and auth once credentials exist.
- Status-page incident 2025-10-10 21:48Z to 2025-10-11 07:03Z 'Domain lookup failures' (about 9 h, relapsed) and 2025-09-15 registration/renewal/transfer errors were not listed in the dossier; the lookup outage matters because availability is single-sourced through OpenSRS lookup.
- cancel_pending_orders is a date-based bulk cancel (required to_date cancels all pending or declined orders up to that date), so it is unsuitable for clearing one stuck draft renewal; process_pending with command=cancel and an order_id is the per-order tool.
- Sub-reseller support (create/modify/get/pay subreseller and the as_subreseller parameter on any command) offers per-tenant funded balances as a possible spend-containment layer despite the single master key. Its enforcement behaviour is unverified, and sub-reseller volume counts toward tier thresholds.
- Doc deprecations not in the dossier: lookup noservice and price_status, get_transfers_in pending_admin, modify whois_privacy_state Y/N values, tld_data registrant_type, and the deprecated-slug 'Renew (premium domain)' page that is the only place premium_price_to_verify for renewals is documented.
- KB conflict not flagged: 'Renewing Domains' says nameservers switch to OpenSRS expired-domain NS 'on the expiry date', while 'Redeeming an Expired Domain' says three days after expiry.
- The control-panel DNS guide limits TXT text to 254 characters and MX/SRV priority to 0-255; long DKIM keys may not fit, and API limits are undocumented. SystemDNS also has no TTL, CAA or NS control even in the control panel; only Storefront's Shopco DNS does.
- Code 300 also means 'Registration is pending contact agreeing to share information per GDPR', not just a rate-limit deferral.
- Registry maintenance windows that stop availability checks, purchases, renewals and updates are published on the status page (Verisign .com/.net SRS 2026-10-11 01:00-05:00Z, 4 hours, plus earlier 45-minute windows 2026-08-23 and 2026-09-20), so the adapter needs a maintenance-window-aware retry policy.
- Transfer-in without auth_info depends on a valid admin email parsed from the losing registrar's WHOIS, otherwise the order goes to manual OpenSRS review; always require the auth code in the Mosshatch flow.
- The .io ccTLD's underlying territory (British Indian Ocean Territory per the TLD chart) carries long-term delegation risk that the dossier flagged as unresearched; still not researched here.

## reg-porkbun
Dossier: `reg-porkbun.md`

### Verifier lens: legal (confidence high; 14 claims confirmed)

The analyst's central conclusion holds: no document I opened forbids resale of second-level domains. The DNRA (effective 2025-03-17) contemplates purchasing "on behalf of a third party" and "reselling", and the v3.44 API spec disclaims any reseller relationship. "Conditional, fallback only" is fair. Quotes are accurate except where noted below. Corrections: (1) the at-any-time, without-notice suspension quote comes from a clause limited to breach or legal requirement, while the truly broad sole-discretion power sits in the Interruption of Domain Services clause; (2) liability is the lesser of fees paid and $500, and Porkbun separately disclaims liability for suspension or loss of a domain and for service unavailability; (3) the indemnity is broader than summarised; (4) "no partner surface" is too absolute because llms-full.txt labels /auth/login a Partner-only endpoint (auth:login key scope); (5) sandbox keys do not need a Porkbun account, since POST /apikey/request with sandbox:true is documented to return them (documented, not exercised); (6) the "which Porkbun says it does not have" attribution for RAA 3.12 agreements overreaches. Missed items: Oregon law and AAA three-arbitrator arbitration in Portland with prevailing-party fee shifting; agreeToTerms semantics; the 15-day WHOIS-accuracy non-response suspension trigger; discretionary expiry handling including WHOIS registrant replacement; the DNRA answer on the auto-renew payment source; no DPA or processor terms; and the DNRA "Fees" definition tied to any written agreement, the only hook for negotiated terms. I could not obtain a Porkbun statement on whether written white-label resale permission or reseller terms are available. That needs a direct request to Porkbun. WebSearch was unavailable (budget exhausted), so all checks were direct fetches of primary URLs.

Not confirmed:
- **corrected**: No document forbids resale of domains; the only resale prohibition is for the Email Service (Product ToS) -> Two resale-type prohibitions exist: Email Service resale, and sale/lease of Porkbun third-level domains. None for standard second-level domains. (Load-bearing part confirmed. I opened the DNRA, Product ToS, Privacy Policy, Abuse Policy, Data Disclosure Policy, Registry Conditions, Ambassador and Marketplace agreements; none prohibits second-level domain resale, and the legal index has no API terms or reseller agreement. The Product ToS Email clause is verbatim ('for Your use only. Any unauthorized resale of the Email Service ... expressly prohibited'). Correction: 'only' is inexact, because the Third-level Domain and Website Service section also says 'You may not sell or lease third-level domains to third parties'. That is irrelevant to .com/.ai/.dev/.io/.app/.studio.)
- **corrected**: No reseller, partner, wholesale program or page exists; affiliate program discontinued -> No PUBLIC reseller/partner/wholesale program; a non-public partner tier with elevated API-key scopes (auth:login) is referenced in the API docs. (Public surface confirmed: I re-probed 15 candidate paths (reseller, resellers, partners, partner, wholesale, enterprise, business, reseller-program, agencies, agency, api, bulk, white-label, whitelabel, developers) and all returned 404. /affiliate shows 'The affiliate program has been discontinued.' KB search for 'resell' returns nothing. Correction: llms-full.txt documents POST /auth/login as 'Partner-only endpoint (requires auth:login access on the API key)', and the spec refers to 'partner-only routes'. So a non-public, partner-gated API capability exists, contradicting a flat 'no partner surface' and the dossier's reading of the /auth routes as trust-and-safety. It is login plumbing, not evidence of a reseller program, and its terms are unknown.)
- **corrected**: Porkbun may suspend or terminate 'at any time, and without notice'; ORDERS_BLOCKED exists -> Termination without notice is conditioned on breach or legal requirement; separate sole-discretion 'any other lawful reason' corrective-action clause gives the at-will effect. (The quoted sentence is conditional: 'at any time, and without notice to You, in the event of a breach of this Agreement or if termination or suspension is or becomes required by any policy of ICANN, and applicable law, or by any governmental authority'. The broad power is in INTERRUPTION OF DOMAIN SERVICES: 'in its sole discretion and without liability ... immediate corrective action ... if Porkbun determines ... (ii) Your conduct may ... place unreasonable demands upon Porkbun ... (iv) for any other lawful reason', with all fees non-refundable. Account holders must give 30 days' written notice to cancel. ORDERS_BLOCKED is in the spec error table ('This account cannot place new orders. HTTP 403') with no published criteria. Substance right (at will in practice, no cure period); the quote alone overstates.)
- **corrected**: Liability capped at $500 -> Lesser of amounts paid and $500, plus a blanket exclusion for suspension/loss/modification of registrations and service unavailability. (Verbatim: 'MAXIMUM AGGREGATE LIABILITY EXCEED THE TOTAL AMOUNT PAID BY YOU FOR THE SERVICES, BUT IN NO EVENT GREATER THAN ... ($500.00)', so the cap is the lesser of fees paid and $500. Porkbun also states it 'WILL NOT BE LIABLE, UNDER ANY CIRCUMSTANCES, FOR ANY (i) SUSPENSION, LOSS, OR MODIFICATION OF YOUR DOMAIN NAME REGISTRATION ... (iii) UNAVAILABILITY OF SERVICES ... OR ANY INTERRUPTION OF BUSINESS', with services 'AS IS' and no lost-profits damages. Practical recourse for a wrongful suspension is effectively nil.)
- **corrected**: Account holder indemnifies Porkbun, ICANN and registry operators for use of domains in its name, IP disputes and registrant transfers (Understated. The indemnity covers third-party claims arising from '(i) Your use of the Services, (ii) Your application for and registration of, or failure to register or renew, a particular domain name; (iii) Your use of any domain name registered in Your name; (iv) Your breach of this Agreement; (v) IP disputes; (vi) processing any registrant transfers; (vii) use of any domain affected by a registrant transfer', including attorneys' fees, extending to affiliates, agents and subcontractors. Also a $50/hour administrative fee plus attorneys' fees if the account holder alleges unauthorised access, and a $120 reversal fee. 'You' is defined to include the account's agents and 'the registrant listed in the WHOIS contact information', which blurs the split between Mosshatch and an end-customer registrant.)
- **corrected**: ICANN RAA 1.24 would classify Mosshatch as a Reseller; RAA 3.12 requires registrar-reseller written agreements 'which Porkbun says it does not have' (Text confirmed in the 2013 RAA: 1.24 (limb (b): actual knowledge, collecting registration data, facilitating entry of the registration agreement); 3.12 'must enter into written agreements with all of its Resellers'; 3.12.1 to 3.12.7 as summarised. The classification of Mosshatch is legal analysis, not fact, but plausible. Correction: Porkbun never says it lacks such agreements; it says only that the API 'does not establish a reseller relationship'. Whether Porkbun holds RAA 3.12 agreements with anyone is unknown. The risk cuts both ways: Porkbun's disclaimer cannot change the RAA definition, and Porkbun may treat the pattern as a compliance problem.)
- **refuted**: Sandbox keys require a Porkbun account (summary sandbox.details and an Unverified item) -> Sandbox key pair obtainable with no account or credentials via POST /apikey/request {"sandbox":true} per docs (unexercised). (llms-full.txt POST /apikey/request: 'No credentials are required' and 'pass sandbox: true to skip approval and get a sandbox key pair back immediately' (pk1_sb_/sk1_sb_, fresh test account with $1000 fake credit, rate limit 20 per IP per hour). I did not call it. Documented behaviour only.)
- **unverifiable**: .ai 2-year minimum (~$165) likely exceeds the $100 API order cap (The cap text is confirmed ('A single API registration cannot exceed $100') and pricing/get shows .ai registration 82.70 and transfer 165.09. Whether the API cost for .ai is 82.70 or a 2-year 165.40, and whether ORDER_TOO_LARGE trips, needs an authenticated dryRun; I made no authenticated calls. The analyst correctly labelled it unverified.)

Missed by the analyst:
- DNRA governing law and dispute resolution is absent from the dossier: Oregon law, AAA binding arbitration on either party's demand, three-member panel in Portland, prevailing-party costs and fees shift; third-party suits against the account holder go to D. Or.; 30 days' written notice to cancel with no refund.
- Each API purchase's agreeToTerms is documented as confirming DNRA + Product ToS + Privacy Policy + auto-renewal terms, so the account holder is bound to all of them per call; this does not replace collecting each customer's own DNRA acceptance, and the DNRA can change on 30 days' email notice (version tracking needed).
- llms-full.txt labels POST /auth/login a 'Partner-only endpoint (requires auth:login access on the API key)' and the spec mentions 'partner-only routes', so a non-public partner tier exists; the dossier treats these routes as trust-and-safety. This supports asking Porkbun for written terms.
- DNRA 'Fees' is defined as prices 'set forth in any written agreement between You and Porkbun', the only textual hook for a negotiated commercial or reseller agreement.
- DNRA makes failure to answer a WHOIS-accuracy inquiry for over 15 days an 'incurable material breach' and a basis for suspension; verification mail goes to the registrant email, and the public API exposes no EPP or status codes to detect the resulting hold.
- DNRA expiry powers beyond the KB: Porkbun may replace the WHOIS registrant contact of an expired domain, park with ads, auction 'anytime after expiration' without notice, decline to offer the Reactivation Period (30-43 days) or RGP, and keep the name in a 120-day ERGP at the RGP fee.
- Auto-renew payment source (listed as unverified in the dossier) is answered by the DNRA: the payment method associated with the account first, account credit only if no method is specified; renewals are final, irreversible and non-refundable.
- Porkbun disclaims liability under any circumstances for suspension, loss or modification of a registration and for service unavailability; the $500 cap is also limited to fees paid. There is no SLA, no partner contact, and support email averages 6 hours (12+ at peak).
- Indemnity breadth (use of the Services, failure to register or renew, any breach), the $50/hour administrative fee for unauthorised-access claims, the $120 transfer-reversal fee, and the DNRA definition of 'You' including the account's agents and the WHOIS registrant.
- No DPA, processor or SCC offering for account holders; Porkbun is a controller and Mosshatch would be an independent controller of the same registrant data (relevant for EU/UK customers).
- Sandbox keys are obtainable without any account via POST /apikey/request {sandbox:true} per docs (technical, outside my lens), contradicting the summary's 'requires a Porkbun account'.
- Registry Conditions were re-issued effective 2026-08-28 and bind existing registrations immediately; registrant indemnities to registries apply.
- Porkbun itself resells CentralNic Reseller for .ca, .cx, .it.com, .la, .nl, .am (registrar of record differs), so the 'processed directly by Porkbun as registrar of record' sentence is not universal; no starter TLD is affected.
- ICANN Transfer Policy II.C.1.2: on a change of registrant the registrar must tell the new registrant they must enter a registration agreement with the registrar, so in the customer-as-registrant model the end customer is in direct contract with Porkbun (and is within the DNRA's 'You').
- No sanctions/OFAC, export-control, assignment or change-of-control clause was found in the DNRA.

### Verifier lens: tech (confidence high; 20 claims confirmed)

The analyst's adapter-coverage rows hold up against the OpenAPI spec (v3.44, 119 operations) and against live calls I made with throwaway sandbox keys. I found no lock, auth-code or transfer-out route in any listing. Five points need correction. (1) Sandbox keys need no Porkbun account: POST /apikey/request {"sandbox":true} returns a key pair immediately (I called it twice). (2) The .ai question is settled in sandbox: 82.70 is per year, the 2-year API cost is 165.40, and creating a .ai is refused with ORDER_TOO_LARGE. (3) The .io (28.12) and .studio (11.84) first-year prices appear in pricing/get and on the website, but the API quotes 51.80 and 32.44 (checkDomain, dryRun, COST_MISMATCH); .dev and .app promos do pass through. (4) A register-attempt rate-limit breach returns HTTP 400 with a generated code, not the documented 429 RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED. (5) The changelog contains no removals or deprecations, but it is not strictly additive. New findings: transferred-in domains arrive without API access, and .io creates return an undocumented FRAUD_BLOCK. Production behaviour for sandbox-only observations stays unverified. WebSearch was exhausted, so there was no external incident search.

- **unverifiable**: New API registrations are created locked (v3.23) (The changelog says a domain registered through /domain/create now comes back with the registrar lock applied. In sandbox, GET /domain/get on a fresh API-created .com returned securityLock:0. Sandbox may not simulate the lock; production behaviour is unverified.)
- **unverifiable**: Behaviour of a repeat /domain/create without an Idempotency-Key is undocumented, so reconcile with GET /domain/get first (Spec says no header means no caching. In sandbox a repeat create of an already-owned name, without a key or with a fresh key, succeeded and charged again ($11.08 each; orderIds 9913724 and 9913725; expireDate unchanged). This is a sandbox artifact: sandbox availability ignores sandbox-owned names (dryRun on my own name says 'available'), while really-registered names such as google.com return DOMAIN_NOT_AVAILABLE. Production behaviour is unverified and the sandbox cannot test duplicate handling.)
- **corrected**: .ai 2-year minimum (about $165) likely exceeds the $100 API cap (unverified); .ai 82.70 may be per year -> 82.70 per year; API registration cost 165.40 (16540 cents); refused with ORDER_TOO_LARGE (Now verified in sandbox on two fresh accounts. checkDomain shows price 82.70 with minDuration 2 (price is per year). A create with cost 8270 returned COST_MISMATCH 'minimum allowed duration (2 years). Expected: 16540 cents ($165.40)'. With cost 16540, and with dryRun cost 0, it returned ORDER_TOO_LARGE 'Registrations over $100 are not currently supported via API'. getRegistrationRequirements/ai shows registrationDurationYears:2. So .ai cannot be registered via the API. A .ai transfer (165.09) is not capped (dryRun and a real sandbox transfer succeeded). The renewal cap is not testable because transferred-in domains lack API access.)
- **corrected**: A sandbox exists and its keys (pk1_sb_/sk1_sb_) are created at porkbun.com/account/api, which requires a Porkbun account -> Sandbox keys can be minted with no account via POST /apikey/request {sandbox:true} (Existence confirmed (spec 'Sandbox / test mode', same base URL, sandbox:true, X-Porkbun-Sandbox header, /sandbox/topup default 100000 cents max 1000000, /sandbox/reset, /sandbox/triggerWebhook). But an account is not required: POST /apikey/request with {"sandbox":true} returns a key pair instantly for a throwaway account with $1000 fake credit (llms.txt: 'Get one instantly, no signup'). Rate limit 20 requests per IP per hour. Called twice, both HTTP 200. Hosting, email and closeouts return SANDBOX_UNSUPPORTED per the spec (not exercised).)
- **corrected**: Sandbox behaviours listed as unverified (transfer completion timing, cap enforcement, 30-day renewal rule, expiry time-shift) (Resolved live. Transfers complete immediately (message 'simulated as completing immediately', getTransfer DONE, holdForDnsSetup ignored). The $100 cap and .ai 2-year term ARE enforced. The 30-day-after-registration renewal rule is NOT enforced by dryRun renew (a domain registered seconds earlier returned wouldSucceed:true). No time-shift mechanism is documented or found; not tested.)
- **corrected**: Rate-limit breaches return HTTP 429 RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED with Retry-After -> Register attempt-limit breach = HTTP 400 with a generated code, no Retry-After (Not true for the register attempt limit. Two parallel creates: the loser returned HTTP 400 with code '1_OUT_OF_1_CREATE_ATTEMPTS_WITHIN_1_SECONDS_USED', no Retry-After header and no next_action. An adapter must treat this code pattern as retryable rather than as a permanent failure. The 429 shape is documented for /apikey/* and hosting velocity guards, and was not exercised.)
- **corrected**: Price .io: register 28.12 (first-year sale), renew 51.80, transfer 51.80 -> API-quoted registration 51.80 (28.12 shown only by pricing/get and the website) (Renew and transfer 51.80 confirmed (checkDomain, dryRun 5180). Registration: pricing/get, /products/domains, /tld/io and the retail website search (Chromium) all show 1st Yr Sale $28.12 vs $51.80. But the authenticated API quote for a fresh sandbox account is 51.80 with firstYearPromo:'no', and a create with cost 2812 returned COST_MISMATCH 'Expected: 5180 cents ($51.80)'. Additionally every .io create in sandbox (dryRun or real, two fresh accounts) returned an undocumented FRAUD_BLOCK '(001)'. Production promo eligibility unverified.)
- **corrected**: Price .studio: register 11.84 (first-year sale), renew 32.44, transfer 32.44 -> API-quoted registration 32.44 (Renew and transfer 32.44 confirmed. The 11.84 sale price is on pricing/get, the pricing page and the tld page, but checkDomain and dryRun quote 32.44 (firstYearPromo:'no'); a create with cost 1184 returned COST_MISMATCH 'Expected: 3244 cents ($32.44)'. Same gap seen for .org (web 7.98 vs API 11.84).)

But the log records non-additive changes: v3.31 changed five flag fields from string '1' or number 0 to integers; v3.13 replaced hosting product+plan with a single sku; v3.20 changed /dns/create refusals from 200 SUCCESS to HTTP 400 with codes; v3.26 fixed an inverted sort; v3.30 changed check and register limits and behaviour. Also v3.22 fixed 'a domain could only ever be transferred in once through the API'. Adapters should tolerate type and status-code drift.)
- **corrected**: Whether API-created domains are automatically opted in to API access (unverified) (Sandbox: an API-created .com has apiAccess:1, but an inbound-transferred domain arrives with apiAccess:0 and is refused for renew, DNS and updateAutoRenew until opted in on the website. The spec has no endpoint that toggles apiAccess (checked every request body). Production behaviour unverified.)
- **unverifiable**: Auto-renew is on by default (KB 70) and can be toggled via API updateAutoRenew (KB 70 (updated Aug 19, 2026) says the setting is enabled by default when you register at Porkbun, and updateAutoRenew worked in sandbox. But a sandbox API-created .com showed autoRenew:0 while the transferred-in .ai showed autoRenew:1, so do not assume the default for API registrations; set it explicitly.)

Missed by the analyst:
- Sandbox keys need no Porkbun account: POST /apikey/request {"sandbox":true} returns a key pair instantly (20/IP/hour). This makes CI-style adapter tests cheap and account-free.
- Sandbox limits for adapter testing: transfers complete instantly and holdForDnsSetup is ignored (PENDING* states untestable); sandbox-created names still show as 'available', so duplicate-registration handling cannot be tested; the 30-day renewal rule is not enforced in dryRun; securityLock stays 0 and autoRenew 0 on API-created domains.
- Register attempt-limit breach is HTTP 400 with the generated code 1_OUT_OF_1_CREATE_ATTEMPTS_WITHIN_1_SECONDS_USED, no Retry-After, no next_action; the documented 429 RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED is not what /domain/create returns. Similar generated codes and missing next_action on the DNS opt-in error.
- Inbound-transferred domains arrive with apiAccess=0 (in sandbox): renew (API_ACCESS_DISABLED), DNS read/write and updateAutoRenew are refused until the account owner opts in on the website ('Opt In All Domains' or per domain). No API endpoint changes apiAccess. The transfer-in adapter needs the account-wide opt-in set up front.
- Undocumented FRAUD_BLOCK '(001)' on every .io create (dryRun and real, two fresh sandbox accounts) while .co/.me/.xyz/.org/.net/.sh/.com/.dev/.app/.studio pass. Cause unknown (sandbox artifact or a real .io screen); needs a question to Porkbun before .io is promised.
- Public pricing/get and the website show first-year sale prices for .io (28.12) and .studio (11.84) and .org (7.98) that the authenticated checkDomain/dryRun quote did not apply (51.80, 32.44, 11.84). An adapter must quote only from checkDomain or dryRun cost:0 and must never use pricing/get as a billing price.
- For min-duration>1 TLDs (.ai), checkDomain 'price' is per year while /domain/create 'cost' must be price x minDuration (16540 for .ai), and the docs' priceType=renewal parameter is not in the schema and is ignored by checkDomain.
- Docs inconsistencies: llms.txt says checkDomain needs no auth (live: API_KEY_REQUIRED); spec says single-check limit raised in 3.29 but the changelog entry is v3.30; TransferDomainResponse example shows success limit 50 beside natural-language 1000; per-TLD requestSchema types whoisPrivacy as yes/no strings while the OpenAPI schema says boolean; updateAutoRenew returns HTTP 200 SUCCESS with per-domain errors in results.
- Changelog contains non-additive changes (v3.31 field type change, v3.13 hosting body change, v3.20 200-to-400 change, v3.26 sort fix), so the 'strictly additive' guarantee should not be relied on blindly; no deprecation notices exist.
- Other API features relevant to Mosshatch that the dossier did not cover: GET /dns/preflight/{domain}?intent=transfer-out|move-nameservers, POST /dns/import is idempotent skip-existing (usable as a converge step), a DomainAttest ownership-verification guide (/llms/guides/verify-domain-ownership), a GoDaddy migration (beta) guide, and account/invite for creating customer-owned accounts.
- The blog's earlier price projections (.io $53.00 from 2026-01-19; .ai $84/$84/$167 from 2026-03-05) never took effect; live .io regular is 51.80 and .ai 82.70, so scheduled-change estimates need a margin.
- Could not check: production behaviour of sandbox-only observations; ACH price flow into API quotes; whether the general per-key budget will be enforced; real-world incident history (WebSearch budget was exhausted).

## reg-resellerclub
Dossier: `reg-resellerclub.md`

### Verifier lens: legal (confidence medium; 21 claims confirmed)

I could not refute the analyst's legal and program findings. Every quote I re-fetched from the LogicBoxes demo-registrar templates and KB (demo.myorderbox.com) matches, and I found no clause that bars resale, white-labelling or automated API ordering. All of this is LogicBoxes' default template, not ResellerClub's own contract. ResellerClub's site and PublicDomainRegistry's legal pages return a Cloudflare 403 challenge, the challenge host is blocked by egress policy, and the WebSearch budget is used up. So ResellerClub-specific terms, the "$199 minimum deposit" claim, slab tiers and KYC remain unverifiable. I found four corrections. (1) The analyst cited the superseded 2013 ICANN RAA; the current RAA (21 Jan 2024, global amendment effective 5 Apr 2024) renumbers 3.18 and adds a DNS Abuse mitigation duty (3.18.2) that flows down to resellers through 3.12. (2) The "legal name and country not editable after signup" claim mis-cites a customer-side KB article; the reseller-side article says only legal name is locked. (3) "Debited amounts never refunded" omits the clause's own exception, "unless such debit is found to be erroneous", and the KB's money-back grace period. (4) The registry auto-renew line "locks our funds" refers to the provider's funds, not the reseller's. The analyst also missed several legal items. The registrant agreement puts default and expired-domain pages with ads on the domain and lets the provider insert ad-serving wildcard DNS records. That agreement also names Massachusetts law and arbitration, and end customers contract directly with PDR. The Master lets LogicBoxes cancel any order within 30 days, and the sanctions ban covers end customers too. Master 17 requires written consent to reference the provider. The templates contain no data-protection or DPA terms, and Appendix A limits use of OrderBox data. A "no less protective" flow-down is required in Mosshatch's own customer terms. The contract's TLD lists omit .ai, .io, .dev and .app. Verdict: the "fallback, conditional resale" conclusion stands. Do not treat the contract findings as ResellerClub's actual terms until a human reads its live agreements.

Not confirmed:
- **unverifiable**: ResellerClub's own live agreements, entities, governing law and clause numbering match the LogicBoxes templates (www.resellerclub.com, manage.resellerclub.com/servlet/ViewAgreementServlet and publicdomainregistry.com/legal all return HTTP 403 (Cloudflare challenge). The challenge host returns 502 on CONNECT (egress policy), and I did not work around it. The template shows 'Demo Registrar' and 'PDR Ltd., a Cayman Islands Limited company' with an Effective Date generated at request time.)
- **corrected**: Negative balance: termination after 10 days, provider may take ownership of all Orders after 30 days; card chargeback lets it transfer customer orders; debited amounts never refunded -> Debited amounts are not refunded 'unless such debit is found to be erroneous' (Appendix C 4(4)); a money-back grace-period refund on deletion exists per KB, possibly minus a deletion charge. (The 10/30-day and chargeback mechanics are verbatim (Appendix C 1(5), 1(6), 2(5)). The chargeback clause is about Mosshatch's own funding payment, not end-customer chargebacks. 'Never refunded' is overstated: 4(4) says 'unless such debit is found to be erroneous', and the KB grants a money-back grace-period refund on deletion (faq1451), possibly with a deletion charge.)
- **corrected**: Sanctions/OFAC screening: no Cuba, Iran, North Korea, Syria, Crimea; continuous checks -> The sanctions restriction applies to Resellers, Sub Resellers and Customers, so end customers must be screened. (The quote is right but truncated. kb/answer/2489 continues 'These include Resellers, Sub Resellers and Customers.' Mosshatch must therefore screen end customers at checkout; violating the OFAC Provision (Master 4(8), 6(12)) allows suspension or termination without refund.)
- **corrected**: Legal name and country are not editable after signup (reseller program requirement) -> Reseller legal name only is locked (faq1493); legal name and country locking is the customer-account rule (faq1492). (The cited faq1492 is a Customer Control Panel article (legal name and country for customer accounts). The reseller article is faq1493: only the reseller's Legal Name (Company Name) is locked after signup; country is not mentioned. Support can change it 'in case of a genuine requirement'.)
- **corrected**: ICANN RAA 3.12 requires the sponsoring registrar to bind resellers (3.12.1, 3.12.2, 3.12.5, 3.12.7); RAA 3.18 abuse duties (abuse contact 3.18.1, 24x7 law-enforcement line 3.18.2) -> Current RAA is the 21 January 2024 version (global amendment effective 5 April 2024). 3.18.2 is the DNS Abuse mitigation duty and the 24x7 law-enforcement contact is 3.18.3. (3.12 text is right. But the analyst cited the 17 Sep 2013 RAA, which is superseded. ICANN now lists the RAA 'Updated 21 January 2024' with a global amendment effective 5 April 2024. In the current text 3.18.1 adds DNS Abuse coverage, a webform option and receipt confirmation; new 3.18.2 requires mitigation on actionable evidence of DNS Abuse; the 24x7 law-enforcement contact is 3.18.3; procedures are 3.18.4. 3.12.2 now cites lookup.icann.org. The analyst also omitted 3.12.3 (identify the sponsoring registrar on inquiry) and 3.12.4 (privacy/proxy specification).)
- **corrected**: Data-handling terms (Master 13(1) LogicBoxes owns the OrderBox Database; 13(3) will not use it to contact customers) -> No data-protection or DPA terms exist in the templates; Appendix A 2(3) restricts the reseller's use of OrderBox data. (13(1) and 13(3) are accurate but incomplete. The Master and Domain Extension contain no GDPR, personal-data, DPA or processor terms (searched). 4(3), 6(2)(3), 6(4) and 13(2) give LogicBoxes and its Service Providers broad access and disclosure rights ('for any other appropriate reason'). Appendix A 2(3) limits reseller use of OrderBox data to performing services and communicating about the Order, Support or Parent's services, and bans mass mailing and selling the data.)
- **unverifiable**: ResellerClub minimum deposit (a search summary claimed $199), price slabs by total receipts, volume commitments, approval and KYC steps (ResellerClub pages return 403 and the WebSearch budget is exhausted (200 of 200). In the LogicBoxes templates and KB I found no minimum deposit, slab table, volume commitment or approval step beyond sanctions screening. faq307 shows automatic sign-up to all products.)
- **unverifiable**: Contract documents cover the six launch TLDs (.com .ai .dev .io .app .studio) (The Extension's TLD definitions and Appendix B (fetched in full) list .STUDIO and .COM but not .ai, .io, .dev or .app. The KB does document .dev/.app (faq1375) and '.AI ... only 2 years' (a752), and .io appears nowhere. The template appendix is probably stale, so contractual coverage of four TLDs is unverified until ResellerClub's live Extension is read.)
- **unverifiable**: ICANN Transfer Policy may have been replaced (open item in the dossier) (ICANN's Consensus Policies page (fetched today) still lists 'Domain Name Transfer Policies' marked as updated 21 Feb 2024, to be implemented by 21 Aug 2025, and lists no replacement. I did not check the GNSO transfer-policy pipeline, so 'not replaced' is not established.)

Missed by the analyst:
- The current ICANN RAA is the 21 Jan 2024 text (global amendment effective 5 Apr 2024). The analyst read the superseded 2013 RAA. New 3.18.2 imposes a DNS Abuse mitigation duty on the registrar that flows to resellers through 3.12. Expect this in ResellerClub's live reseller agreement.
- ICANN consensus policies binding resellers through Extension 2(3) were not enumerated. The Registration Data Policy is effective 21 Aug 2025 (updated 12 May 2026 for Urgent Requests). Others are the Registration Data Marketing Restriction Policy, Registration Data Reminder Policy, Expired Registration Recovery Policy and Transfer Policies (updated 21 Feb 2024).
- The registrant agreement (Massachusetts law, PDR arbitration) makes the end customer contract directly with PDR. It also lets the Company put default and expired pages with ads on the domain and keep the revenue, insert ad-serving wildcard DNS records when a customer uses the provider's DNS and configures no wildcard, run ED Transfers, and move a domain to another registrar without consent. The wildcard clause bears directly on the provider-hosted DNS feature and on a no-ads brand promise.
- Master 6(6): LogicBoxes may deny or cancel any order within 30 days of processing. Master 6(3): it may cease offering a product on 'reasonable notification'. Master 8(5)(1) and 8(6)(3): API access is suspended on a termination notice and Orders may be transferred. Master 6(8) covers taking ownership 'in case of Termination'. There is no exit or continuity covenant for customer domains.
- Sanctions restriction explicitly covers Sub-Resellers and end Customers (kb/answer/2489), so Mosshatch must screen customers at checkout. Violations allow termination without refund (Master 4(8), 6(12)).
- Master 17 (Publicity) requires prior written consent to reference LogicBoxes or its Service Providers. Master 24(6) forbids statements contradicting the Agreement. Master 20 forbids assignment without consent (relevant to an acquisition). Master 12(5) has a staff non-solicit.
- Master 4(5) requires Mosshatch's own end-customer terms to be 'no less protective of LogicBoxes'. This flows sole-discretion suspension, uncapped indemnity and liability limits down to consumers and may conflict with consumer-protection law. The reseller may customise customer agreements (faq335), but the Registrar Registrant Agreement must be shown 'as is'.
- Liability asymmetry: LogicBoxes' liability is capped at 3 months of payments (Master 10) while the reseller's indemnity (Master 11) is uncapped. Section 23 lets LogicBoxes enforce in the Contractee's home jurisdiction.
- Notice mechanics: Master 26(3) deems notices to the Contractee given when sent to the email in the OrderBox database, and 26(4) says LogicBoxes need not send other notices. 3(3) and 15(2) mean price, term and agreement changes arrive by email or Control Panel. A monitored legal/billing inbox is essential.
- The templates contain no DPA, GDPR or processor terms. LogicBoxes claims ownership of the OrderBox Database (13(1)) and broad disclosure rights (13(2)). Appendix A 2(3) restricts the reseller's use of the data (no mass mailing, no selling, use only for services and communicating about the Order), so Mosshatch's own marketing use of customer data is not clearly permitted.
- The Extension's TLD definitions and Appendix B omit .ai, .io, .dev and .app (they list only .studio and .com of the six). The template is likely stale, but contractual authorisation for those four TLDs is unverified.
- IANA also lists 1085 'Click Registrar, LLC dba publicdomainregistry.com' as a related accredited registrar. Appendix B maps TLD groups to different registrar entities, so the sponsoring registrar and applicable registrant agreement vary by TLD.
- Product sign-up (faq307): a reseller is automatically signed up for all products and can only set 'Do Not Sell' for a product with no existing orders (exit friction). There is no per-TLD approval step in the platform documentation.
- Payment mechanics with legal weight: exchange rate set by LogicBoxes and 'undisputable' (Appendix C 2(2)); card processing charge at LogicBoxes' discretion (2(3)); set-off rights (1(7)); error corrections at any time (1(8)); Business Day uses Indian public holidays (Master 1(4)).
- PublicDomainRegistry.com (the sponsoring registrar's own site and legal pages, including its arbitration agreement) is also behind the Cloudflare challenge and could not be read.

### Verifier lens: tech (confidence medium; 20 claims confirmed)

I re-fetched about 75 pages of the LogicBoxes demo-registrar KB (demo.myorderbox.com), the WHMCS ResellerClub module docs, the FOSSBilling adapter, the Vercel Static IPs doc, ICANN, IANA and RDAP. Almost every documented technical claim in the dossier held up against the primary page, including the 13-method coverage rows, the sandbox/Test URL, the unpublished rate limits with a 24-hour IP block, and the 3-IP allow-list. Three small corrections: the renew-on-locked-order error string is not in the renew doc, reseller lock also blocks transfer-away at the registry, and billing/reseller-balance.json is documented only for sub-resellers. Pricing is a total gap. I could not obtain any ResellerClub price, so none of the .com/.ai/.io/.dev/.app/.studio register or renew figures can be checked. ResellerClub's own pricing pages are Cloudflare-challenged, and Chromium cannot pass the challenge because the egress policy rejects brunhild.challenges.cloudflare.com. The vendor KB shows only 0.0 placeholders. Third-party aggregators are also blocked, and WebSearch budget is exhausted. Runtime behaviour (duplicate register, demo simulation depth) also needs a live demo call, which is impossible from here (test.httpapi.com is blocked and there are no credentials). The dossier discloses these gaps honestly, so it is reliable on what it claims but does not settle economics.

Not confirmed:
- **corrected**: renew of a locked/suspended order fails with 'Domain Status does not allow this operation.' (cited to answer/746) -> Error string is documented for theft-protection toggle and delete on registry-locked/suspended domains, not for renew; renew-on-locked behaviour is undocumented. (The string is absent from answer/746. It appears only in faq457 (theft protection/lock toggling) and faq1451 (delete), described as the error when the domain is locked/suspended at the Registry. No fetched page documents renew behaviour on a locked order.)
- **corrected**: setLock: theft protection enable/disable, locks.json, reseller-lock add/remove; optional otp + 2fa-type=email; theft protection auto-enabled after inbound transfer; reseller lock is a separate provider-side hold -> Both theft protection and reseller lock block transfer-away at the registry; lock is reseller-only to remove. (Endpoints, otp/2fa params and auto-enable on transfer-in (faq553) are correct. But faq457 says a reseller Lock also causes a transfer-away request to 'fail at the Registry itself' (except .AU and .RU), is unsupported for .DE .ES .EU .NL .NZ .UK .WEB.IN, and can be removed only by the locking reseller or the provider, never the customer.)
- **unverifiable**: ResellerClub register and renew prices for .com, .ai, .io (and .dev, .app, .studio) are unverified (I could not obtain any of the requested price cells (12 minimum: .com/.ai/.io register and renew and more). All sources were blocked or placeholder-only (ResellerClub pages, tld-list.com and domcomp.com Cloudflare 403, Wayback returns only a 2014 pointer, Verisign price URL redirected to homepage, WebSearch budget exhausted). Correct basis for a real figure is reseller-cost-price.json (or panel 'Your Cost Price' row per WHMCS docs) after signup, not a public retail page.)

(answer/1110 documents it as 'Getting a Sub-Reseller's Available Balance' with a required reseller-id of the Sub-Reseller. The KB has no documented endpoint for the reseller's own available or locked balance (only customer-balance.json). resellers/details.json exposes totalreceipts and fundthreshold but no balance. Analyst listed own-id use as unverified; documentation says sub-resellers only.)

Missed by the analyst:
- No documented API to read our own Advance Account available/locked balance: billing/reseller-balance.json (answer/1110) requires a Sub-Reseller's reseller-id, and resellers/details.json (answer/809) has only totalreceipts and fundthreshold. Funding-health monitoring would have to rely on the low-funds email or on catching 'Order Locked in Processing' actions.
- resellers/details.json (https://demo.myorderbox.com/kb/answer/809) documents Password and Personal Identification Number (pin) in its response field list; treat that response as secret and never log or persist it.
- 'Order Locked in Processing' is not only insufficient funds: faq1270 also lists 'Connection to the concerned Registry could not be established', and the action then completes asynchronously. A timed-out or failed-upstream register can still finish later, which strengthens the no-blind-resend rule and means a reconcile job is mandatory.
- Currency and payment basis for wholesale cost: Advance Account is held in US cents and INR, payments are converted at a rate 'determined by LogicBoxes ... undisputable', a card processing charge is deducted, only cheque/wire/demand draft/card are accepted, and Selling/Accounting currency is chosen at sign-up and changeable only before the first transaction (Master Agreement Appendix C 2; faq404).
- Premium names are strictly zero-margin: the reseller, sub-reseller and customer pay the same price and the reseller cannot set a selling price (answer/1953). Any Mosshatch fee model must account for this.
- Vercel Static IPs give one IP pair per region; ResellerClub's self-serve cap is 3 single IPs, so a second region (4 IPs) needs a support request. Static IPs also do not cover middleware/edge, so all registrar calls must run in a pinned-region serverless function.
- With invoice-option=NoInvoice the register/renew/transfer responses omit invoiceid and sellingamount, so per-order wholesale cost cannot be read from the response and must come from the cached cost-price table or transactions search.
- reseller-cost-price.json returns every product family ('huge number of products') and the KB warns of longer wait times; fetch and cache it in a background job (Neon), never inline in a request path.
- DNS service can be activated only for orders whose Customer sits immediately under the reseller (faq580), and the KB says add-record duplicate behaviour is not documented; combine with the 'many customers with no orders' abuse rule when designing one-customer-per-user.
- Transfer-in doc (answer/758) has a stale 'auth code required' TLD list that omits .AI/.IO/.DEV/.APP and a stale tnc list; always send the auth code and tnc=y for our TLDs rather than trusting those lists.
- Registry-continuity risk for .io was not researched by the analyst or by me; IANA record is unchanged since 2023-01-18 (manager Internet Computer Bureau Limited), and .io is mentioned in no vendor KB page, so whether ResellerClub sells .io through the API at all remains open.

### Verifier lens: skeptic (confidence high; 20 claims confirmed)

Cloudflare, Vercel and Hover are not viable as reseller sources today. I found nothing after 2026-09-16 that changes the conclusions. Corrections are second-order:
- AWS .io ($71), .dev and .app were not repriced on 2026-07-01. Only .com (15 to 16), .ai (129 to 137) and .studio (13 to 44) changed.
- AWS .ai requires a 2-year minimum registration ($274 up front at $137/yr) and supports no privacy protection. The dossier missed both.
- Internet.bs test-host error 107015 is not specific to .dev, .app and .studio. It also hits .xyz, .tech, .biz, .club, .online, .shop and .me, while .net, .org, .co, .info, .us and .uk work. That points to a broad test-host limitation, not TLD-specific lack of support.
- "Lowest sampled prices" for Internet.bs holds only for .ai, .dev, .io and .studio. GoDaddy's reseller wholesale ($10.69) and Vercel retail ($11.25) beat its .com price ($11.30), and GoDaddy's promo .app price ($9.49) beats its $14.78.
Still unresolved and load-bearing:
- GoDaddy API Terms and Reseller Agreement (Akamai 403 again in curl and Chromium).
- Squarespace and Internet.bs terms.
- Spaceship and Epik entirely (Cloudflare challenges, plus an egress block for the Epik docs host).

The pricing PDF's Updated table changes only com (15 to 16), ai (129 to 137) and studio (13 to 44). .dev $17, .app $20 and .io $71 are unchanged legacy prices, so '.io $71 since 2026-07-01' is wrong for .io. .studio rose 3.4x.)

Missed by the analyst:
- AWS .ai has a 2-year minimum registration ('Registration: Two to ten years. Renewal: Two to nine years.') and 'Privacy protection: Not supported', so the $137/yr figure means at least $274 up front (docs.aws.amazon.com/Route53/latest/DeveloperGuide/ai.html). The dossier omitted both facts.
- AWS agreement risk terms beyond 3.12: 3.12.8 (reseller accepts liability for harm from the customer's use of the name), 2.4 (indemnity to AWS, the registrar and the registry), 2.2.2 (AWS may reject any registration), 3.10.5 (you stay bound while any domain remains in your account, even if RDAP names a third party) and 3.1.1 (a third-party WhoisProxy.com proxy is switched on automatically 'except where prohibited'). AWS Service Terms 13.1 bars creating a hosted zone for a domain you lack authority over. The registrar of record is Amazon Registrar, Inc. or other accredited registrars (2.1).
- AWS hosted-zone quota is 500 per account by default. Each RegisterDomain creates a hosted zone at $0.50/month for the first 25 and $0.10 after (deleted within 12 h is free), so per-domain DNS cost and a second quota raise matter at scale.
- AWS .io ($71), .dev ($17) and .app ($20) did not change on 2026-07-01; only .com, .ai and .studio did. .studio went from $13 to $44, which makes AWS a poor .studio source.
- GoDaddy v3 spec has no renew, transfer or contact-update paths; those still need v1/v2, whose sso-key auth is 'scheduled for deprecation in 2026' (developer.godaddy.com/en/docs/api-users/domains). Standard .com renewal on the public v3 table is $22.99 versus $10.69 reseller wholesale, so only the (unread-terms) Reseller plan has usable economics.
- Cloudflare blog post dated April 30, 2026 (blog.cloudflare.com/agents-stripe-projects/): a protocol co-designed with Stripe Projects lets agents or platforms provision a Cloudflare account and register a domain for a signed-in user. The end user accepts Cloudflare's terms, so this is account provisioning, not resale, but it is a competing distribution path.
- Cloudflare DRA 1.3 makes the account holder the holder of record and liable for third-party licensees. Even if a reseller product ships, the current model makes Mosshatch the registrant of record, not the customer.
- eNom: Points are non-refundable for any reason, a 7-year records-retention duty applies, eNom may assume direct control of your customers if you become unavailable, and the API test environment needs an active (paid-enrollment) reseller account. The sale prices on .dev, .app and .io have no stated term, and the .ai term basis is unstated.
- Margin reality check: eNom Essential-tier wholesale for .io ($60) exceeds Vercel's public retail renewal ($46) and Internet.bs's list price ($49.98). eNom .studio ($42) exceeds Vercel retail ($21.99 first year, $36 renewal). Only the Enterprise tier ($100k/yr spend) gets close, so launch-tier margins on .io and .studio would be thin or negative at retail parity.
- Squarespace bills the reseller monthly in arrears at the negotiated wholesale rate, and renewals return to the reseller's billing: credit exposure and reporting duties. The '(2024)' program launch year is not evidenced.
- Internet.bs test-host error 107015 is not specific to .dev, .app and .studio. It also affects .xyz, .tech, .biz, .club, .online, .shop and .me, while .net, .org, .co, .info, .us and .uk work.
- Not checkable this session (WebSearch budget exhausted; sites blocked): news after 2026-09-16 on Cloudflare registrar-as-a-service, the GoDaddy API and Reseller agreement text, Squarespace and Internet.bs terms, Spaceship and Epik, ICANN's per-transaction gTLD fee, and the .io ccTLD registry status (IANA record last updated 2023-01-18).

## registrar-data-obligations
Dossier: `registrar-data-obligations.md`

### Verifier lens: skeptic (confidence high; 23 claims confirmed)

I re-fetched the primary ICANN sources and the five assigned claims hold: the 15-day verification rule, the RAA 3.12 flow-down list, the Registration Data Policy (RDP) effective date, the WHOIS sunset date, and the April 2024 DNS Abuse amendment scope. No RAA global amendment exists after 5 Apr 2024 as of 2026-09-29. I found three corrections: retention framing, Transfer Policy status, and a few wording points. The main gaps are 2026 developments in DNS Abuse policy that target API-driven bulk registration, RAA 3.20 breach notice, and the DNSSEC/IPv6/IDN specification. WebSearch was unavailable (shared budget spent), so the 2025-2026 sweep paged ICANN's own announcements, Board resolutions and the Sept 2026 policy scorecard. Claims outside the ICANN lens (GDPR, UK GDPR, EDPB, ICO, .ai/.io registry terms, OFAC program list) were mostly not re-checked. The exceptions were NIS2, CCPA, OFAC eCFR figures, the DPF appeal status and IANA records, which I confirmed.

Not confirmed:
- **corrected**: RAA 3.7.7.1-3.7.7.2: registrant updates within 7 days and answers accuracy inquiries within 15 days; failure is a material breach and basis for suspension (dossier B08). -> Material breach arises from WILLFUL provision of inaccurate information, WILLFUL failure to update within 7 days, or failure to respond for over 15 days to accuracy inquiries.
