Only the 15-day non-response limb is unqualified. (Dossier F07 quote is right but B08 drops the 'willful' qualifiers.)
- **corrected**: RDP s12 / FAQ: TDRP-necessary data kept 15 months; other RAA retention (sponsorship + 2 years, records for Term + 2 years) stays; relay logs 180 days; no duty beyond 2 years after deletion or transfer-out. -> ICANN FAQ Q7 and Q9 say the RDP reduces retention from two years to 15 months for the defined RDP registration data set (sponsorship + 15 months); the dossier still treats registrant/tech contact data as sponsorship + 2 years. RAA 3.4.2 records (submissions, registration contracts and correspondence, account records) are kept 'During the Term of this Agreement and for two (2) years thereafter', where Term is the RAA term, so there is no per-domain 2-year cap. 180-day log retention is confirmed. (RDP s12 and Implementation Note F confirmed. The dossier's design implication 4 and checklist A09/B15 retention timers should be redone by data class.)
- **corrected**: Transfer Policy: ICANN's in-force text still has 60-day locks; the reformed policy (TAC, 30-day locks) is reported only by secondary sources and unverified. -> In-force page still shows the Feb 2024 policy with 60-day locks, but the reform is primary-sourced: ICANN Board Resolution 2026.06.07.04 (7 Jun 2026) adopted all 47 Transfer Policy Review recommendations 'subject to prioritization'; the Sept 2026 scorecard lists Transfer Policy implementation start as TBC. Not yet effective. (Board resolution page and Chair's blog 24 Jun 2026 fetched. A sibling dossier in the same folder (icann-transfer-policy.md) already records this; registrar-data-obligations.md is inconsistent with it. A separate Change of Registrant Data (CORD) Policy is also planned.)
- **unverifiable**: GDPR/UK GDPR/EDPB/ICO/SCC statements, .ai registry FAQ and .io terms/dispute policy, OFAC program list, Chagos ISO 3166 status, Stripe/Neon/Vercel/Resend DPA assumptions. (Outside the ICANN skeptic lens and not re-fetched by me, so I cannot mark them confirmed. Treat them as analyst-reported.)

Missed by the analyst:
- DNS Abuse Mitigation PDP 1 Initial Report (GNSO, 18 Aug 2026; comment closed 28 Sep 2026): eight preliminary recommendations would require registrars to run Associated Domain Checks on actionable DNS Abuse evidence; Implementation Guidance 3.1 lets a registrar rely on a Reseller for investigation steps but keeps the registrar fully responsible under RAA 3.12. Board delivery expected Jul 2027.
- DNS Abuse PDP 2 (initiated Dec 2025, not yet chartered): 'checks to establish trust for new registrants prior to providing access to high-volume registration tools'. The Final Issue Report (4 Dec 2025) names unrestricted API access for new customers as a top gap, citing INFERMAL (API access linked to a 401% rise in malicious registrations). This targets Mosshatch's agent/API model and supports the dossier's velocity-limit idea.
- ICANN Advisory on Registrars' Use of Communication Web Forms in RDDS (25 May 2026; announcement 26 May 2026): reads RDP s9.2.3 as allowing a contact form with pre-populated reasons and no free text.
- Board adopted all 47 Transfer Policy Review recommendations on 7 Jun 2026 (Res. 2026.06.07.04), including Rec 21 (registrar MAY deny a transfer for a name identified as a DNS Abuse source) and a separate CORD Policy; implementation start TBC, nothing effective yet. The dossier calls this unverified.
- RAA 3.20: registrar must notify ICANN within 7 days of any unauthorized access to or disclosure of registrant account information or registration data. Not in the dossier; a reseller holding registrant data needs a breach-notice SLA to the upstream shorter than this (the dossier only covers GDPR's 72 hours).
- RAA 3.19 Additional Registrar Operations Specification: registrar must let customers use DNSSEC by relaying DS/DNSKEY add/remove/change orders to registries via EPP (RFC 5910) and show DNSSEC status in RDAP; must accept IPv6 nameserver addresses where glue is offered; IDN rules. A reseller storefront/API needs DS management; the dossier checklist omits it.
- Retention: RAA 3.4.2 'Term of this Agreement + 2 years' is a whole-agreement clock, and the RDP FAQ (Q7, Q9) says 15 months replaces two years for the defined RDP data set. The dossier's per-domain retention timers need redoing.
- Logo License Specification / RAA 2.2: the registrar may not sublicense ICANN's trademarks to any Reseller without ICANN's written approval and must notify ICANN of suspected trademark infringement by resellers. This is the basis of 3.12.1.
- ICANN accuracy advisory (16 Nov 2015): registrars may delegate validation and verification to resellers but stay ultimately responsible; renewal alone does not trigger re-verification; a qualifying transfer FOA can satisfy verification; format-only complaints need correction but not re-verification.
- ICANN Compliance enforcement of the DNS Abuse rules is active: 192 investigations in the first six months (5 Apr to 5 Oct 2024), breach notices to one registry and one registrar, 154 cases resolved, 2,700+ names suspended (8 Nov 2024 report); ICANN published a complaint how-to guide on 20 Nov 2025.
- Privacy/proxy pipeline: the PPSAI program is now projected for Q3 2027 (Sept 2026 scorecard); if Mosshatch or an operator ever registers names as Registered Name Holder on behalf of a customer, that arrangement is a 'Proxy Service' under the P/P Specification definitions.
- RDRS was extended by the Board (30 Oct 2025) to Dec 2027 and remains voluntary for registrars; SSAD Supplemental Recommendations are due for public comment about Oct 2026 with Board delivery about Mar 2027. Future disclosure duties may change, but nothing is binding for Phase 0.
- The 2026 Base Registry Agreement (Board 12 Mar 2026) covers only the 2026 new-gTLD round and does not change the RAA or the .com/.dev/.app/.studio agreements.

## stripe-fees
Dossier: `stripe-fees.md`

### Verifier lens: skeptic (confidence high; 19 claims confirmed)

Nearly all numbers in the dossier hold. I re-fetched stripe.com/pricing, the local-payment-methods page, the refund and dispute docs, Stripe's monitoring-programs page, Visa's VAMP fact sheet (PDF text extracted), the restricted-businesses list and the service terms on 2026-09-29. Every rate, fee and threshold matches. I recomputed all five order sizes with Decimal half-up and every table cell matches. I found three corrections and a few misses. (1) The derived "$40.59 total cash out" for a lost countered $10 dispute double-counts the 59c original fee. The dispute-time debit is $40.00 and the net loss versus no sale is $30.59. (2) "Network programs unreachable at launch" is true only for the Excessive tiers. Stripe documents a Visa Non-compliant tier at 5 events and 0.5% where Visa "may assess fees", and that tier is reachable at launch volume. A transaction that appears in both TC40 and TC15 is also counted twice. (3) "Never use the Tax API" is wrong above a $100 order, because Tax Basic's 0.5% is charged on the tax-inclusive total and the API is a flat $0.50. Beyond those: the analyst's "secondary-only" Radar Plus $0.07 and Pro $0.09 rates are confirmed on Stripe's own page, and the Tax-Basic-on-tax-inclusive-total question is resolved. Float arithmetic gives $0.73 instead of $0.74 at $15, so Mosshatch must use integer cents. Restricted-businesses conclusion confirmed (page updated 2026-09-22; no domain, registrar, hosting or DNS entry; the no-value-added, undisclosed-products, payment-facilitation, stored-value and chargeback-evasion lines exist as quoted). No public 2026 change to the 2.9% + 30c / 1.5% / 1% structure or the $15/$15 dispute fees was found. The Visa $8 event fee, Mastercard's own rulebook numbers and the Radar migration-trial date remain unverifiable at a primary source. WebSearch was unavailable (session budget exhausted), so the 2026-changes check relied on Stripe's own pages only.

Not confirmed:
- **corrected**: A lost, countered dispute on a $10 order costs $30.59 in Stripe fees plus the $10 reversed; table column 'Total cash out incl. reversed P' = $40.59 / $45.74 / $56.03 / $92.04 / $184.65 -> Dispute-time debit $40.00 at $10 (P+$30); net loss vs no sale $30.59 (fee+$30) plus any unrecovered wholesale cost ($30.59 (0.59 fee + 15 + 15) is right as the net loss versus no sale. But the sale already netted $9.41, so the 'plus the $10 reversed' total double-counts the original fee: the balance debit at dispute time is P + 30 = $40.00 / $45.00 / $55.00 / $90.00 / $180.00, not the $40.59...$184.65 column. Wholesale cost unrecovered from the registrar is additional.)
- **corrected**: Design guidance: use Checkout automatic tax and never the Tax API (until a subscription plan is justified) -> No-code cheaper below $100 tax-inclusive; API cheaper above $100 (only in jurisdictions where registered) (Break-even between 0.5% and $0.50 is a $100 tax-inclusive order. At $150 the API costs $0.50 vs $0.75 (dossier's own table: $5.20 vs $5.45). The 5%-of-a-$10-order point holds for small carts. Also API Transaction calls are separate from payments and need extra integration work.)
- **corrected**: Visa and Mastercard network programs are both unreachable at launch volume, so Stripe's own risk review is the real constraint -> Excessive tiers unreachable at launch; the Non-compliant tier (5 events and 0.5%, Stripe-documented) is reachable and may carry Visa fees (True for the Excessive tiers (Visa 1,500 events; Mastercard ECM 100 chargebacks). But Stripe's monitoring-programs page also lists a Visa 'Non-compliant' tier at VAMP count 5 and ratio 0.5%, and says Visa 'may assess fees to merchants that exceed the Non-Compliant threshold'. Five disputes/EFWs on ~1,000 Visa payments in a month reaches it. Stripe also states that a transaction appearing in both TC40 and TC15 is counted twice, and that a spike can trigger monitoring before 0.75%. Practical alarm level is 0.5%.)
- **unverifiable**: Visa VAMP merchant fee is $8 per fraudulent or disputed transaction with a 3-month grace period for first violation (MRC ('assessed a fee of $8 per fraudulent or disputed transaction'; 'three-month grace period') and Chargeflow ('~$8') both say it, but Visa's fact sheet states no fee amounts and I found no Visa or Stripe document with the figure. Secondary only.)
- **corrected**: Paddle, Lemon Squeezy and Polar are 5% + 50c (Polar Pro 3.8% + 40c) and cost roughly 1.5-2x direct Stripe; none confirms domain names -> MoR premium ~1.3x (Polar Pro) to ~2.1x (Managed Payments at $150) (Fee schedules confirmed on Paddle, Lemon Squeezy and Polar pages (Polar Early Member 4% + 40c; +1.5% international at LS and Polar). Multiples: Managed Payments 1.6-2.1x, 5%+50c about 1.7x, but Polar Pro (3.8% + 40c, excluding $20/month) is only about 1.3x. Domain names are not addressed by any of them; LS bars 'Services of any kind' and Polar bars 'Marketplaces. Selling others' products or services', both risk signals for a reseller.)
- **unverifiable**: Published dispute rates for digital goods (0.54% Chargeflow, 0.6-1.2% and 3.62% PayCompass, 0.26% Sift) are inconsistent vendor figures (All four figures reproduce on the cited pages as quoted, and none names an underlying dataset for digital goods; the real-world base rate cannot be verified from a primary source.)

Missed by the analyst:
- Implementation trap: binary floating point gives 15*0.029+0.30 = 0.7349999 and round(...,2) = 0.73 (Python), while Stripe and Excel give $0.74. Mosshatch code and margin checks must use integer cents or Decimal, not floats.
- Visa counts a transaction twice when it appears in both the TC40 fraud report and the TC15 dispute report (Stripe monitoring-programs docs). An EFW that later becomes a dispute therefore counts double in the VAMP numerator.
- Stripe's documented Visa Non-compliant tier (5 events, 0.5%) is reachable at launch volume and Visa 'may assess fees' there; the dossier only discusses the Excessive tier.
- A refund processed as a reversal (usually within about 2 hours of capture) is the only refund path that can prevent an early-fraud-warning report; the dossier presents only authorization-cancel (no report) versus captured-then-refunded (reported). Stripe's EFW cut-off is charges 'roughly less than or equal to your dispute fee', not worthwhile above 35% over it (about $20), slightly looser than the dossier's 'at or below roughly $15'.
- Payment-method mix changes cost: with dynamic payment methods, any method enabled in the Dashboard can appear on Checkout. Klarna is 5.99% + 30c, Affirm 6% + 30c, Afterpay 6% + 30c, versus 2.9% + 30c for cards, Apple Pay, Google Pay, Cash App Pay and Amazon Pay. Setting capture_method=manual also filters out methods that cannot hold (ACH, iDEAL). Disable BNPL or exclude it per session if the fee model assumes cards only.
- Tax Basic's 0.5% is charged on the tax-inclusive total (Stripe support page), and the API alternative is $0.50 per Transaction-API call plus $0.05 per Calculation call beyond 10. Break-even is a $100 order, so the API is cheaper on the $150 order size.
- Stripe's Visa/Mastercard fine pass-through: service terms 2.5 makes the user responsible for all Assessed Fines; the dossier cites this but does not connect it to the Non-compliant tier or to reserve sizing.
- Opt-in dispute resolution/deflection (Visa Verifi/Ethoca via Radar rules) removes disputes from the dispute rate and VAMP count, but deflection lookups cost $15 per Visa resolution and $29 per Mastercard resolution; at $10-$25 order sizes that is about the same as the $15 received fee, so its benefit is VAMP hygiene rather than cash. Sign-up is in Dispute settings, not automatic.
- Polar's dispute fee is $15 regardless of outcome and LS/Polar percentage fees apply to the VAT-inclusive total, so MoR comparisons at low order values are slightly worse than the pre-tax table shows.
- Stripe's pricing page top summary tile shows stablecoins at 1.5% while the Link/payment-method section shows 0.8% promotional through 2027-01-01 then +0.2%: internal inconsistency on Stripe's page, irrelevant to Mosshatch cards but a reminder that page snapshots should be dated.
- WebSearch was unavailable to this verifier (session budget of 200 consumed), so the 2026-fee-change check could not use news or community sources; only Stripe's own pages were scanned.

## stripe-order-flow
Dossier: `stripe-order-flow.md`

### Verifier lens: skeptic (confidence medium; 16 claims confirmed)

The Stripe facts in the dossier hold up. I re-fetched the primary sources on 2026-09-29 and every load-bearing quote matched: manual capture on Checkout, the 7-day CIT and 4d18h Visa MIT windows, the 7-day auto-cancel, webhook retries, no ordering guarantee, off-session SCA behaviour, and cancel-is-free versus refund-never-returns-fee. The recommended order state machine has real holes. Six framing points need correcting. Confidence is medium because the facts are high-confidence but the machine's holes (section C of the notes) affect money and registrations. Nothing was tested against a live or sandbox Stripe account.

Six corrections to the dossier:
- "Never from the redirect" is stricter than Stripe. Stripe requires webhooks and also recommends a landing-page trigger through the same idempotent function.
- Vercel's `request.body` is a lazy getter, so it only bites when accessed. Using `request.text()` is still the safe choice.
- Stripe's own pages disagree on the webhook resend horizon: 15 days versus 30 days for the CLI.
- Extended authorization is an IC+ feature, but blended-pricing users can request it through Stripe support.
- The Checkout Session `payment_status` enum definition ("paid" means funds are available) implies "unpaid" for an authorized-but-uncaptured Session. That is only an inference; the actual value is untested.
- The Session object doc says you cannot cancel a Checkout PaymentIntent (expire the Session instead), while the PaymentIntent cancel doc allows it in `requires_capture`. The dossier cites only the second.

Holes in the state machine, worst first:
- **Capture retry after a 5xx:** the dossier says to retry capture with the same idempotency key. Stripe caches 500s and replays them for that key, and treats a 500 as indeterminate. The right move is to GET the PaymentIntent, then retry with a new key.
- **Two payable Sessions or PaymentIntents for one order:** nothing prevents this, and the guard checks only `order_id`, which is the same across attempts.
- **Approved-amount guard versus Checkout tax:** Checkout computes tax from an address typed after passkey approval. An `amount_capturable == total_minor` guard can therefore cancel legitimate authorizations.
- **`capture_before` availability:** it is nullable for cards and absent from `payment_method_details.link`, so the 24-hour guard cannot run for Link.
- **Unexpected capture:** a `payment_intent.succeeded` before registration has no transition.
- **Landing page:** no landing-page step is modelled.
- **Duplicate-event handling:** `(object id, type)` deduplication would drop legitimate repeat events.
- **Idempotency parameter drift:** a computed `expires_at` under a fixed Session key breaks retries.
- **Late Radar review:** a review can open after authorization.

The remaining items (cancel-versus-refund events, saved card after cancel, explicit payment-method set) are in section C of the notes.

Not confirmed:
- **corrected**: F7: Card-not-present authorization windows: Visa MIT 5 days (4d18h) / CIT 7; Mastercard, Amex, Discover 7/7; read capture_before -> capture_before is nullable for cards and not exposed for Link; use a fallback deadline (e.g. authorization time + 4 days) or exclude Link (Windows confirmed verbatim. Correction: payment_method_details.card.capture_before is typed nullable, and the payment_method_details.link hash contains only country and funding_source_group, so there is no capture_before for Link-type payments and no documented Link window. The dossier's AUTHORIZED transition and 24h guard need a fallback deadline or Link excluded.)
- **corrected**: F11: Cancelling a requires_capture PaymentIntent releases the hold; a Checkout PI can be cancelled directly only in requires_capture -> Documented with a Stripe-internal contradiction; sandbox-verify PI cancel on a completed manual-capture Session (The PaymentIntent cancel doc says exactly this. But the Checkout Session object doc says 'You can't confirm or cancel the PaymentIntent for a Checkout Session. To cancel, expire the Checkout Session instead', and Session expire works only on open Sessions. The dossier does not record this tension; confirm the cancel exception in the sandbox spike.)
- **corrected**: F8: Extended authorization (up to 30 days) is IC+ only, CIT only, +0.08% Visa other categories, not for Link-type, mainly for unknown final amounts -> IC+ feature; blended-pricing users can request access via Stripe support (All constraints confirmed except 'IC+ only': the page says it is offered to IC+ users and blended-pricing users can contact Stripe support to get access. The recommendation not to use it stands.)
- **corrected**: F1/F2/F3 and TL;DR 1: Fulfil from webhooks, never from the redirect; trigger events are checkout.session.completed (+ async_payment_succeeded) -> Webhooks are the guarantee; the landing page should call the same idempotent reconcile after checking the session belongs to the signed-in user (Quotes confirmed verbatim. But Stripe's fulfilment guide requires webhooks AND recommends triggering fulfilment from the landing page ('Trigger fulfillment on your landing page [Recommended]', 'webhooks can sometimes be delayed') via the same idempotent function. 'Never from the redirect' overstates it. The dossier's state machine has no landing-page step at all.)
- **unverifiable**: U1/F3: Checkout Session payment_status on checkout.session.completed for a manual-capture Session is undocumented (The actual value cannot be confirmed without a sandbox. However the object doc defines paid as 'The payment funds are available in your account' and unpaid as 'not yet available', which implies unpaid for an authorized-only Session, so Stripe's sample (fulfil if payment_status != 'unpaid') would not fulfil. The dossier's choice to gate on PI requires_capture is right; U1 is slightly understated as 'undocumented'.)
- **corrected**: F25: Dashboard resend 15 days, CLI resend 30 days, List Events last 30 days -> Resend reliably 15 days (Stripe pages disagree on 30); List Events 30 days (webhooks.md says CLI resend works up to 30 days, but event-destinations.md says events 16-30 days old cannot be resent or have delivery attempts viewed. List Events 30 days confirmed. Plan reconciliation around 15 days for resends.)
- **corrected**: F21: On Vercel, Node-style /api handlers get a pre-parsed request.body; use the Web-standard handler and request.text() -> request.body is a lazy getter; hazard only if accessed (Vercel docs: request.body is populated with a parsed version 'when possible' but is a JavaScript getter 'only computed when it is accessed'. Pre-parsing only hurts if you touch request.body on JSON. The Web-standard request.text() recommendation is still the safest.)
- **corrected**: F48: Idempotency semantics (first result incl. 500 replayed, ~24h retention, parameters compared) and the dossier's use of retry-with-same-key for transient 5xx in CAPTURE_FAILED -> After a 500, GET the PI then use a new key; same key only for network errors, 409 and 429 (F48 text confirmed. But dossier 11.2 says retry capture with the same key on 5xx for up to 6 h, which contradicts Stripe: 500s are cached and replayed for that key, and a 500 is indeterminate; retry the same key only for no-response network errors, 409 and 429. After a 500, GET the PaymentIntent and re-capture with a new key if still requires_capture.)
- **corrected**: F37/F42: Checkout tax is computed from the address in the Session; Google Pay with Stripe Tax needs a shipping address; and design implication 'authorized amount must equal approved amount' -> Bind approval to subtotal plus a tax ceiling (or collect address before approval); do not require exact equality with a pre-Checkout estimate (F37/F42 confirmed (tax-checkout: Checkout assesses tax from the billing/shipping address the customer provides in the Session; Google Pay needs a collected or saved shipping address). But that means the final amount is not known at passkey approval, so the equality guard amount_capturable == total_minor can wrongly cancel legitimate authorizations or the approval no longer binds the charged amount. Needs address-first flow, a bounded tax ceiling, or re-approval.)
- **corrected**: Dossier state machine safety under duplicate/out-of-order webhooks and capture-after-registrar-failure (section 11) -> Add the transitions and guards listed in section C of the verification notes before implementation (Conditional single-statement UPDATEs plus re-fetch handle plain duplicates and reordering, but the machine has these gaps: (1) capture retry policy after 5xx; (2) two Sessions/PIs per order not prevented (guard checks only order_id); (3) approved-amount equality vs Checkout tax; (4) capture_before missing for Link and nullable for cards; (5) no transition for PI succeeded before registration; (6) no landing-page/reconcile step; (7) (object id,type) used as a drop rule; (8) Session create key with computed expires_at; (9) Radar review not re-checked before capture; (10) charge.refunded on cancel unknown; (11) saved card remains after cancelled order; (12) payment methods not pinned in the Session.)

Missed by the analyst:
- Landing/success_url handling: Stripe's fulfilment guide recommends also triggering fulfilment from the landing page. The machine needs a step that authorises the session_id against the signed-in user's order, calls the same idempotent reconcile and polls, instead of waiting for webhook or the 5-minute cron.
- Capture 5xx handling contradicts Stripe's rule that 500s are cached and replayed under the same idempotency key and are indeterminate; after a 500, GET the PaymentIntent, then capture with a new key if still requires_capture.
- Two payable Sessions/PIs per order: attempt N+1 created while attempt N is open, or two tabs. AUTHORIZED guard must match session/PI id and attempt to the order's current attempt, confirm the old Session is expired (expire only works on open Sessions and errors if the customer just paid), and cancel any extra PI.
- Approved amount vs Checkout tax: tax is computed inside Checkout from an address entered after passkey approval, so exact equality of amount_capturable with the pre-approval total is unsafe; use address-first collection or a subtotal-plus-tax-ceiling rule with re-approval.
- capture_before is nullable on cards and absent for Link (payment_method_details.link has only country and funding_source_group; no documented Link window); define a fallback deadline or exclude Link at launch.
- Unexpected capture (payment_intent.succeeded while order is AUTHORIZED/REGISTERING, e.g. Dashboard capture, misconfigured method, ACH slipping in, or Stripe's private-preview automatic_delayed capture) has no transition; add a PAID_BEFORE_REGISTRATION register-or-refund path and re-fetch the PI before any cancel call.
- Pin the Session's payment methods explicitly (payment_method_types or allowed_payment_method_types plus payment_method_options[card|link][capture_method]=manual) so Dashboard drift cannot add ACH or other auto-capture delayed methods; behaviour of unsupported methods under payment_intent_data.capture_method=manual on Dahlia is undocumented.
- Session-object doc says a Checkout PaymentIntent cannot be cancelled (expire the Session instead) while the PI cancel doc allows it in requires_capture; sandbox-verify before relying on the CANCELING step.
- (object id, type) dedupe as a drop rule would discard legitimate repeats (payment_failed per attempt, refund.updated, partial charge.refunded, dispute.updated); use it only to coalesce, and set processed_at only after the transition commits.
- Idempotency key/parameter drift: Session create uses a fixed key but computes expires_at=now+N per call; persist computed parameters (expires_at, amount_to_capture) on the order before the first call.
- Re-check PI.review immediately before capture; a Radar review can open after AUTHORIZED and capture is a separate action from review approval.
- Cancel wording: docs say cancel releases the authorization without a Refund object, but cancel API and capture_before docs say 'automatically refunded'; charge.refunded behaviour on cancel/expiry is undocumented, so refund handlers must be state-guarded (only from PAID).
- setup_future_usage attaches the PaymentMethod after confirmation (i.e. at authorization), so a cancelled order can leave a saved card on the Customer; detach on cancel unless separately consented.
- error_on_requires_action=true on off-session renewal PaymentIntents to avoid leaving dangling requires_action intents.
- TLDs with pending/asynchronous registration beyond the hold window cannot use authorize-register-capture; a defined capture-then-refund fallback (fee lost) is needed.
- Fee/records of Stripe Tax (0.5% or $0.50 where registered) and Radar on authorizations that are later cancelled or partially captured are unverified (U6).
- Possible card-network penalties or scrutiny for a high ratio of cancelled authorizations are not addressed by any Stripe page; track the auth-cancel ratio.
- Stripe docs disagree on webhook resend horizon (15 days vs 30 days for CLI); design reconciliation around 15 days for resends and 30 days for List Events.

## tax-vat
Dossier: `tax-vat.md`

### Verifier lens: skeptic (confidence medium; 31 claims confirmed)

I re-fetched the primary sources myself. Stripe Tax code and pricing claims, the Merchant-of-Record fees and prohibited lists, the EU OSS mechanics, the VIES API, and the UK, Norway, Canada, Japan and Switzerland thresholds all held up. Four things are wrong or overstated. (1) "Or 200 transactions" is stale for North Carolina and Maine. The current statutes have a single $100,000 test, and Stripe's own pages are out of date. (2) Kansas is misclassified as taxable-only; K.S.A. 79-3702 counts cumulative gross receipts from sales, so the gross-sales group is at least 23 states, not 22. (3) The dossier says Washington does not mention domain registration, but WAC 458-20-15503(m) does. It says advertising services do not include web hosting or domain name registration, which is not a taxability holding. (4) "Register for me requires a US bank account" is conditional on states that charge fees or require banking details. The big open questions are not closed. No EU, HMRC, Japanese or US state source I could reach says whether a domain registration is an electronically supplied service or taxable, and WebSearch was still exhausted, so I could not look for contrary sources. Australia, India, Mississippi, Wyoming, the Canadian provinces and Name.com's tax clause remain unverifiable. Two omissions matter for a $15 product. The 200-transaction prong is reached at about $3,000 of revenue in states that kept it. Economic nexus for non-sales taxes (Washington B&O, Texas franchise tax) is not covered.

Not confirmed:
- **corrected**: 'Register for me' is for remote sellers only and requires a US bank account -> US bank account required only in states that charge fees or require banking details; non-US owners also need EIN plus SSN/ITIN; applicants who already received a notice or opened an online account with the state are ineligible (Remote-seller condition confirmed. The bank-account claim is conditional in Stripe's text: 'Have a US bank account. This is required in states that charge fees or require banking details.' The dossier's truncated quote hides the condition. TaxJar filing separately needs a US bank account for remittance.)
- **unverifiable**: Mississippi threshold is $250,000 gross sales (Stripe and the Sales Tax Institute agree, but I could not reach a Mississippi DOR or statute page.)
- **refuted**: Most states are $100,000 'or 200 transactions', including ME, NC and WY -> NC: $100,000 only. ME: $100,000 only (taxable sales). WY: unresolved. (North Carolina G.S. 105-164.8(b)(9) has only 'gross sales in excess of $100,000'. 36 M.R.S. 1754-B counts gross sales over $100,000 with no transaction test. Stripe's own NC and ME pages still say 'or 200 transactions', so the dossier copied a stale aggregator. Wyoming conflicts (Stripe says 200; Sales Tax Institute says removed 7/1/2024) and no primary source was reachable. Because the prong matters at $15 per sale, this is a material error, though in the conservative direction.)
- **corrected**: 22 states count gross sales including nontaxable revenue; 9 count tangible goods only; 12 count taxable only -> At least 23 gross-receipts states (Kansas added); Maryland and Rhode Island unresolved (Confirmed on primary sources: gross in TX, WA, NC; TPP-only in CA, NY, AL, FL; taxable-only in ME and NJ. Kansas is wrong: K.S.A. 79-3702(h)(1)(G) counts 'cumulative gross receipts from sales by the retailer to customers in this state', so it belongs in the gross group. Maryland and Rhode Island could not be checked. The rest rest on Stripe's per-state pages.)
- **corrected**: Washington excludes web hosting from digital automated services; domain registration is not mentioned -> WAC 458-20-15503(m) mentions domain name registration (as not an advertising service); no taxability holding (The web hosting exclusion is confirmed (WAC 458-20-15503, exclusion for storage, hosting and back-up). Domain registration is mentioned: subsection (m) says 'Advertising services do not include web hosting services and domain name registration.' It is not a taxability ruling, but it contradicts 'not mentioned'.)
- **unverifiable**: Marketplace facilitator laws exist in every sales-tax state plus DC (Stripe's US page says so; not checked against statutes.)
- **unverifiable**: FastSpring publishes no price and no public prohibited-products list (Pricing page says 'Get your pricing quote'. Four guessed policy URLs returned 404; absence of a list is not provable.)
- **unverifiable**: Quebec QST CAD 30,000, BC PST CAD 10,000, Saskatchewan and Manitoba 1 transaction (Stripe's Canada province pages match, but no provincial primary source was fetched, and whether a domain registration is taxable in each province is unaddressed.)
- **unverifiable**: Australia: register at AUD 75,000 in 12 months; sales to GST-registered businesses excluded (ATO returned 403; legislation.gov.au renders only a shell. Stripe's Australia page matches the figure.)
- **unverifiable**: India OIDAR: no threshold, register from first B2C sale (Only Stripe's India page was reachable; it says so, but no Indian primary source.)
- **unverifiable**: Name.com Reseller Agreement passes sales, use and VAT taxes to the reseller (The policy page did not render under curl or Chromium (about 1,300 characters), so I could not re-check the clause.)

Missed by the analyst:
- The 200-transaction prong is the practical trigger for a $15 product: 200 sales is about $3,000 of revenue, so in states that kept the transaction test (AR, DC, GA, HI, MD, MI, MN, NE, NV, NJ, OH, PR, RI, VT, VA, WV) nexus arrives at roughly 3% of the dollar threshold. The dossier lists the numbers but does not draw this out.
- Economic nexus for non-sales taxes is not covered. Washington B&O uses the same $100,000 gross-receipts nexus (RCW 82.04.067) and taxes service revenue regardless of sales-tax taxability. The Texas Comptroller page states a $500,000 gross-receipts economic nexus for franchise (margin) tax. Ohio and Oregon have CATs. A sales-tax-only ledger will miss these.
- Stripe Tax's fee can apply even when the calculated tax is zero (how-tax-works docs), which matters for voluntary registration in a state where domains are nontaxable.
- Stripe's per-state pages are stale in places (NC, ME). The Sales Tax Institute chart (as of 8/1/2026) is a free cross-check that shows removals of the 200-transaction test in AK, IL, IN, KY, ME, NC, SD, UT and WY, but its 'gross sales' label is wrong for ME and NJ, so neither aggregator should replace the statutes.
- UK accepts alternative evidence of business status when a customer has no VAT number, and Switzerland's reverse charge depends on VAT-registered status or more than CHF 10,000 a year, so the proposed 'valid VAT ID only' B2B gate is stricter than some regimes require and misses the Swiss threshold.
- Stripe Managed Payments needs an eligible digital PTC and a fully automated product; if Stripe finds the product ineligible the seller bears the indirect tax. Paddle asks sellers of products under $10 to negotiate custom pricing (relevant to cheap TLDs).
- Register for me also refuses applicants who already received a notice or opened an online account with the state, or who acquired a business; non-US owners need an EIN plus SSN or ITIN.
- Unresolved because WebSearch stayed exhausted: any primary source classifying domain registration as an electronically supplied service or as taxable in a specific state. Member-state guidance (Germany, Ireland, Poland, Belgium and others) is where such a statement would most likely exist and was not checked.

## tech-a11y-standards
Dossier: `tech-a11y-standards.md`

### Verifier lens: skeptic (confidence high; 25 claims confirmed)

I re-fetched about 45 load-bearing claims from primary sources and refuted none. Every number, date and quotation I re-checked matches the dossier. That covers the WCAG 2.2 and WCAG 3 status, the ETSI PDF, EUR-Lex, German and Irish statutes, Seyfarth and ADA.gov, npm, and the WebAIM, caniuse and MDN data. Contrast values were recomputed from the brief's palette, and I ran my own axe-core 4.13.0 probes. There are two substantive corrections and one caveat. (1) The claim that the microenterprise exemption is lost "as soon as" thresholds are crossed is too strong. Recommendation 2003/361/EC Art 4(2) requires two consecutive accounting periods, counts headcount in annual work units including owner-managers, and aggregates partner and linked enterprises. (2) The claim that the Ledger is the WCAG 2.5.8 "Equivalent" control omits "on the same page" from the SC text, so do not rely on it. This is moot at 44 px targets. (3) EN 301 549 v3.2.1 is the reference per AccessibleEU, but the Commission's harmonised-standards pages list only the WAD decision (EU) 2018/2048. I found no EAA citation page for any version, and I could not check the OJ itself after 7 Sep. Two other checks added value. The Night|Lantern|Night ring's 3.10:1 floor is a proof for all solid backgrounds (3.1003:1, derived analytically), not a sample. The only pending EAA amendment, COM(2026) 590, deletes Art 24(1) on procurement and is irrelevant here. Missed items are listed below.

Not confirmed:
- **corrected**: The EU legal reference for the EAA remains EN 301 549 v3.2.1 (WCAG 2.1 AA) until v4.1.1 is cited in the OJ -> AccessibleEU says v3.2.1 remains the reference until v4.1.1 is cited, but no OJ citation under the EAA was visible for any version. The Commission's harmonised-standards pages list only Implementing Decision (EU) 2018/2048 (+2021 amendment) under the Web Accessibility Directive. Treat v3.2.1 as the practical reference, not a statutory one. (Commission page https://single-market-economy.ec.europa.eu/single-market/goods/european-standards/harmonised-standards/accessibility-websites-and-mobile-applications_en lists only the WAD decision, and its index has no EAA page. German BFSGV s.3 says 'Stand der Technik' and a Bundesfachstelle list, not a frozen version. Whether v4.1.1 was cited after 7 Sep is unverifiable here; AccessibleEU news through 24 Sep announces no citation.)
- **corrected**: Microenterprise (fewer than 10 persons, turnover or balance sheet <= EUR 2M) providing services is exempt (Art 4(5)); the exemption ends when the enterprise outgrows the thresholds -> Exemption text and thresholds are right. But under Recommendation 2003/361/EC Art 4(2) status is lost only when ceilings are exceeded over two consecutive accounting periods. Headcount is annual work units and includes owner-managers and partners (Art 5). Partner (>=25%) and linked enterprises are aggregated (Arts 3, 6). Turnover excludes VAT, and new firms use a bona fide estimate (Art 4(3)). 'Lost as soon as' is too strong. (Art 3(23) and Art 4(5) verbatim in the Directive; recital 53 'genuinely fulfil' Rec 2003/361/EC confirmed. How each Member State applies the two-period rule is a lawyer question.)
- **corrected**: The Ledger table serves as the WCAG 2.5.8 'Equivalent' exception for scene tags -> SC 2.5.8's Equivalent exception requires the function to be achievable 'through a different control on the same page that meets this criterion'. A separate Ledger view reached by a link probably does not qualify. Do not cite it as the exception; rely on the 44 px target rule. (The dossier's paraphrase in sections 2 and 5 drops 'on the same page'. Verified in the SC text and the Target Size (Minimum) Understanding.)
- **unverifiable**: That a domain-registration shop is an EAA e-commerce service, and that v4.1.1 has not been cited in the OJ since 7 Sep 2026 (The text supports the first for consumer purchases via a website, but no guidance names domains. For the second, WebSearch and the OJ search were unavailable; the only evidence is negative (no citation announced through 24 Sep on AccessibleEU; none on Commission pages).)

Missed by the analyst:
- Commission Recommendation 2003/361/EC Art 4(2), 4(3), 5, 3 and 6: status lost only after two consecutive accounting periods over the ceilings; headcount in annual work units including owner-managers and partners; partner and linked enterprise aggregation; VAT-exclusive turnover; bona fide estimate for new firms. This changes the 'exemption ends when it grows' wording and the A-04 DECISIONS.md test.
- SC 2.5.8 Equivalent exception requires the other control to be 'on the same page'; the dossier's 'Ledger is the Equivalent' reliance omits this.
- Spain Royal Decree 707/2026 on cognitive accessibility (in force 2 Jan 2027) reportedly obliges businesses among others to provide plain-language/Easy Read information (AccessibleEU 14 Sep 2026). Only the summary was read; scope for an online domain shop is unknown. Relevant if Spain is a target market.
- EN 301 549 v4.1.1 national transposition dates (doa 30 Nov 2026, dop/e 31 May 2027, dow 31 May 2028), Annex ZB and clause A.2 (EAA conformity mapping); AccessibleEU describes it as adding six new WCAG 2.2 requirements.
- The Commission's harmonised-standards pages list only Implementing Decision (EU) 2018/2048 (+2021 amendment) for EN 301 549 under the WAD. No EAA citation of any EN 301 549 version is visible, so 'legal baseline v3.2.1' is a practical, not statutory, statement. Germany's BFSGV s.3 uses 'Stand der Technik' and a Bundesfachstelle list rather than a frozen version.
- The only pending EAA amendment on EUR-Lex is COM(2026) 590 (9 Sep 2026), which deletes Art 24(1) (procurement). It is irrelevant to Mosshatch; recorded so nobody re-chases it.
- Commission v Bulgaria (C-646/24) for failure to transpose the EAA: transposition gaps exist, so check each target Member State individually.
- WCAG 2.2 AAA criteria 2.4.13 Focus Appearance and 2.4.12 Focus Not Obscured (Enhanced) are absent from the A-02 AAA target list; the 2 px / 2 px / 2 px ring likely meets 2.4.13 but was not measured. The separate red flash threshold is not addressed.
- ESLint is now at 10.11.0 while eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y (last release Oct 2024) tops out at ESLint ^9, a real install blocker for a React lint gate.
- Checklist A-51 should assert target-size appears in the union of passes, violations, incomplete and inapplicable, not just passes or violations, or a route with no undersized targets fails the CI check spuriously.
- WebAIM #11 regional split (JAWS 72.5% vs NVDA 15.6% in North America; NVDA far ahead in Asia, Africa/Middle East and South America; Europe about even): NVDA deserves more test weight than the 32.9% headline if the customer base is global.
- Summary wording: 'coloured text needs alpha 0.80 or more (... Pool 0.91)' is inconsistent because 0.80 does not make Pool text pass (needs 0.907-0.915); the body's A-40 rule (no Pool text on glass) is correct. Playwright 1.63.0 was published 2026-09-04, not 2026-09-29 (that is its registry 'modified' date).
- Minor US developments: Missouri's anti-abusive-website-litigation law (operative 28 Aug 2026, applies to Missouri-resident defendants) and the NFB challenge to Title II deadline extensions (June 2026); neither changes Title III exposure for an out-of-state online seller.

## tech-fonts-csp-headers
Dossier: `tech-fonts-csp-headers.md`

### Verifier lens: skeptic (confidence high; 19 claims confirmed)

I found nothing to refute in the dossier's load-bearing claims. I re-fetched the licences, Google Fonts CSS and font files, MDN, the specs, Vercel, Stripe, OWASP, BCD and npm myself, and rebuilt the font subsets. I also ran my own Chromium 141 probes and a fresh Vite 8.3.1 + React 19.3.0 + three 0.186.1 build under the exact enforced CSP. The licence, CSP, form-action, SameSite, Referrer-Policy, Permissions-Policy and Cache API results all reproduced. There is one 12-byte size correction (20,368 B, not 20,356 B, for the Atkinson Next subset). One of the analyst's "unverified" items is now resolved: a relative Reporting-Endpoints URL works in Chromium, and report-to delivers over HTTPS. I also found gaps that matter once GLB models or drei are used: connect-src needs blob:, and drei's defaults load from CDNs that this CSP blocks. Firefox, Safari and live-Vercel behaviour remain unverified because only Chromium and no deployment were available.

Not confirmed:
- **corrected**: pyftsubset build: Young Serif 400 = 18,440 B (hinted 27,256 B); Atkinson Next variable clamped to wght 400-700 = 20,356 B; total 38,796 B, 36.4% under Google's pair; name IDs 0/13/14 kept; no arrows, check or cross glyphs -> Atkinson Next clamped 400-700 subset = 20,368 B (not 20,356 B); total 38,808 B; saving still 36.4% (Re-ran the dossier's exact recipe (fonttools 4.66.1, brotli 1.2.0, google/fonts main files) and got 18,440 B and 27,256 B for Young Serif, exact. The Atkinson Next subset came out 12 B larger (20,368 B). Name records 0/13/14 survive in both subsets, and the axis is clamped to 400..700. Neither Google TTF has U+2190-2193, U+2197, U+2713 or U+2715 in its cmap. My own Vite build also copied the files at 18,440 and 20,368 B.)
- **corrected**: CSP report-to needs Reporting-Endpoints over HTTPS; relative endpoint URL acceptance and HTTPS delivery were unverified; report-to is Chrome 70 / Safari 16.4 / Firefox 149 -> Relative Reporting-Endpoints URL (csp="/api/csp-report") is valid per W3C Reporting spec and delivers in Chromium 141 over HTTPS as application/reports+json; over plain http://localhost nothing is delivered; when both report-uri and report-to are present only the report-to POST is sent (BCD numbers are confirmed (Firefox 149). W3C Reporting 3.3 parses each endpoint 'with base URL set to response's url'. In full Chromium 141 over HTTPS (self-generated cert trusted via --ignore-certificate-errors-spki-list) a relative URL and an absolute URL both delivered a report within 0.6 s. http://localhost delivered nothing in 30 s. Playwright's default headless shell delivered nothing even over HTTPS, so negative results from that binary are not evidence. Firefox and Safari were not tested.)
- **unverifiable**: Vercel-specific behaviour: merge order of overlapping header rules, Sec-Fetch-* reaching functions, and the /(.*) rewrite coexisting with /api (These need a live Vercel deployment and I did not deploy. Docs only say the filesystem takes precedence over rewrites. The dossier's draft avoids the merge-order question by using disjoint header names per rule.)
- **unverifiable**: Firefox and Safari behaviour for form-action redirects, SameSite on the Stripe return, Trusted Types and report-to (Only Chromium 141 is installed. Spec and MDN/BCD text agree with the dossier, but I could not run other engines.)

Missed by the analyst:
- connect-src also needs blob: once GLB/GLTF models with embedded textures are loaded. My Chromium 141 test under connect-src 'self' shows fetch(blob:...) and createImageBitmap(await fetch(blobURL)) are refused, and they work only with 'blob:' listed. fetch('data:...') is likewise refused without connect-src data: (the dossier had this as inferred, not tested). three@0.186.1 GLTFLoader.js lines 2633-2660 pick ImageBitmapLoader (except Safari <17 / Firefox <98), line 3368 does URL.createObjectURL(blob) for bufferView images, and ImageBitmapLoader.js:173 uses fetch(url). The dossier's table lists img-src blob: for GLTF textures and connect-src data: only.
- If @react-three/drei (10.7.9 current; tech-webgl-three.md already cites drei's PerformanceMonitor) is adopted, its defaults conflict with connect-src 'self' / script-src 'self': useGLTF loads Draco decoders from https://www.gstatic.com/draco/versioned/decoders/1.5.5/, Environment presets load from raw.githack.com, Matcap/Normal textures and Ktx2 use cdn.jsdelivr.net, and troika-three-text 0.52.5 (drei Text) fetches font-fallback data from cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/lojjic/unicode-font-resolver@v1.0.1 (override with unicodeFontsURL). troika-worker-utils 0.53.0 also spawns Blob workers, which need worker-src blob:. All of these need self-hosting plus the worker-src change.
- Reporting-Endpoints accepts a relative URL (W3C Reporting 3.3 resolves it against the response URL; Chromium 141 delivered over HTTPS). Use csp="/api/csp-report" instead of the APP_ORIGIN placeholder so preview deployments do not report to a hard-coded production origin. When both report-uri and report-to are present, Chromium sends only the report-to POST (application/reports+json array), so the handler must parse that shape.
- Vercel docs were reorganised: /docs/security/encryption and /docs/headers/security-headers now live under /docs/cdn-security/ (the old paths redirect for HTML but the .md variants 404). Update citations.
- Device Bound Session Credentials shipped in Chrome 147 per BCD 8.1.3 (Secure-Session-Registration, Secure-Session-Challenge, Secure-Session-Response, Secure-Session-Skipped, Sec-Secure-Session-Id; Firefox and Safari false; web-features baseline false). It is an optional Chromium-only hardening for the __Host- session cookie and does not change the recommendation.
- Vercel documents that Strict-Transport-Security can be modified with custom response headers, so an explicit STS in vercel.json is the supported override. This partly answers the analyst's duplicate-or-replace question; runtime behaviour still needs a preview deployment.
- The draft Permissions-Policy payment=() would also disable the Payment Request API (Apple Pay / Google Pay) in an embedded Stripe iframe if Embedded Checkout or Elements is adopted later. This is reasoned, not tested.
- Braille Institute pages remain unreadable (bot challenge, not worked around), so claims about Braille Institute terms or language counts for Atkinson Hyperlegible Next stay unverified. The OFL text itself imposes no RFN or naming restriction.

## tech-kms-envelope-encryption
Dossier: `tech-kms-envelope-encryption.md`

### Verifier lens: skeptic (confidence high; 18 claims confirmed)

I found nothing to refute. I re-fetched about 20 primary sources (AWS docs, price list and SLA; Vercel docs; NIST; GCP; Azure; Node; npm) and every load-bearing number, quote and date held. The cost table recomputes exactly. Four points change how the dossier should be read. (1) The headline AWS cost excludes the key-rotation surcharge, even though the design turns rotation on. The surcharge takes the 3-key floor from $3 to about $9 per month by year 3. (2) The analyst's open question about the STS session length Vercel's credentials provider requests is answered: the provider defaults to durationSeconds=3600, and callers can override it. (3) Vercel's Region Name column labels iad1 as us-east-1, but network co-location with AWS is still not verifiable. (4) AWS's revoke-sessions deny does not affect sessions assumed after the revocation, so the trust-policy or DisableKey step is the real stop. The analyst missed several things: AWS IAM now treats the Vercel global OIDC issuer as a shared provider that requires an aud condition, Vercel Deployment Policies can lock down who deploys to production, and direct kms:Encrypt is a simpler design for values up to 4 KB. Latency, HCP Vault prices, Vercel token revocation, team-slug reuse and live key-policy behaviour remain unverifiable. WebSearch was unavailable (budget exhausted), so recent-change hunting used primary changelog, document-history and sitemap pages. Notes are in verify_file.

Not confirmed:
- **unverifiable**: HCP Vault Dedicated dollar prices (secondary source: about $0.62/h dev, about $1.58/h Essentials, $72.92 per client per month) (hashicorp.com/en/products/vault/pricing still returns the Vercel Security Checkpoint (HTTP 429). I did not try to bypass it, and WebSearch was unavailable. The official tiers page confirms only the pricing model (hourly base cost plus a per-client hourly fee on Essentials/Standard), not the dollars. This does not affect the AWS recommendation.)
- **unverifiable**: KMS request latency from Vercel regions, whether Vercel can revoke an issued OIDC token, whether a released Vercel team slug can be re-registered, and the sub of a production build token (No provider publishes KMS latency numbers and the sandbox is not on Vercel. Vercel's OIDC docs, the accounts docs and the docs sitemap say nothing about token revocation, team-slug reuse after a rename or delete, or the sub of a production build token. Plan a latency benchmark from an iad1 function and ask Vercel support about slug reuse.)

Missed by the analyst:
- AWS IAM now treats the Vercel global OIDC endpoint (https://oidc.vercel.com) as a SHARED OIDC provider (IAM doc history 2025-06-06 and 2025-08-01). The role trust policy must evaluate oidc.vercel.com:aud, or role creation or update fails with MalformedPolicyDocument. The Team issuer (oidc.vercel.com/<team>) is a private provider with no mandatory claim. This supports the Team-mode recommendation, and any project still in Global mode must keep the aud condition. Source: https://docs.aws.amazon.com/IAM/latest/UserGuide/id_roles_providers_oidc_secure-by-default.html
- Vercel Deployment Policies (https://vercel.com/docs/deployments/deployment-policy, last_updated 2026-07-14) restrict per environment which Git orgs/repos and which deployment mechanisms (Git, CLI, Deploy Hooks) can deploy to Production. This directly shrinks the 'anyone who can run code in production' blast radius and is missing from the hardening list.
- Direct kms:Encrypt/Decrypt is a simpler design for values up to 4,096 bytes (API_Encrypt: 'Encrypts plaintext of up to 4,096 bytes'). It costs the same number of KMS requests as GenerateDataKey+Decrypt and removes local crypto (nonce, AAD canonicalisation, plaintext-DEK handling), and ReEncrypt and the encryption-context policy still work. The dossier lists the limit but never evaluates this option. Trade-offs: a hard 4 KB ceiling and the plaintext goes to KMS over TLS.
- Vercel's AWS guide and the provider package support a custom audience (audience, jti, skipTokenCache, durationSeconds). It must be added to the IAM OIDC provider's Audiences and to the trust policy, and it gives the prod role a dedicated aud. The provider's default durationSeconds is 3600.
- Optional network pinning: Vercel Static IPs (Pro/Enterprise, $100/month per project plus Private Data Transfer, project-wide not per environment; KB updated 2026-09-28) would allow an aws:SourceIp condition on the role or key policy so stolen STS credentials cannot be replayed from elsewhere. The cost exceeds all KMS spend, so it is an option, not a default.
- Vercel Connect (https://vercel.com/docs/connect, updated 2026-09-18) is a new team-level credential broker for agents ('without storing provider keys'). It is not a KEK substitute but is adjacent to the agent scoped-token design.
- Vercel's documented default failover order for iad1 (cle1 = us-east-2, yul1, sfo1, pdx1, ...) means an unpinned AWS_REGION can silently point KMS calls at another Region during an outage. A single-Region KMS key is itself exposed to a us-east-1 event; a multi-Region replica costs +$1/month.
- AWS's own revoke-sessions caveat: sessions assumed after 'Revoke active sessions' are not denied. The emergency runbook should treat trust-policy or provider removal, or DisableKey, as the primary stop, not the session revoke.
- NIST SP 800-38D Rev.1 second pre-draft proposes dropping GCM tags shorter than 96 bits and adding wGCM. It does not affect the 16-byte-tag design, but the 2^32 rule still comes from the 2007 text.
- The AWS KMS and IAM document histories show no 2026 change to KMS pricing, quotas, encryption-context or rotation rules (KMS latest entry 2026-03-06, service-principal grants). The only 2026 OIDC-related IAM entry is GitHub-specific (2026-05-12). Nothing found changes the conclusions.

## tech-mcp-cli-agent-surface
Dossier: `tech-mcp-cli-agent-surface.md`

### Verifier lens: skeptic (confidence high; 27 claims confirmed)

I re-fetched about 40 of the analyst's findings from primary sources and re-ran the SDK behaviour tests myself. Nearly all hold, and none of the headline design conclusions is refuted. Confirmed by direct fetch or local run: MCP 2026-07-28 is current and stateless, DCR is deprecated in favour of CIMD, the SDK v2 handler does no Origin or auth validation, and the SDK's approval-related behaviour reproduces (elicitation is unreliable, so approval must be server-enforced). I reproduced this against @modelcontextprotocol/server@2.2.0 with my own test script: Origin evil.example returned 200; a 2026 client without the elicitation capability got HTTP 400 / -32021; a 2025 client got an isError result. The RFC 8628 numbers, the Vercel, Claude and GitHub secret-scanning facts, the npm versions and dates, and the WebAuthn L3 Recommendation status (25 Aug 2026) also check out. Two corrections and one caveat: (1) F78 is stale, because the live Vercel HTML docs now say Secrets are allowed in Development, "reversing the earlier rule"; the analyst read the stale .md rendering. (2) F70's 25k-token limit is right, but Claude Code persists over-limit output to a file under ~/.claude/projects instead of truncating it. (3) F87: in a headless container the default @napi-rs/keyring path threw AccessDenied on set/get/delete instead of silently falling back, so every operation needs try/catch, not just the constructor. Missed items that matter: Stripe agent-tagged keys carry built-in approval rules; Anthropic's connector page documents that Claude apps follow specs only through 2025-11-25 and lists no elicitation support; Claude Code has a headersHelper for short-lived tokens; and the official MCP OAuth client-credentials extension exists. Unresolved: keytar archive status, any real Vercel deployment behaviour, and which clients other than Claude Code speak 2026-07-28. WebSearch was already at its 200-call budget.

Not confirmed:
- **corrected**: F70: Claude Code warns above 10,000 MCP output tokens and 'caps' output at 25,000 -> 25,000-token default limit; over-limit results persisted to disk with a file reference (not truncated); per-tool override up to 500,000 chars; claude.ai/Desktop ~150,000 chars and 240 s timeout (The numbers are right, but over-limit output is saved to a file under ~/.claude/projects/.../tool-results and replaced by a file-path message, not truncated. Tools can raise the threshold via _meta['anthropic/maxResultSizeChars'] (max 500,000 chars). Anthropic's connector page gives claude.ai/Desktop a different limit of about 150,000 characters per result and 240 s per call.)
- **corrected**: F78: Vercel Development-environment variables cannot use the 'sensitive' tier and stay merely 'encrypted' -> Vercel now allows write-only 'Secret' (formerly Sensitive) variables in Development; vocabulary is Config vs Secret (Stale. The live HTML docs say the opposite: CLI env page 'Development | config default | Use --sensitive or --visibility secret for Secret. Secrets are allowed in Development'; the sensitive-variables page says 'Secrets are allowed in Development. This reverses the earlier rule'. The .md twin of the pages the analyst saved still has the old wording. Low impact on MossHatch design, but do not cite Vercel as lacking secret Development vars.)
- **corrected**: F87: @napi-rs/keyring on Linux silently falls back to in-memory keyutils, and pinning {linux:{store:'secret-service'}} makes construction throw instead -> Default path may hard-fail at operation time (AccessDenied) in containers; pin the store and catch on every call (README wording matches and the pinned secret-service Entry threw at construction (DBus error). But in this headless container the default Entry constructed fine and then setPassword/getPassword/deletePassword all threw 'Couldn't access platform storage: AccessDenied' (no silent fallback), and explicit keyutils worked once then failed on a second run. Wrap every get/set/delete in try/catch; do not rely on the fallback.)
- **unverifiable**: Support for MCP 2026-07-28 in Cursor, VS Code and claude.ai/Desktop is undocumented (No primary source says any of them speaks 2026-07-28. Anthropic's connector page says Claude follows authorization specs 2025-03-26/06-18/11-25 only. VS Code release notes 1.130-1.139 and the Cursor changelog have no mention. Keep the dual-era default.)

Missed by the analyst:
- Stripe agent-tagged restricted API keys are automatically subject to approval rules requiring a designated reviewer for sensitive actions (payouts, refunds, account config) - the closest commercial precedent for server-enforced human approval; the dossier cites only the tag (https://docs.stripe.com/keys, 'API keys for autonomous agents').
- Anthropic's 'Build an MCP server for Claude' page (https://claude.com/docs/connectors/building/index.md) says Claude follows only the 2025-03-26/2025-06-18/2025-11-25 authorization specs, does not yet support resource subscriptions, sampling or draft capabilities (elicitation not listed as supported), and limits tool results to ~150,000 characters and calls to 240 s on claude.ai/Desktop (Claude Code: 25,000 tokens, MCP_TOOL_TIMEOUT). Resolves part of the 'unverified' list and supports the dual-era + server-side approval design.
- Claude Code headersHelper (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/mcp): a command that emits a JSON header map on each connect, re-run on 401/403, 10 s timeout, disables OAuth fallback. A mosshatch CLI could supply short-lived mh_at_ tokens from the keychain instead of 30-day static PATs in .mcp.json.
- Claude Code persists over-limit MCP output to a file under ~/.claude/projects/.../tool-results rather than truncating it, and supports _meta['anthropic/maxResultSizeChars'] up to 500,000 chars - relevant for secret-returning tools and pagination advice (F70 wording).
- Official MCP extensions io.modelcontextprotocol/oauth-client-credentials (RFC 7523 JWT assertions preferred) and enterprise-managed-authorization exist as the standards-track machine-to-machine option; Claude's auth page also lists oauth_anthropic_creds and custom_connection types (contact mcp-review@anthropic.com). Client support unknown.
- mcp-remote 0.14.3 (2026-09-21) has --protocol auto to bridge 2026-07-28 servers for legacy stdio clients (default is legacy). Needed only if the server ever sets legacy:'reject'; Better Auth's reference config does use legacy:'reject', which would not work with hosted Claude today.
- The spec's compatibility matrix (https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/basic/versioning) states legacy client + modern-only server 'Fails ... no fall-forward mechanism' - the normative reason to keep the dual-era default; not cited in the dossier.
- Vercel docs changed vocabulary and rules: Sensitive is now Secret/Config and 'Secrets are allowed in Development' (F78 stale); the .md endpoints of Vercel docs can serve older text than the HTML pages, so prefer the HTML.
- Microsoft's 2026-07-31 update reassesses Storm-2372 as a sub-cluster of Midnight Blizzard.
- W3C Secure Payment Confirmation (Editor's Draft 2026-07-02, https://w3c.github.io/secure-payment-confirmation/) is the WebAuthn-based browser-displayed payment confirmation; still an ED, so the 'page must show the details itself' assumption stands.
- @napi-rs/keyring default path threw AccessDenied on set/get/delete in a container instead of falling back; design must catch on every operation, not only construction.
- No advisories in the npm bulk advisory database for @modelcontextprotocol/server 2.2.0, sdk 1.31.0, mcp-handler 2.2.0 or @napi-rs/keyring 2.1.0 as of 2026-09-29; the draft MCP changelog is empty (no pending spec changes).

## tech-neon-drizzle-resend
Dossier: `tech-neon-drizzle-resend.md`

### Verifier lens: skeptic (confidence high; 23 claims confirmed)

I re-fetched about 30 load-bearing claims from primary pages, the npm registry, published package tarballs and a headless-Chromium render of Neon's pricing table. Nearly all prices, limits, versions and Postgres or driver semantics match. That includes neon-http throwing on db.transaction() in drizzle-orm 0.45.3 and 1.0.0-rc.4, and also in the newer 1.0.0-rc.5-5935859 build. Five items need correction or a nuance. (a) The "6 database-connectivity incidents" is a component-tag count: one of the six (2026-07-11) was closed as not impacting databases, and another is about scale-to-zero. (b) SES's $0.10 per 1,000 is no longer the default for new accounts, which start on Essentials at $0.16 from 2026-07-21. (c) "No hard spending cap" is incomplete, because Neon's API spec has per-project consumption quotas that suspend computes. (d) The N55 register "quote" is a paraphrase, not verbatim. (e) SOC 2 report gating: the pricing-page table shows Scale-only, which settles the analyst's caveat, but Neon's Platform Terms promise the SOC 2 report on request under the DPA audit clause. The most decision-relevant miss is that drizzle-orm 0.45.x's migrator skips migrations whose journal timestamp is older than the newest applied one; 1.0-rc fixes this. WebSearch was unavailable (budget exhausted), and trust.neon.com stayed unverifiable behind a Cloudflare challenge.

Not confirmed:
- **corrected**: [N55] Console/API/CLI-created roles (including the project default role) get neon_superuser (CREATEDB, CREATEROLE, BYPASSRLS, REPLICATION); SQL-created roles get only basic privileges -> Same conclusion, but cite the roles-page wording: 'Roles created in the Neon Console, CLI, or API, including the role created with a Neon project, are granted membership in the neon_superuser role'. (Substance is correct. But the register 'quote' ('automatically receive neon_superuser membership ...') does not appear verbatim on the page, so the 'every quote machine-checked' statement is false for this row. The page also says BYPASSRLS applies only to projects created after Aug 15, 2023 (irrelevant for a new project). neon_superuser additionally holds pg_write_all_data and pg_read_all_data.)
- **corrected**: [N51] Neon status history shows '6 database-connectivity incidents (May-Jul 2026)' across regions -> 4-5 genuine connectivity or start-failure events (05-08, 05-10, 05-25, 07-15, plus the 05-07 scale-to-zero fault) in the window read, each under about 5 h; 6 is the raw component-tag count. (Six non-maintenance entries carry the 'Database Connectivity' component, so the tag count is right. But the 07-11 us-west-2 entry was closed with 'did not impact Database or project operations', and the 05-07 entry (tagged sa-east-1) is about us-east-1 computes not scaling to zero. Only 05-08 (us-east-1 Cell 6, ~1,000 projects with slow or stuck compute starts, 2 h) is a Partial Service Disruption. The page has a 'Next Page' link that was not read. The analyst's summary says May-Jul and the dossier TL;DR says May-Sep.)
- **corrected**: [A5] Amazon SES costs $0.10 per 1,000 emails (headline price for the alternative) -> New SES account default: Essentials $0.16 per 1,000 emails; a-la-carte $0.10 per 1,000 by switching ($0.10 a-la-carte is still listed, but the SES pricing page footnote 3 says new accounts start on the Essentials plan from 2026-07-21 at $0.16 per 1,000 (0-10M) unless they switch to a-la-carte. Pro is $0.22 + $105/mo; Enterprise $0.23 + $500/mo. Sandbox limits (200 msgs/24 h, 1 msg/s) confirmed.)
- **corrected**: [N58] Neon has no hard spending cap; only notifications at 80% and 100% of a threshold -> No dollar-denominated cap; per-project usage quotas (API settings.quota) can suspend computes when exceeded; autoscaling max CU is the other lever. (Notification-only spending alerts are confirmed (Free none; Launch and Scale yes). But the Neon API spec documents ProjectQuota under project settings.quota (active_time_seconds, compute_time_seconds, written_data_bytes, data_transfer_bytes, logical_size_bytes): when a quota is exceeded all active computes are suspended. That is a usage cap, not a dollar cap, and the spec does not state plan gating. A hard stop on a production DB is itself an outage risk.)
- **unverifiable**: Unverified item: Neon Trust Center content and what a Launch customer can obtain (trust.neon.com returned a Cloudflare 'Just a moment' challenge even in headless Chromium; the challenge host is denied by the egress proxy, and I did not work around it. The pricing-page table and the Platform Terms give the two conflicting signals described under missed.)

Missed by the analyst:
- Drizzle 0.45.x migrator skips out-of-order migrations. drizzle-orm 0.45.3 pg-core/dialect.js applies a migration only if Number(lastDbMigration.created_at) < migration.folderMillis, and folderMillis is the journal 'when' timestamp (migrator.js line 22). drizzle-kit 0.31.11 'migrate' delegates to drizzle-orm/node-postgres/migrator. Two PR branches that each generate a migration and merge in the wrong order will silently drop the earlier-timestamped one. 1.0.0-rc.4 switches to a name-set difference (migrator.utils.js getMigrationsToRun) and removes journal.json. This is decision-relevant for 'pin 0.45.x vs 1.0-rc' and for the per-PR Neon branch plan.
- Neon contract facts (neon.com/dpa redirects to neon.com/platform-terms, 'Product Specific Schedule (Neon)', Last Updated Aug 5, 2026): the contracting party is Databricks, Inc. under the Databricks Master Cloud Services Agreement; plans auto-renew month to month; Databricks may change the agreement and pricing and continued use is consent; customers must not put PCI-DSS cardholder data in Customer Content; the sub-processor list is Databricks' (databricks.com/legal/databricks-subprocessors, Last Updated June 9, 2026) plus Grafana Labs. This answers the analyst's 'Neon Sub Contractors page not fetched'.
- The same Neon DPA audit clause says 'Upon request, Databricks ... will supply (on a confidential basis) the SOC 2 report for the Platform Services and summary copies of the penetration testing report(s)'. That conflicts with treating SOC 2 report access as Scale-only; counsel should reconcile the pricing-table gating with the contract text before deciding Launch is sufficient.
- Neon Launch support tier is 'Billing support' only (Scale: Standard; Free: Community). The dossier tabulates it but its 'Launch is the minimum for production' implication does not flag that Launch carries no technical support entitlement.
- Role privilege nuance for the audit design: neon_superuser also carries pg_write_all_data and pg_read_all_data, so a Console-created role (including neondb_owner) can write to any table regardless of REVOKEs. Only the raising trigger plus not using such a role at runtime protects the audit table, and an owner can disable or drop its own triggers, so the audit-table owner should be a SQL-created migrator role.
- Neon API per-project consumption quotas (settings.quota) exist and suspend computes when exceeded; the dossier says no hard cap is documented.
- Neon product changes in the last two weeks: 'Neon backend generally available' on 2026-09-18 (Lakebase Postgres branding; Functions with UTC-cron and object-created triggers that fire even when compute is scaled to zero, available in aws-us-east-1). This is a candidate outbox-worker trigger that the dossier left unverified; runtime limits not read. @neon/sdk moved to v6 with a breaking named-parameter API.
- Vercel PrivateLink to Neon is documented (needs Advanced Networking; pricing not found), so the 'IP Allow + $100 Static IPs is uneconomic' conclusion has a PrivateLink alternative that still requires Neon Scale.
- Neon root-branch restore from another branch leaves an undeletable backup branch; relevant to GDPR erasure and restore-as-incident advice.
- Resend changelog items since July: GitHub Secret Scanner auto-revokes exposed API keys (2026-07-16); Message-ID added to all email webhooks (2026-07-08); Email Suppressions dashboard/API (2026-07-28); Free tier 3 domains (2026-08-25); SSO (2026-09-01); Headless Webhook API (2026-09-16). Resend's subprocessor list includes Svix, Inngest, Cloudflare, Snowflake, Stripe, PlanetScale and Supabase, so webhook payloads and logs transit more parties than the dossier names.
- SES new-account default plan is Essentials ($0.16 per 1,000) from 2026-07-21, not a-la-carte $0.10.
- Neon status history has an older 'Next Page' that neither analyst nor verifier read, and the 6-incident count includes entries the vendor closed as no-impact (07-11) or unrelated to connectivity (05-07 scale-to-zero).
- The dossier's claim that every register quote was machine-checked verbatim is false for at least N55 (paraphrase); other register rows should be spot-checked before quoting them to counsel.

## tech-npm-versions
Dossier: `tech-npm-versions.md`

### Verifier lens: skeptic (confidence high; 27 claims confirmed)

I found nothing to refute. I re-fetched about 35 load-bearing claims from primary sources (npm registry, the Node.js and Vercel docs, Microsoft, Vite, Stripe, MCP, Drizzle, SimpleWebAuthn), and every version, date, engine or peer range, advisory and Vercel fact matched. I also reproduced the key experiments myself. A clean install of the 35 requested packages plus the two @types packages gave 494 packages with no ERESOLVE. npm audit gave the same 9 vulnerable packages, 6 moderate and 3 high, covering the same 17 advisories. The analyst's recommended pin set installs 260 packages with 0 vulnerabilities. Bare typescript@7 next to typescript-eslint 8.71.0 fails with ERESOLVE. skipLibCheck false gives 72 errors under both TS 7 and TS 6. Vitest's default include picks up Playwright specs. The esbuild override works, and drizzle-kit generate runs. Beyond the analyst's work, I ran tsc 7, tsc6, vite build, vitest, eslint, size-limit and drizzle-kit generate on official Node 24.21.0 and 26.10.0 binaries, which closes the analyst's "Node 24/26 not executed" gap. The analyst missed several things, listed under missed; none of them changes the recommendation (Node 24.x, the TS alias layout, skip @vercel/node, the esbuild override). Nuances: "all dev-only" depends on classifying @vercel/node and drizzle-kit as devDependencies, and the CVE-2026-39356 severity applies only if untrusted input reaches SQL identifiers or aliases.

Not confirmed:
- **corrected**: Vercel disables Node 20 for Builds/Functions on 2026-10-01 for new deployments (existing deployments keep running) -> 2026-10-01 (2026-11-01 only with undocumented env VERCEL_ALLOW_DISCONTINUED_NODEJS20=1) (Date and behavior confirmed (changelog 14 Jul 2026 and build-utils discontinueDate). Correction/addition: build-utils getOptions() honors env VERCEL_ALLOW_DISCONTINUED_NODEJS20=1, which moves the cutoff to 2026-11-01. It is undocumented in what I read, so do not rely on it.)
- **corrected**: npm audit on the all-latest set: 9 packages / 17 advisories (6 moderate, 3 high), all dev-only, from @vercel/node 16.0.2 and drizzle-kit 0.31.11 -> 9 packages / 17 advisories; dev-only only when @vercel/node and drizzle-kit are devDependencies (Counts, packages, GHSA ids and the bogus fixAvailable downgrades reproduced exactly. The 'all dev-only' label holds only because the analyst put @vercel/node and drizzle-kit in devDependencies; in my all-dependencies install, npm audit --omit=dev showed the same 9. True for the recommended manifest, not intrinsic to the packages.)
- **unverifiable**: Licenses: MPL-2.0 items (axe-core, @axe-core/playwright, lightningcss, edge-runtime) are dev-only; production tree is permissive (I confirmed MPL-2.0 for axe-core, @axe-core/playwright, lightningcss and edge-runtime@2.5.9 via the registry, but did not re-run the full-tree license census or the prod-vs-dev split, so the 'no copyleft in prod tree' part is not re-checked.)

Missed by the analyst:
- npm has moved on and only npm 10.9.7 was tested: npm dist-tag latest is 12.1.0 (12.0.0 on 2026-07-08, 12.1.0 on 2026-09-22; engines ^22.22.2 || ^24.15.0 || >=26). Node 24.21.0 and Node 26.10.0 bundle npm 11.19.0 and 11.19.1. I ran npm ci on the recommended lockfile with npm 11.19.0 and 12.1.0 on Node 24: 0 vulnerabilities, all checks pass.
- npm 11.19 and 12.1 block dependency install scripts by default (bundled docs: 'Dependency install scripts are blocked by default ... allowScripts'). npm ci warns that 3 packages have blocked scripts (esbuild x3 copies); fsevents (macOS) and, in the all-latest set, protobufjs also have install scripts. Everything still works with scripts blocked, but the scaffold needs an allowScripts policy and CI on Node 24 will print these warnings.
- Vercel docs map only Node 20 and 22 to npm 10 (package-managers page, last_updated 2026-08-11); which npm Vercel uses for Node 24 builds is undocumented. Vercel's default install for a package-lock.json project is npm install, not npm ci (Node.js runtime docs), so set installCommand to npm ci if lockfile-exact deploys are wanted.
- Vercel's builder source (@vercel/node 16.0.2 dist/index.js) resolves the project's typescript and, when that module lacks the legacy JS API (native TS 7), switches to registerNativeCompiler and spawns its tsc bin. Both the bare-TS7 and the alias layout are therefore handled by that builder version. The production builder version Vercel actually runs is not visible.
- @vercel/build-utils 14.13.2 includes env VERCEL_ALLOW_DISCONTINUED_NODEJS20=1, which extends the Node 20 cutoff from 2026-10-01 to 2026-11-01. It is undocumented in what I read, so treat it as a revocable escape hatch.
- Node 24.21.0 and 26.10.0 execution of the toolchain (tsc 7, tsc6, vite build, vitest, eslint, size-limit, drizzle-kit generate) is now verified with checksum-verified official binaries, closing the analyst's 'Node 24/26 not executed' gap. Live services and a real Vercel deployment remain untested.
- CVE-2026-39356 only affects apps that pass untrusted input into sql.identifier()/.as()/alias names; static schemas or allowlisted names are unaffected (advisory text). The >=0.45.2 floor stands, but real exposure depends on whether Mosshatch builds dynamic identifiers from request data. OSV also lists the 1.0 line as affected below 1.0.0-beta.20, so the RC (rc.4) is fixed.
- TypeScript dist-tag next is already 7.1.0-dev.20260929.1 and drizzle-orm has 1.0.0-rc.5-* snapshot tags; both the TS 7.1 API and Drizzle RC5 look close, which could change the alias layout and the drizzle-kit override decisions soon.
- The two SimpleWebAuthn 14.0.2 GHSAs are not in OSV (404), so their severity and affected ranges rest solely on the maintainer changelog; no independent advisory source exists yet.

## tech-vercel-platform
Dossier: `tech-vercel-platform.md`

Static IPs at $100, 1-day logs, Drains at $0.50/GB, and WAF 40/40 with per-region counters also hold, as do the Enterprise-only failover and the incident counts (133/3/28/81, reproduced exactly from the status pages). Two recommendations are stale. The advice to enable the team policy "Enforce Sensitive Environment Variables" is out of date. Vercel deprecated that policy on 2026-08-24 when it replaced the Sensitive toggle with Config and Secret types, and it introduced "Separate Production Secret Values". The Pro CDN "1M vs 10M" conflict is explained by Flat Rate CDN, which went GA for Pro on 2026-09-08 and which the analyst never mentioned. Since 2026-09-09, "All Deployments" Vercel Authentication (production protection) is also free on every plan. The analyst also missed some legal terms. Hobby and trial-Pro content can be used for AI model training. Terms §9.2 makes the customer responsible for PCI and says Vercel is not a payment processor. The 2026-06-04 terms update makes the customer responsible for the actions and costs of AI and third-party tools. Whether Workflows is GA stays unverifiable. A 2026-08-27 Vercel blog still calls Workflow v5 beta, and I found no GA entry in the 1,618-entry feed. The Pro multi-region count (3 vs 5) is unresolved but irrelevant to a single-region iad1 design. I could not check real-world cron miss rates, whether the cron caller passes Deployment Protection, or the ops billed per Queues message. The GitHub API returned 403, and WebSearch was out of budget.

Docs and the pricing page do not label Workflow Beta while Queues is labelled Beta. GA status itself cannot be established from primary sources, so keep it off the money path.)
