No changelog raising it to 5 exists in the feed. Leans 3 but unresolved; immaterial for a single-region design.)

Docs page (2026-08-28) still shows the old wording, so docs are inconsistent. Unknown whether Secret is still barred from Development. Config values remain readable by anyone with project access.)

The pricing page's '10M / month included; then $2 per 1M' is the legacy on-demand mode. The dossier never mentions Flat Rate CDN.)

Missed by the analyst:
- Terms s3 (Your Content): on a Hobby plan or a trial Pro plan you agree Vercel may use Your Content to train AI/ML models and share it with third parties. Paid Pro has Model Training off by default (opt-in), and it can be opted out via team settings or an Enterprise plan. Anything deployed during a Pro trial or on Hobby (code and any embedded data) is in scope. https://vercel.com/legal/terms
- Terms s9.2 (PCI): 'Vercel is not a payment processor'; the customer is responsible for PCI DSS compliance and agrees to comply with Vercel's Documentation on implementing the Services for payment processing. Vercel's compliance page recommends a processor-hosted iframe/redirect so card data never touches Vercel infrastructure. This is a stronger customer-side obligation than the dossier's 'Vercel supports PCI'. https://vercel.com/legal/terms and https://vercel.com/docs/security/compliance
- Legal-terms update 2026-06-04: customer is responsible for actions, and any costs, of AI Functionality and Third-Party Tools (CI, agents, scripts) granted account access; Vercel may charge accrued fees ahead of the billing cycle for unusual or suspicious usage (partial invoices); new customer representations on third-party credentials and OFAC sanctions; DPF complaints now go to VeraSafe. https://vercel.com/changelog/updates-to-legal-terms-june-2026
- Threshold billing / partial invoices enabled for Pro teams on 2026-06-10: mid-cycle invoices once on-demand usage hits a threshold. Relevant to a small team's cash-flow surprises and interacts with Spend Management, which excludes seats and add-ons. https://vercel.com/changelog/threshold-billing-is-now-enabled-for-pro-teams

Projects can be moved to a 'Flex CDN' network but must use Vercel DNS. https://vercel.com/docs/pricing/flat-rate-cdn

This gives a free way to keep a pre-launch production site private, but the dossier does not analyze its effect on cron, Stripe/Resend/registrar webhooks and agent API callers on the production domain. https://vercel.com/changelog/protect-production-deployments-for-free-on-every-plan
- Workflow blog 2026-08-27 states Workflow v5 is in beta and v6 is planned, and 'Workflow steps now support extended function durations' (2026-07-24) is itself beta. These are the freshest signals on Workflow maturity. https://vercel.com/blog/the-best-workflow-engine-is-a-programming-language
- DPA facts the analyst left unopened: dated 2026-03-17 (effective 2026-03-31), SCCs Modules 1-3 and UK IDTA incorporated, primary processing in the US, subprocessor list at security.vercel.com, breach notice 'without undue delay'. https://vercel.com/legal/dpa
- Vercel Passport went GA on 2026-07-31 but remains Enterprise-only per the deployment-protection pricing table, so it is not an option on Pro. https://vercel.com/docs/deployment-protection/usage-and-pricing
- Vercel's own docs are internally inconsistent in several places (sensitive-env docs vs 2026-08-24 changelog; Node versions page last_updated 2026-02-27 still lists 20.x; region page 5 vs Fluid page 3). Recommend the launch checklist cite changelog dates alongside doc URLs and re-check before launch.

## tech-webauthn-passkeys
Dossier: `tech-webauthn-passkeys.md`

### Verifier lens: skeptic (confidence high; 14 claims confirmed)

I re-fetched about 25 primary sources directly and re-ran the library claims in my own harness. Nearly every load-bearing fact holds: package versions, the Node 22 floor, library defaults, the "stateless, replay is yours" finding, WebAuthn L3 REC 2026-08-25, NIST 800-63B-4 numbers, OWASP quotes, browser-support versions (MDN BCD 8.1.3) and the Unit 42 and Proofpoint dates. There is one correction and several important misses. (1) The 30 min idle / 12 h absolute session values are inside NIST's AAL2 SHOULDs but outside OWASP's own ranges (idle 2-5 min for high-value apps, absolute 4-8 h). (2) The Unit 42 research is more serious than summarised. A "Silver" variant makes the cloud authenticator return UV=1 even when the RP requires and validates UV, and a "Golden" variant extracts all synced private keys. As of 2026-08-03 Google had only removed the key from logs and the key cannot be rotated. So UV=1 is not proof of a local human gesture for Google-synced credentials on Windows, and the opt-in BE=0 mode matters more than the dossier says. (3) The ICANN Transfer Policy section on ClientTransferProhibited and AuthInfo codes (I.A.5.3) forbids making unlock or auth-code release more restrictive than contact or nameserver changes. This directly constrains the proposed transfer-out veto window and the asymmetric 7-day hold. (4) Node 22 reaches end of life on 2027-04-30 and Vercel deprecates Node 20 on 2026-10-01, so launch on Node 24. The only claims I could not re-derive are the Chrome 128 ROR version, Apple's own signCount 0 statement, and the api.w3.org status call (Cloudflare 403). WebSearch was exhausted and two of my own Bash calls were denied, so I did not read dossier sections 5-15 directly and audited them only through the structured summary.

Not confirmed:
- **unverifiable**: Related Origin Requests ship in Chrome/Edge 128 (Only passkeys.dev (last updated Sep 21, 2026) gives 128+. web.dev says only that Chrome supports it. The Chrome 128 release pages are JS-rendered without ROR text and the chromestatus entry is stale ('No active development'). The 5-label practical maximum is confirmed on passkeys.dev.)
- **corrected**: Session design idle 30 min / absolute 12 h is consistent with the cited guidance -> NIST-conformant only; suggest ~15 min idle / 8 h absolute or document the deviation from OWASP (2-5 min idle for high-value, 4-8 h absolute) (Consistent with NIST AAL2 SHOULDs (<= 1 h idle, <= 24 h absolute) but not with OWASP Session Management, which the dossier also cites: idle 'Common idle timeouts ranges are 2-5 minutes for high-value applications and 15-30 minutes for low risk', absolute 4-8 hours for a full-day office application. 12 h is outside that range and 30 min is the top of the low-risk band. A registrar is high-value. Defensible because the seven risky actions use per-action step-up, but it should be labelled a deliberate deviation.)
- **corrected**: Unit 42 (2026-08-03): malware on a Windows endpoint can take over accounts protected by Google-synced passkeys, bypass user verification and extract all synced private keys -> UV enforcement blocks only the base variant; Silver and Golden defeat UV for synced (BE=1) Google credentials on Windows. Hardened BE=0 mode is the only RP-side mitigation. (Date, author scope (Google Password Manager in Chrome on Windows with TPM, malware already present, responsibly disclosed) confirmed. But the dossier understates it. The base Pass-ta-key attack fails against RPs that require AND validate UV (GitHub rejects; eBay was fixed). The 'Silver' variant registers an attacker-controlled UV key with the cloud authenticator (no attestation check on new UV keys), producing UV=1 assertions 'even when user verification is enforced and validated', reusable from the attacker's machine. The 'Golden' variant reads the security-domain secret from Chrome memory and decrypts all synced passkeys, with 'no way to rotate or revoke the SDS'. Google only removed the SDS from log output so far. This partly answers the analyst's open question on vendor fixes: not fixed as of the article.)
- **unverifiable**: Synced passkeys commonly report signCount 0 (Touch ID / iCloud specifically) (General statement supported by SimpleWebAuthn docs ('certain high profile authenticators, like Touch ID on macOS, to always return 0'), Unit 42 ('synchronized passkey systems ... constant signCount') and spec 6.1.1 (counter optional). Apple's own statement was not reachable, so the Apple-specific claim stays unverified.)
- **corrected**: ICANN interaction with delaying unlock / transfer-out / auth-code release was not researched (dossier lists it as needing a lawyer) -> Any delay on unlock/auth-code release must not be more restrictive than contact/NS changes (Transfer Policy I.A.5.3); treat as a hard constraint, not just a lawyer flag (I checked the current ICANN Transfer Policy (updated 21 Feb 2024, implement by 21 Aug 2025). Section I.A.5.3: registrars may not use any mechanism to remove ClientTransferProhibited or release the AuthInfo code that is 'more restrictive than the mechanisms used for changing any aspect of the Registered Name Holder's contact or name server information'. 5.1/5.2: five calendar days if no self-service facility. 5.4: no refusal over a payment dispute. Change of Registrant triggers a 60-day transfer lock (II.C.2). So the dossier's optional transfer-out veto window, and a 7-day hold that covers transfer-out and NS changes but not contact changes, could breach parity unless applied symmetrically or cleared by counsel and the upstream registrar. Section 3.7.1 permits denial for 'evidence of fraud'.)

Missed by the analyst:
- Unit 42 Silver and Golden attacks defeat UV enforcement for Google-synced (BE=1) credentials on Windows (attacker registers its own UV key; SDS extractable from Chrome memory; SDS cannot be rotated; Google only removed it from logs as of 2026-08-03). So 'UV is the second factor' fails against endpoint malware on that stack, and the dossier's 4.1 'what step-up cannot defend' list should add it. Promote the opt-in BE=0 hardened mode for high-value accounts.
- ICANN Transfer Policy I.A.5.3 parity rule (unlock and auth-code release may not be more restrictive than contact or nameserver changes), the 5-calendar-day rule in 5.1/5.2, and the Change of Registrant 60-day transfer lock (II.C.2) if account email doubles as registrant email. This bears directly on the optional transfer-out veto window and the asymmetric 7-day post-recovery hold.
- Session timeouts (30 min idle / 12 h absolute) are looser than the OWASP ranges the dossier itself cites (2-5 min idle for high-value apps; 4-8 h absolute). Only NIST AAL2 is met.
- BE=0 is an unauthenticated, self-reported bit under attestation 'none'. NIST says RPs SHOULD use attestation when available to gauge confidence in a syncable authenticator. The hardened mode should consider direct attestation for security keys (SimpleWebAuthn supports MDS) while keeping 'none' as the default.
- Node lifecycle: Node 22 EOL 2027-04-30, Node 24 EOL 2028-04-30 (nodejs Release schedule.json); Vercel deprecates Node 20 on 2026-10-01 (Vercel changelog, 14 Jul 2026). Start on Node 24.
- On a PQC-capable runtime (likely Node 24 on Vercel) SimpleWebAuthn 14 prepends ML-DSA-44 (-48) to the default supportedAlgorithmIDs and throws PQCNotSupportedError when verifying such a credential on a runtime without PQC. The analyst's harness ran only on Node 22.22.2 (list [-8,-7,-257]); pinning [-7,-257] in both generate and verify should be mandatory, not optional.
- npm's advisory database did not flag 14.0.1 even though the CHANGELOG says 14.0.2 fixed two GHSA advisories (GHSA-2g3p-m8c9-hhwh, GHSA-j3h4-m3m2-7p7j), so npm audit cannot enforce the '>= 14.0.2' pin. It shows no advisories for 14.0.3 as of 2026-09-29. GitHub advisories were not reachable to check for newer ones.
- The WebAuthn L3 Recommendation text states 'no substantive changes since the CR Snapshot of 26 May 2026', so nothing in the final REC changes the dossier. L3 deprecates rp.name, while SimpleWebAuthn still requires rpName (no action needed).
- OWASP's Passkey sheet says not to expose raw credential IDs in UIs, URLs or logs, and to rotate the session identifier at authentication. Both are consistent with the design but not stated as checklist items.
- Not resolved (search budget exhausted): Chrome 128 as first ROR version from a vendor source, Apple's own signCount statement, FIDO CXP/CXF final status (only a 2024 Working Draft was retrievable), SquareX extension-hijack research, and Entra passkey sync status after 2025-11-03.

## tech-webgl-three
Dossier: `tech-webgl-three.md`

### Verifier lens: skeptic (confidence high; 29 claims confirmed)

I tried to refute the dossier by re-fetching about 30 load-bearing claims myself. I found no refutation. Almost every number, date and quote reproduced. I re-ran the key three r186.1 experiments in headless Chromium (SwiftShader, so API semantics only) and re-bundled with esbuild. Two claims need correcting. Budget B8 ("MSAA off", labelled "cited") is not what Arm says, since Arm recommends 4x MSAA. The drei PerformanceMonitor bounds switch to [60,100] above 100 Hz, not a fixed [40,60]. Three new facts sharpen the picture. WebKit's aggressive-thermal rAF halving is an internal setting that defaults to off, so by default only Low Power Mode halves rAF. Safari/iOS 27 now exists. WebKit's canvas noise injection has a broader trigger than known trackers. The Phase 0 decision (WebGL2 + WebGLRenderer + ShaderMaterial) stands. Device-only risks stay unverifiable: thermals, ALU cost, mediump, iOS memory ceilings, numeric context limits.

Not confirmed:
- **corrected**: B8: 'MSAA off' budget is 'cited' by Arm, Qualcomm and the three default -> B8 = MSAA off as engineering judgement, not cited; Arm recommends 4x MSAA on tile-based GPUs (Arm's MSAA page says 'Use 4x MSAA because it is not expensive and provides good image quality improvements' and 'Do not use more than 4x MSAA without checking performance'. Qualcomm says only 'consider no anti-aliasing' and that MSAAx2 is nearly free. Antialias:false with analytic fwidth AA is a reasonable judgement (DPR 1.75 supersampling; ANGLE default-framebuffer MSAA cost unmeasured) but is not vendor-cited. The dossier body F40 quotes Arm accurately; only the B8 table basis/type overstates.)
- **corrected**: F41: drei PerformanceMonitor defaults (250 ms x 10, threshold 0.75, step 0.1, bounds [40,60]) -> bounds = refreshrate > 100 ? [60,100] : [40,60] (drei 10.7.9 core/PerformanceMonitor.js: iterations 10, ms 250, threshold 0.75, step 0.1, factor 0.5, flipflops Infinity, but bounds = refreshrate => refreshrate > 100 ? [60,100] : [40,60], with refreshrate inferred from the max fps seen. The dossier body states this correctly; the structured summary flattens it to [40,60], which matters on 90/120 Hz phones.)

Missed by the analyst:
- Safari 27 / iOS 27 have shipped or are in beta. caniuse-db (2026-09-28) has ios_saf 27.0-27.2 and safari 27/27.1/27.2/TP columns (iOS 27 usage still 0%), and Apple has Safari 27 release notes plus a 27.2 beta. The 27 notes show only WebGL fixes: context-state reset on context loss, and a regression where an opaque WebGL canvas hid stacked canvases. Safari 26.5 also fixed WebGL shader compilation of NaN/infinity values. No conclusion changes, but the 75.45% iOS 26.x mix will shift, and the device matrix should include iOS 27 and early-iOS-26 builds.
- WebKit's own Safari 26.0 features post says 'WebGPU supersedes WebGL on macOS, iOS, iPadOS, and visionOS and is preferred for new sites and web apps.' This is a direction-of-travel signal the dossier does not quote. It does not change Phase 0, because three's ShaderMaterial is unsupported in WebGPURenderer.
- WebKit's aggressive-thermal rAF halving is off by default (RespondToThermalPressureAggressively is internal, default false; plain ThermalMitigation is not in the half-speed set). On iOS, the software-visible rAF halving is Low Power Mode (and visually-idle/cross-origin-idle), not thermal state. Sustained thermal throttling therefore shows up only as slower GPU frames, and the adaptive controller must react to frame times. This resolves one of the analyst's open items, for WebKit main only.
- Canvas readback noise in WebKit is broader than 'known trackers'. Document::noiseInjectionPolicies() adds Minimal when AdvancedPrivacyProtections::FingerprintingProtections is on (or a site quirk applies), and CanvasBase applies noise post-processing to the full buffer for non-2D contexts, including WebGL. Apple's public 26.0 text describes only 'known fingerprinting scripts'. Whether Fingerprinting Protections is on by default for normal or private browsing on iOS 26/27 was not verified. Snapshot code should assert on decoded output and not assume exact pixels.
- High-refresh phones. Chromium main cc/base/features.cc has kThrottleMainFrameTo60Hz 'Enabled on Android, after a field trial showed improvements' (WebView variant disabled by default; the eligibility rule was not traced). Chrome Android is likely capped at 60 Hz main frames, but WebViews, in-app browsers and other engines may run rAF at 90/120 Hz. An explicit frame cap and refresh-rate-aware controller bounds (drei switches at >100 Hz) are prudent, because the 16.7 ms budget silently becomes 8.3 ms otherwise.
- Chromium picks the context to evict by least-recent flush id, not creation order, so an idle background WebGL canvas is the one lost. The numeric max_active_webgl_contexts still could not be located in Chromium source, so the mobile limit stays unverified.
- The Qualcomm guide also says to prefer alpha blending over discard when combining passes, while listing blending among LRZ/Early-Z disablers, and advises doing discard/blend work late in the frame. The dossier's flat 'no blending, no discard on full-screen layers' is consistent but less nuanced than the vendor text.
- Vendor draw-call and MSAA advice (Arm <500 draw calls, 4x MSAA is cheap) is for native GLES/Vulkan. WebGL draw calls carry extra validation and ANGLE/IPC overhead, and default-framebuffer MSAA resolve cost through ANGLE on Mali/Adreno is unmeasured, so both the 60-call cap and the MSAA-off choice are judgement calls, not vendor limits.
- Android WebGPU coverage source is stale. The web.dev post is dated 2025-11-25, the gpuweb wiki says Xclipse 'probably 154' and Chrome Android 154 is now current, and no Chrome-team post after 'New in WebGPU 146' (Feb 2026) was findable. The Android WebGPU GPU-vendor gate may already be wider than stated; re-check before relying on it in a WebGPU-migration argument.

## tech-wire-it-recipes
Dossier: `tech-wire-it-recipes.md`

### Verifier lens: skeptic (confidence high; 31 claims confirmed)

I re-fetched about 45 claims from vendor docs, OpenAPI/swagger specs, changelogs and RFCs on 2026-09-29. None were refuted. The Vercel, Netlify, Neon, Resend, Porkbun, Cloudflare, Route 53, Gandi, RFC and HSTS numbers all match, and my own DoH probe reproduced the non-monotonic TTLs. Four things need correcting or adding. (1) Resend domain creation is not always free: the domain quota is Free 3 (raised from 1 on 2026-08-25), Pro 10, Scale 1,000, with a $20/mo add-on for 100 more. (2) Neon project-scoped API keys are Editor-level and cannot create or delete projects, so `mode: create` and the project-delete rollback are incompatible with a pasted project-scoped key. (3) The DMARC RFC the dossier would check, RFC 7489, was obsoleted by RFC 9989 in May 2026. (4) Many TLDs beyond .dev and .app are HSTS-preloaded, so the list must not be hard-coded. Vercel's own KB also warns that the newer 216.198.79.1 pool has regional reachability problems (Brazil, Oman) and recommends www as primary. The Resend CNAME variant for domains created after Aug 2026 is in effect today and is the likely default for new domains. Still untestable: whether a project-scoped Vercel token can call the domain config endpoint, the CNAME-variant record values, and Vercel Functions UDP/53 egress. Notes are in the verify file.

Not confirmed:
- **corrected**: Neon API keys: personal, organization (admins only), project-scoped (admins only); secret shown once (presented as a usable paste-a-key connect path) -> Project-scoped key = Editor on one project; no project create, no project delete. (The three key types and admin-only creation are right, but the dossier omits the capability limits. Project-scoped keys have Editor access to one project, 'cannot delete the project or manage who can access it', and cannot 'create new projects'. So a project-scoped key supports mode=reuse only. mode=create needs a personal key (with org_id) or an org key, and the Neon rollback DELETE /projects/{id} cannot be done with a project-scoped key.)
- **corrected**: POST /domains is a free, reversible provider-side create (costsMoney:false in the sample recipe) -> Conditional: free only while the team has spare domain slots (Free 3, Pro 10, Scale 1,000; +100 for $20/mo). (Reversible yes, free only within plan quota. The pricing page and the 2026-08-25 changelog give Free 3 domains (raised from 1), Pro 10, Scale 1,000, Enterprise contractual, plus a 'Domains - $20 / mo' add-on for 100 more on Pro/Scale. A team at its quota cannot create the domain without upgrading. Read the domain count first and mark the step conditionally billable.)
- **corrected**: DMARC placement for subdomain senders was unverified and referenced RFC 7489 -> Cite RFC 9989 (May 2026), not RFC 7489. (RFC 9989 (DMARC, Proposed Standard, May 2026) obsoletes RFC 7489 and RFC 9091. Its DNS Tree Walk (s4.10) means a record at _dmarc.<org domain> covers subdomains unless one publishes its own. Multiple DMARC records at one name are all discarded, so the skip_if_exists policy is right.)
- **unverifiable**: Vercel Functions outbound UDP/53 is undocumented (unverified item) (The functions limitations page mentions only TCP sockets and HTTP requests under file descriptors, with no statement on UDP. Consistent with the analyst's finding; needs a real test.)

Missed by the analyst:
- Vercel's own KB warns that the newer 216.198.79.1 anycast pool has reported regional reachability failures (Brazil, Oman) and recommends www as primary with the apex redirecting to it; the recipe should default to www-primary for Vercel and Netlify, not just offer it.
- Neon project-scoped API keys are Editor-level: they cannot create or delete projects. Recipe connection metadata needs canCreateProject/canDeleteProject flags, and mode=create or project-delete rollback must be refused for such keys.
- Resend domain quota makes POST /domains conditionally billable (Free 3, raised from 1 on 2026-08-25; Pro 10; Scale 1,000; +100 for $20/mo add-on). The plan phase needs a GET /domains count precondition, and costsMoney should be conditional.
- Resend partially_verified in the CNAME variant means sending works but there is no fallback server. The status derivation must not treat it as failed.
- Resend became a GitHub secret-scanning partner on 2026-07-16 and auto-revokes exposed re_ API keys. Never write RESEND_API_KEY to a repo, and handle a sudden 401 from a revoked key with a re-mint path.
- DMARC is now RFC 9989 (May 2026), which obsoletes RFC 7489 and RFC 9091. It uses a DNS Tree Walk, and duplicate DMARC records at one name are all discarded.
- The HSTS-preloaded TLD set is large (page, new, day, foo, prof, nexus, ing, meet, phd, rsvp, boo, dad, esq, channel, hangout, mov, zip, bank, insurance, and Google-owned brand TLDs). The cert gate should come from a lookup, not a hard-coded .dev/.app pair.
- Vercel GET /v9/domains/{domain}/verification returns {txtRecord, verificationDomain} and POST /v9/domains/{domain}/claim completes team-level claim. The TXT value is API-supplied.
- Vercel sensitive env vars can only be created in Preview and Production, are write-only, and can be made sensitive only by remove-and-re-add. Rollback cannot restore prior values; it can only delete what the recipe created.
- Neon 'scoped credentials' (Credentials API) cover Object Storage and AI Gateway only, not Postgres. The Neon changelog (2026-09-11 Claimable Neon, 2026-09-18 backend GA) shows an agent-provisioning direction worth re-checking for partner/OAuth stance before Phase 4.
- Long TXT values (DKIM keys; Netlify DNS documents 255 characters per TXT) need string-splitting support in any registrar DNS write path if a provider moves to 2048-bit keys. Design caution only, not sourced beyond the Netlify doc line.

## tld-registry-facts
Dossier: `tld-registry-facts.md`

### Verifier lens: skeptic (confidence high; 13 claims confirmed)

The dossier holds up on every load-bearing point I could re-check against a primary source. I refuted nothing outright and found no 2026 announcement that changes the .com price, the ICANN fee, the .io status or the .ai figures. Verisign's 10-Q, its 2026 SEC filing list, the .com RA text, ICANN's FY26 fee notice and the FY27 budget, the IANA records, the treaty text, ICANN's Consensus Policies page and the ICANN Bylaws all match. Corrections and open items. (1) The .ai 2-year minimum predates 2026-03-05; only the $70 to $80 per year step is dated then. (2) The dossier's line that expired .ai names are auctioned via Dynadot is outdated; DNW 2026-06-29 says the registry's Namecheap partnership. (3) The Chagos update needs reframing. The legislation was shelved after the US withdrew support. On 2026-09-23 the Guardian and BBC report the deal is on hold and "can't go ahead without US support". "UK will rework" is accurate but overstates momentum, and The Times headline (2026-09-24) says ministers believe it is dead. (4) The FY27 registrar-fee vote result is still not findable: ICANN's announcements list to 2026-09-24 has no FY27 approval item. The FY27 budget adds that if registrars do not approve, registries pay the fee and may pass it through (Base RA 6.3). (5) The dossier omits the .com RA 7.3(d)(iii) cost pass-through route and the 30-day carve-out in Base RA 2.10(b) for renewal increases already noticed within the previous 12 months. (6) All .ai, .io and .studio wholesale figures and the .studio +29% (2026-10-06) and .io +12% (2027-01-19) steps remain third-party only. I could not re-check them against registry documents, because none are public.

Not confirmed:
- **unverifiable**: FY27 keeps the $0.20 transaction fee (vote result not found) (The FY27 budget (May 2026) says 'If approved, these fees will be billed at $0.20 per transaction', that ICANN 'does not intend to raise fees in FY27', and that the fees 'reflect the same rates as in the FY26 budget'. The Board adopted the FY27 plans at its 3 May 2026 meeting (announcement 2026-05-11). I paged ICANN's announcements listing (about 2026-02-28 to 2026-09-24): no FY27 registrar-fee approval notice exists. The budget also says that if registrars do not approve, registries pay ICANN and may pass the fee to registrars (Base RA 6.3), so it could show up inside wholesale. The amount is the same either way.)
- **unverifiable**: .ai wholesale is $80 per year ($160 per 2-year minimum), effective 2026-03-05 (No registry notice is public (nic.ai FAQ dated 2025-04-24 has no prices; the registrar portal is login-gated). Strong secondary agreement: DNW 2026-02-02 ($70 to $80/yr, $140 to $160 per 2 years, effective March 5, +14%), Domain Incite 2026-02-03 (+14.3%), DNW 2026-06-29 ('wholesale cost is $80 per year, with a two-year minimum'), the Cloudflare at-cost mirror $80.00 (updated 2026-09-19), and the Porkbun API 82.70 per year. No 2026 announcement after February changes it.)
- **corrected**: .ai requires a 2-year minimum on registration and renewal, transfers add 2 years, and this applies 'since 2026-03-05' -> 2-year minimum term is older than 2026-03-05; only the $80/yr price is effective 2026-03-05 (The minimum and the transfer add-on are confirmed by Porkbun ('minimum term of 2 years for registrations and renewals. .AI transfers include a 2 year renewal'), Dynadot, and Cloudflare docs ('.ai domains require a minimum 2-year registration for transfers', page updated Apr 24, 2026). But the minimum predates 2026-03-05: the February 2026 articles already describe it at the old $70 per year price. Only the price step is dated 2026-03-05. Identity Digital's transfer-adds-2-years change dates from 2025-01-15 (DNW 2025-01-23).)
- **corrected**: Expired .ai domains are auctioned through Dynadot (Jan 2025 statement; current process unverified) -> Expired .ai names go through the registry's Namecheap auction partnership (per DNW 2026-06-29); details unpublished (DNW 2026-06-29 says an expired .ai will either be 'auctioned off through the .ai registry's partnership with Namecheap or fully expire'. The Dynadot-run auction cited in the dossier is outdated. The nic.ai FAQ still links to '.AI auctions' without detail.)
- **unverifiable**: No ICANN, IANA or registry (Identity Digital/ICB) statement on .io continuity is newer than ICANN's 2024-11-14 blog (Absence claim. I found no counterexample: ICANN announcements (about 2026-02-28 to 2026-09-24) and blogs page 1 have no .io or Chagos item; IANA record unchanged; nic.io home, FAQ, terms, rules and policy have no continuity statement; Identity Digital newsroom (latest 2026-09-23) has none; Domain Incite .io tag newest is 2026-04-13. ISO 3166 status of IO could not be checked (iso.org Cloudflare challenge, not worked around).)
- **unverifiable**: Third-party tracker lists a +29.0% .studio wholesale increase effective 2026-10-06 (values masked) (Re-rendered in Chromium today: /tld/studio shows 'Scheduled Change +29.0% ... Effective 2026-10-06' and /upcoming lists .studio +29.0% among about 100 Identity Digital gTLDs dated Oct 6, 2026. Dollar values are masked and no registry or registrar notice was found, so it stays third-party only.)
- **unverifiable**: Tracker shows a scheduled +12.0% .io increase on 2027-01-19 (values masked) (/tld/io and /upcoming reproduce '+12.0% Jan 19, 2027' (also .info +11.9% the same date); tracking started 2026-01-19 with '+11.1% since tracking started'. No registry notice found. Cloudflare .io is $32 first year and $50 renewal on today's mirror.)

Missed by the analyst:
- FY27 budget note (appendix 8.1): if registrars do not approve the variable fees, registries pay ICANN and may pass them through to registrars (Base RA 6.3), so the $0.20 could be folded into wholesale rather than billed as a registrar fee. The Board adopted the FY27 OP&B on 3 May 2026 (announcement 2026-05-11), but no FY27 registrar-fee vote announcement exists in ICANN's listing to 2026-09-24.
- ICANN updated the Registration Data Policy on 2026-05-12 with a 24-hour response requirement for urgent disclosure requests for non-public gTLD registration data. It is not in effect until a law-enforcement authentication mechanism exists. The dossier lists only the 2025-08-21 effective date.
- The FY27 budget says the most recent RAA version was approved in January 2024, but the dossier's reseller checklist cites the 2013 RAA text. The 2024 amendments (DNS abuse) need checking before the obligations list is finalised. I did not open the 2024 RAA text.
- The .com RA 7.3(d)(iii) cost pass-through route (up to 7% for a new Consensus Policy or documented extraordinary expense), and the 7.2(d) CPI adjustment of ICANN registry-level fees, so the $0.25 is a floor and the 7% ceilings are not absolute.
- Base RA 2.10(b) carve-out: 30 days' notice is enough for a renewal increase up to a price already noticed within the previous 12 months. Separately, ICANN lists Global Amendments and a Renewal Notice for .dev/.app/.studio and a Board-approved Base gTLD RA dated 2024-01-21, so the dossier's 2014 base RA text may not be the operative agreement.
- Expired .ai names now go through the registry's Namecheap auction partnership, not Dynadot (DNW 2026-06-29). The same article notes a growing wave of first .ai renewals at $160+ per 2 years.
- Chagos framing: the Guardian related-story headline dated 2026-04-11 says the UK shelved its legislation after the US withdrew support; on 2026-09-23 the Guardian and BBC report the deal on hold pending US support, and The Times headline (2026-09-24, paywalled) says ministers believe it is dead. The reworking line overstates momentum.
- Verisign announced a .net increase from $10.91 to $12.00 effective 2027-03-01 (8-K, 2026-08-27). It is not in scope for the six TLDs but shows Verisign gives the full notice period, so the next .com step should be announced about six months ahead.
- ICANN approved the 2026 Base RA on 2026-03-12 and the 2026 new-gTLD round closed 2026-08-12 with more than 1,600 applications. It does not obviously affect the six TLDs, but whether .dev/.app/.studio move to the new RA on renewal is unknown.
- Dynadot's per-TLD pages now render currency by client geography (CNY for my client), so the dossier's Dynadot USD figures are not reproducible everywhere. Cloudflare's own product page was unreachable (challenge host blocked by egress policy), so Cloudflare prices still rest on the cfdomainpricing.com mirror.

## tm-hatchkind
Dossier: `tm-hatchkind.md`

### Verifier lens: skeptic (confidence high; 36 claims confirmed)

I re-fetched the USPTO, TMview, EUIPO, RDAP, DNS and standards sources myself. The core facts hold. There is no HATCHKIND, HATCHKINDS, HATCH KIND or HATCH-KIND mark in USPTO, TMview (81 offices) or EUIPO, and HATCHKINZ (Reg. 7054458, class 28) is the closest hit. The HATCH crowding counts reproduce exactly (USPTO 23 live solo marks, 18 registered and 5 pending; by class 9=6, 35=9, 36=5, 42=10, 45=2; 83 live marks containing HATCH; TMview 159 records, 104 live). Both .com domains are as RDAP describes, created 2026-09-29T17:48:38Z at Namecheap. The MDN, web.dev and Google-blog cookie and same-site statements are accurate. Corrections: (1) for .com the 60-day post-creation transfer block is a registry-enforced rule (.com Registry Agreement Appendix 7), not just registrar discretion, so transfers cannot complete before about 2026-11-28; (2) Gmail's baseline sender rules (SPF or DKIM, spam rate under 0.30%) apply to all senders since 2024-02-01, and only DMARC is specific to the 5,000+/day tier; (3) "Firefox is still considering Related Origin Requests" is a January 2026 snapshot and looks stale (Mozilla standards-position issue #1052 closed 2026-03-11 labelled "position: positive", per a GitHub search listing whose body I could not read); (4) the "63 HATCH-compounds" count does not reproduce (I get 60); (5) the summary's Amazon source_url for the open-web claim is not where that quote came from. Unverifiable for me: open-web absence (WebSearch budget exhausted; I did not scrape around it), WIPO and UK IPO direct searches (blocked; not bypassed), .co by RDAP, and domain ownership. The Google-blog "precedent" supports app-versus-content isolation but not card-to-card isolation, since Google uses per-user or per-document subdomains where documents must be isolated. Counsel is still needed for anything legal.

Not confirmed:
- **corrected**: 63 live HATCH-compounds (single tokens starting with HATCH) in the five classes. -> about 60 (60 with alphanumeric tokenization, 61 counting cancelled classes, 66 with whitespace tokenization) (I could not reproduce 63; the count is definition-sensitive and does not change the conclusion.)
- **unverifiable**: Open-web search found no exact 'hatchkind' page; 'Hatch' is used by many unrelated brands. (WebSearch refused all three of my queries (session budget 200 of 200 exhausted) and I did not route around it. The summary's source_url (an Amazon store page) is not the origin of the quoted text, which is the search tool's own summary. Non-search checks I could do found nothing named hatchkind: iTunes software search, Bluesky handle resolution (hatchkind.bsky.social unresolved, control OK), and GitHub user search.)
- **corrected**: Related Origin Requests are supported only in Chrome and Safari, Firefox is still considering the feature, and Chrome caps at 5 labels. -> Chrome and Safari support it; the web.dev 'Firefox still considering' text is dated January 2026 and is likely stale as of 2026-09-29. Mozilla standards-position issue #1052 'WebAuthn related origins' is closed (2026-03-11) with label 'position: positive'; Firefox implementation status is unverified. Chrome cap of 5 labels is confirmed. (The web.dev page still carries the January 2026 sentence, so the analyst quoted it faithfully. The issue body was unreadable to me (repository not allowed for the GitHub tool), so only metadata from the search listing is used. The design conclusion (no passkey UI on hatchkind.com) does not depend on this.)
- **corrected**: Resend recommends sending from subdomains; Gmail's bulk sender rules (over 5,000/day since 2024-02-01) require DMARC and spam rate below 0.30%. -> Resend quote confirmed. Gmail: since 2024-02-01 ALL senders must meet SPF or DKIM, PTR, TLS, spam rate below 0.3% and RFC 5322; only senders above 5,000/day additionally need SPF and DKIM together and DMARC (policy may be none). (The dossier's section 5.10 says Gmail's rules apply above 5,000 messages per day, which understates the baseline tier.)
- **corrected**: ICANN Transfer Policy lets a registrar deny a transfer requested within 60 days of creation, so moving the two domains may be blocked until about 2026-11-28. -> For .com it is a registry-enforced block: .com Registry Agreement Appendix 7 says transfers 'may not occur ... at any other time within the first 60 days after the initial registration', enforced by the SRS. Earliest transfer is therefore about 2026-11-28, not merely at registrar discretion. (Policy item 3.7.5 verified on the ICANN page; the registry agreement text fetched from itp.cdn.icann.org, and an earlier verifier reports the same text.)

Missed by the analyst:
- Python's PyPA 'Hatch' project manager and 'hatchling' build backend (PyPI hatch 1.18.1, hatchling 1.32.4; also an npm package named hatch) are a real developer-community name collision for a CLI- and agent-facing product; the dossier's list of Hatch web uses omits them.
- 15 U.S.C. 1112 says classification is for USPTO administration and does not limit or extend rights, so 'class 45 is the least crowded' is arithmetic only; likelihood of confusion turns on relatedness of goods and services, and HATCH LABS (class 45), Hatch Ltd. (EU and UK 2026 filings including 42 and 45) and Playful Software (42) sit adjacent to registrar and software services.
- TMview coverage is uneven: CNIPA data is dated 2026-09-03, India and 8 other offices 2026-05-04/05, Jordan 2022-07 and Russia 2022-02, so 'covers 81 offices' needs that qualifier.
- Current domain state matters for launch: http:// on both apex domains 302-redirects via Namecheap URL Forward, www.hatchkind.com is a Namecheap parking page that loads third-party parity.domains and Plausible scripts, and HTTPS to the apex fails (TLS reset, no certificate), so share links and OG unfurls cannot work until hosting and certificates exist.
- A .com transfer within 60 days of registration is blocked by the registry (Appendix 7), not just discretionary, so the reseller-API-registration alternative is the only route to an upstream registrar before about 2026-11-28.
- The DMCA agent designation renews every three years (37 CFR 201.38(c)(4)) and costs $6 per the Cornell copy of 37 CFR 201.3; the dossier left both unverified.
- The dossier's Firefox statement on Related Origin Requests is a January 2026 snapshot; Mozilla's standards position reportedly moved to positive on 2026-03-11.
- SameSite=None cookies and the Lax+POST default window weaken the 'cross-site means cookies isolated' shorthand unless app cookies set SameSite explicitly and stay host-only.
- The Gmail baseline sender requirements apply to all senders since 2024-02-01, not only above 5,000/day.
- The structured summary's source_url for the open-web claim (an Amazon store URL) does not correspond to the quoted text, which came from the WebSearch tool's own summary.

## tm-mosshatch
Dossier: `tm-mosshatch.md`

### Verifier lens: skeptic (confidence high; 26 claims confirmed)

I could not refute the core conclusions. The RDAP results reproduce: mosshatch.com was created 2026-09-29T17:48:38Z at Namecheap, privacy-masked, in the add period; .net, .org, .dev, .app, .studio, .ai and .io return 404 against same-server controls. Overall this is a knockout that holds, not clearance.
